# The timeline

Every dated event on the board, 978 of them, grouped by the era the board assigns and then by year. Each line gives the date, what happened, the node it hangs on, the strand of the story it belongs to and the tier it carries.

A note on tiers in this file: timeline entries carry either a claim tier, 1 to 3, or a board tier, A to C, depending on how the entry was written. Both are reproduced as stored and labelled for what they are.

## THROUGH 1969: Estimate through Blue Book

109 events sit in this era.

### 1944

- **1944-11-01, Foo fighters.** Allied aircrews over Europe and the Pacific report glowing objects pacing their aircraft. The 415th Night Fighter Squadron files repeated reports; no Axis weapon is ever identified as the cause. On the node Project SIGN. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
### 1945

- **1945-07-09, White Sands Proving Ground established.** Seven days before Trinity, the Army stands up the proving ground that will become the largest military installation in the United States and the geographic centre of the 1947-1951 New Mexico record. On the node White Sands Missile Range, New Mexico. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1946

- **1946-01-22, Truman creates the Central Intelligence Group and the post of Director of Central Intelligence.** A Presidential directive on the coordination of foreign intelligence activities provides that personnel assigned from State, War and the Navy 'shall collectively form a Central Intelligence Group and shall, under the direction of a Director of Central Intelligence, assist the National Intelligence Authority', the Director to be designated by the President and to sit as a non-voting member of the Authority. Rear Adm. Sidney W. Souers is the first holder and signs as Director of Central Intelligence within two weeks. On the node Rear Adm. Sidney W. Souers. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1946-03-01, Project RAND created.** The Army Air Forces establish Project RAND inside Douglas Aircraft, inventing the contractor-operated analytical partner form that every institution in this domain would later copy. On the node RAND Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1946-03-05, The British-U.S. Communication Intelligence Agreement is signed.** STANCIB and the London Signal Intelligence Board sign a twelve-paragraph agreement for unrestricted exchange of the products and methods of communications intelligence. It defines third parties as everyone outside the United States, the British Empire and the Dominions, and states that 'It will be contrary to this agreement to reveal its existence to any third party whatsoever.' A search of all six numbered pages returns no occurrence of flying, saucer, unidentified, aerial phenomen or anomalous. On the node British-U.S. Communication Intelligence Agreement (UKUSA), 5 March 1946. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1946-07-01, Ghost rockets over Scandinavia.** Sweden logs hundreds of unexplained rocket-like objects; US and UK intelligence send officers, including Gen. James Doolittle, to assess. No Soviet programme is ever identified. On the node Project SIGN. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1946-08-01, Atomic Energy Act of 1946 makes classification a property of a category.** Public Law 79-585, 60 Stat. 755, chapter 724, S. 1717, is approved. Section 10(b)(1) defines restricted data as all data concerning the manufacture or utilization of atomic weapons, the production of fissionable material or its use in the production of power, excluding only what the Commission affirmatively determines may be published. Section 2(a) establishes the Atomic Energy Commission, section 9(a) transfers the Manhattan Engineer District's holdings to it, section 10(b)(4) reaches concealment and destruction with a capital penalty, and section 10(b)(5)(C) gives the FBI exclusive jurisdiction over violations. On the node Atomic Energy Act of 1946 (McMahon Act). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1946-08-01, The Office of Naval Research is created, the same day as the Atomic Energy Act.** Public Law 79-588, 60 Stat. 779, establishes an Office of Naval Research in the Office of the Secretary of the Navy, headed by a Chief of Naval Research appointed by the President with the advice and consent of the Senate. Section 6 frees its research contracting from five named sections of the Revised Statutes and from performance bonds; section 5(b) keeps obligated research appropriations available four further years. It carries the same approval date as the Atomic Energy Act of 1946 at 60 Stat. 755, not a date two days later. On the node Office of Naval Research. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1946-12-31, The Armed Forces Special Weapons Project takes effect, holding the military half of the Manhattan Project.** AFSWP is established by joint directive of the Secretaries of War and the Navy 'effective as of midnight 31 December 1946', operating directly under the Army Chief of Staff and the Chief of Naval Operations and responsible for carrying on the military service functions of the Manhattan Project retained under armed forces control. The founding memorandum is signed on 29 January 1947 by Robert P. Patterson and James V. Forrestal; Major General Leslie R. Groves becomes first Chief on 28 February 1947. Its funds come by working fund advances from the Atomic Energy Commission out of money formerly available to the Manhattan Project. On the node Armed Forces Special Weapons Project. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1947

- **1947-06-24, Kenneth Arnold sighting.** Boise pilot Kenneth Arnold reports nine objects near Mount Rainier. Wire coverage coins 'flying saucer' and the national wave begins. On the node Kenneth Arnold. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1947-07-06, Brazel reports the Foster Ranch debris.** Mac Brazel reports the debris to Chaves County Sheriff George Wilcox, who calls Roswell Army Air Field. Maj. Jesse Marcel of the 509th Bomb Group and Capt. Sheridan Cavitt of the CIC are sent to retrieve it. On the node Roswell Incident. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-07-08, RAAF announces possession of a disc.** Roswell Army Air Field's information office states that the intelligence office of the 509th Bomb Group has gained possession of a disc. The 509th is the only atomic-armed combat group in the world. On the node RAAF Press Release, 8 July 1947. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-07-08, Ramey retracts the same day.** Brig. Gen. Roger Ramey displays weather balloon and radar target material at Fort Worth and identifies it as the recovered object. The FBI's Dallas office records the same account by teletype. On the node Roger M. Ramey. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-07-08, RAAF press release.** Walter Haut, public information officer of the 509th Bomb Group, writes and distributes the release announcing that Roswell Army Air Field has come into possession of a flying disc, naming Maj. Jesse Marcel Sr. as the recovering officer. Fort Worth retracts it the same day. On the node Walter Haut. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-07-08, RAAF announces a disc, and takes it back the same day.** 1st Lt. Walter Haut issues the 'flying disc' release; within hours Brig. Gen. Roger Ramey presents the material at Fort Worth as a weather balloon. The Air Force will concede in 1994 that the balloon statement was a cover story. On the node Roswell Incident. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-07-08, Roswell announces, Fort Worth retracts.** Roswell Army Air Field publicly announces that the 509th Bomb Group has come into possession of a flying disc. The material is flown to Eighth Air Force headquarters at Fort Worth, where it is presented to the press the same day as a weather balloon and radar target. Four official explanations for the event will follow over the next fifty years. On the node Roswell. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1947-07-26, National Security Act signed.** President Truman signs the National Security Act of 1947, creating the Central Intelligence Agency, the National Security Council and an independent Air Force. The entire institutional architecture within which the UAP record would be created and concealed comes into being seventeen days after the Roswell press release. On the node Central Intelligence Agency. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-07-26, Research and Development Board established.** The National Security Act of 1947 (61 Stat. 495) creates the Research and Development Board, the statutory coordinating body for defence research. Truman appoints Vannevar Bush its chairman, continuing him from the Joint Research and Development Board the service secretaries had set up after V-J Day. On the node Research and Development Board. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-09-18, US Air Force established.** The Air Force becomes an independent service and inherits the flying disc problem, Wright Field, and the Air Materiel Command intelligence apparatus that would run SIGN, GRUDGE and BLUE BOOK. On the node United States Air Force. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-09-23, Twining memo.** Lt. Gen. Nathan Twining writes that 'the phenomenon reported is something real and not visionary or fictitious', and directs consideration of a 'high security project' unknown to his own command. On the node Twining Memorandum: AMC Opinion Concerning "Flying Discs". Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-10-30, Schulgen collection memorandum distributed.** Air Force intelligence tasks worldwide collectors on disc-type craft, listing near-hover capability, extreme light weight construction and 'Nuclear propulsion (atomic energy)'. On the node Schulgen Collection Memorandum: Intelligence Requirements on Flying Saucer Type Aircraft. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1947-12-30, Project SIGN directed.** On Twining's recommendation the Air Force establishes its first official UFO project inside Air Materiel Command intelligence at Wright Field. It begins operating 23 January 1948. On the node Project SIGN. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1948

- **1948-01-13, Wright-Patterson and Kirtland are both constituted.** On the same day the Air Force formally establishes Wright-Patterson AFB, the address of foreign materiel exploitation, and Kirtland AFB, the nuclear weapons and directed energy complex inside Albuquerque. On the node Wright-Patterson Air Force Base, Ohio. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1948-02-22, Edgar Albert Fouche born, Americus, Georgia.** Find a Grave memorial 180498305, citing an obituary from Mason Funeral Home, gives the birth date and birthplace. The Library of Congress name authority attached to LCCN 98002961 independently gives 'Fouche, Edgar Rothschild, 1948-'. On the node Edgar Fouche. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1948-05-14, RAND incorporated as an independent nonprofit.** RAND separates from Douglas Aircraft and incorporates as a nonprofit, establishing the legal template: permanent, sole-source, answerable to a military customer, outside the civil service. On the node RAND Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1948-07-01, Estimate of the Situation written and destroyed.** Project SIGN staff conclude the objects are interplanetary. Air Force Chief of Staff Gen. Hoyt Vandenberg rejects the estimate for lacking proof and orders copies destroyed. The Condon Report records this twenty years later. On the node Estimate of the Situation (1948). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1949

- **1949, Scully's Variety columns.** Frank Scully publishes two Variety columns describing a recovered saucer and dead occupants, sourced to Silas M. Newton and Leo A. Gebauer. On the node Frank Scully. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1949-02-11, Project SIGN becomes Project GRUDGE.** The successor project concludes reports are misinterpretation, mass hysteria and hoaxes posing no direct threat to national security, and recommends reducing the scope of investigation. On the node Project GRUDGE. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1949-02-16, Los Alamos green fireball conference.** AEC and Air Force personnel, with Edward Teller and Lincoln LaPaz, meet at Los Alamos over the green fireball incursions above the atomic complex. The Department of Energy's institutional predecessor is in the UAP record from the first decade. On the node United States Department of Energy. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1949-02-16, Nuclear scientists meet at Los Alamos about the green fireballs.** Edward Teller and Lincoln LaPaz attend a conference on the anomalous objects over northern New Mexico's nuclear installations. LANL's own account records the phenomenon was ultimately dismissed as a national security threat. On the node Los Alamos, New Mexico. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1949-02-16, Los Alamos green fireballs conference.** Laboratory Director Norris Bradbury, Edward Teller and Frederick Reines meet at Los Alamos with FBI, AEC and military representatives on the green fireballs over the New Mexico nuclear complex. LANL still holds the minutes. On the node Los Alamos Green Fireballs Conference. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1949-06-20, CIA Act of 1949 creates unvouchered spending.** Section 8 of the Central Intelligence Agency Act, now 50 U.S.C. 3510(b), permits Agency sums to be 'expended without regard to the provisions of law and regulations relating to the expenditure of Government funds', with confidential, extraordinary or emergency objects 'accounted for solely on the certificate of the Director'. The oldest and bluntest black-budget instrument in American law, enacted two years after Roswell and never narrowed. On the node 50 U.S.C. 3510 - CIA unvouchered funds (CIA Act of 1949, sec. 8). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1949-12-01, Project Twinkle established.** A network of green fireball observation and photographic stations is stood up over New Mexico. It is never fully implemented and produces no conclusive result. On the node Los Alamos National Laboratory. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1950

- **1950, Behind the Flying Saucers.** Henry Holt publishes Scully's book: a 99-foot disc, sixteen bodies, Hart Canyon near Aztec, March 1948. It becomes a bestseller and supplies the genre's narrative furniture. On the node Behind the Flying Saucers (Scully, 1950). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1950-03-22, Guy Hottel FBI memorandum.** The special agent in charge of the FBI Washington Field Office records a third-hand account of three 50-foot circular craft recovered in New Mexico, each with three three-foot occupants in metallic cloth, attributed to high-powered radar interference. The memo closes: 'No further evaluation was attempted.' On the node Aztec, New Mexico claim, March 1948. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1950-11-21, Smith writes the 'Geo-Magnetics' memorandum.** Wilbert Brockhouse Smith, Senior Radio Engineer of the Canadian Department of Transport, writes a Top Secret memorandum to the Controller of Telecommunications recording what he had been told in Washington: that the flying saucer question was the most highly classified subject in the United States government, rating higher than the H-bomb, and was being worked by a small group headed by Vannevar Bush. Library and Archives Canada quotes the same memorandum's operative line: 'the correlation between our basic theory and the available information on saucers checks too closely to be mere coincidence'. On the node Wilbert Brockhouse Smith. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1950-11-21, A foreign official records the American classification level.** Wilbert B. Smith, a radio engineer in Canada's Department of Transport, writes an internal memorandum to the Controller of Telecommunications stating that the flying saucer subject is classified higher in the United States than the hydrogen bomb and that a small group is studying it. Its evidentiary value is its provenance: a foreign government official reporting internally, not a claimant addressing the public. On the node Legacy Program. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1950-12-02, Project Magnet approved.** The Canadian Department of Transport approves Smith's request. Project Magnet is the first government programme anywhere whose stated purpose includes applying recovered UAP data to practical engineering. On the node Project Magnet (Canada). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
### 1951

- **1951-01-01, Battelle contracted to Project GRUDGE.** Capt. Edward Ruppelt creates the contract he calls Project BEAR with the Battelle Memorial Institute to supply scientific support to the Air Force UFO investigation. The first private contractor enters the file. On the node Project BEAR (Battelle contract to Project GRUDGE). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1951-01-01, MIT Lincoln Laboratory established.** Lincoln Laboratory is created to build SAGE, the first continental air defence system - the architecture that determines what American airspace surveillance is built to see. On the node MIT Lincoln Laboratory. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1951-04-04, Truman establishes the Psychological Strategy Board by secret directive.** A directive to the Secretary of State, the Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence, classified secret, establishes a Board reporting to the National Security Council for the formulation of over-all national psychological objectives, policies and programmes. Its editorial footnote names the first Director: 'Gordon Gray, President of the University of North Carolina and former Secretary of the Army, was appointed Director of the new board.' The same directive forbids the Board and the Director to perform any psychological operations themselves. On the node Gordon Gray. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1951-06, UK Flying Saucer Working Party reports.** Britain's first official body on the question reports and recommends no further investigation - the same self-terminating conclusion Project Condign would reach in 2000. On the node Flying Saucer Working Party (UK). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
### 1952

- **1952, Project Magnet report recommends acquiring the technology.** Smith's report concludes that 'we are forced to the conclusion that the vehicles are probably extra-terrestrial, in spite of our prejudices to the contrary' and recommends 'a substantial effort towards the acquisition of as much as possible of this technology, which would without doubt be of great value to us'. On the node Project Magnet (Canada). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1952-01-01, Battelle contracted for Project Stork.** The Air Force outsources the statistical analysis of Project Blue Book's case files to Battelle Memorial Institute in Columbus, Ohio, under the codename Project Stork. On the node Project Stork (Project White Stork). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1952-03-01, Project Blue Book begins under Ruppelt.** Capt. Edward Ruppelt renames and professionalises the project, coining the term 'unidentified flying object' to replace 'flying saucer'. On the node Edward J. Ruppelt. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1952-03-01, Ruppelt briefs the Beacon Hill circle.** Blue Book director Edward J. Ruppelt gives a 'flying saucer talk' recorded as AF-ATIC-FILM, 03/52 - a briefing into the classified summer study on aerial reconnaissance hosted in the MIT/Lincoln Laboratory complex. On the node AF-ATIC-FILM, 03/52 ('flying saucer talk'). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1952-03-31, Project Stork begins.** Work begins on Project Stork, designation PPS-100, later carried as contract AF-19741, for the Air Technical Intelligence Center. The first status report of 25 April 1952 describes progress 'from its inception on March 31, 1952'. Seven status reports survive, totalling 107 pages, and the transcribing archive records that further reports referenced in the text are not in NARA custody. On the node Project Stork (Project White Stork). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1952-04, Project Second Storey established.** The Defence Research Board establishes a committee under NRC astronomer Peter Millman. It builds a standardised report form with a weighting factor to minimise the 'personal equation', meets six times, concludes UFOs are neither a security threat nor of scientific interest, and recommends ending all Canadian military investigation. On the node Project Second Storey (Canada). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1952-07-19, Washington DC radar-visual sightings.** Unidentified targets are tracked at Washington National, Andrews AFB and the ARTCC over consecutive July weekends, with visual corroboration and scrambled interceptors. On the node 1952 Washington DC Sightings. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1952-09-11, Chadwell memo to the DCI.** CIA's Assistant Director for Scientific Intelligence tells the Director that July 1952 produced 250 official reports and that the situation 'contains two elements of danger' with national security implications. On the node H. Marshall Chadwell. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1952-11-04, NSA established by classified presidential memorandum.** Truman creates the National Security Agency by a memorandum that is itself classified - the first of the two agencies on this board whose existence was a secret. On the node National Security Agency. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1953

- **1953, Newton and Gebauer convicted.** Scully's two sources are convicted of fraud after millionaire Herman Flader presses charges over 'doodlebug' devices sold on the claim that they used recovered alien technology. On the node Aztec, New Mexico claim, March 1948. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1953-01-09, The Pentacle Memorandum.** Battelle's H. C. Cross writes to Miles E. Goll at Wright-Patterson under Project Stork, ref. G-1579-4, SECRET, proposing that 'many different types of aerial activity should be secretly and purposefully scheduled' in areas of high UFO reporting. On the node The Pentacle Memorandum. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1953-01-14, Robertson Panel convenes.** A CIA Office of Scientific Intelligence panel sits for four days and recommends stripping UFOs of special status through a mass-media 'debunking' programme, canvassing Disney and the Navy Special Devices Center, and monitoring civilian UFO groups. On the node Robertson Panel. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1953-01-14, Robertson Panel convenes uninformed.** The CIA-convened panel meets five days after the Pentacle memorandum. Per Jacques Vallee it is never briefed on the Battelle proposals - a separate body had already fixed the boundaries of what it could consider. On the node Robertson Panel. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1953-01-17, Robertson Panel reports.** The CIA-convened panel under H. P. Robertson recommends stripping the phenomenon of its 'aura of mystery' through education and debunking, and the monitoring of civilian UFO groups 'because of their potentially great influence on mass thinking'. American UFO information management begins here. On the node Central Intelligence Agency. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1953-01-17, The Robertson Panel recommends a debunking programme.** A CIA Office of Scientific Intelligence panel recommends that the national security agencies strip UFOs of their special status through a programme of mass-media 'training and debunking', and that civilian UFO groups be watched because of their potential influence on mass thinking. This is the documented origin point of the psychological-operations domain. On the node Psychological Operations & Perception Management. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1953-02-16, Durant records the proceedings.** CIA officer Frederick Durant's memorandum to the Assistant Director for Scientific Intelligence becomes the only substantive account of what the panel actually discussed. On the node Frederick C. Durant III. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1953-03-25, The FBI investigates Project Winterhaven as possible fraud.** An FBI report out of Cincinnati records that Thomas Townsend Brown 'is apparently in Washington attempting to obtain a research and development contract for his so-called Project Winterhaven' and 'anticipates a contract of approximately a million dollars'. The Washington Field Office is directed to determine any possible violation of the Fraud Against the Government statute, and to review Brown's patents at the Patent Office. On the node Thomas Townsend Brown. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1953-08-26, AFR 200-2 issued.** The Air Force regulation on UFO reporting restricts public release on unexplained cases to the bare fact that ATIC will analyse the data. Revised 12 August 1954. On the node Air Force Regulation 200-2. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1953-08-26, AFR 200-2 puts the release restriction in writing.** Air Force Regulation 200-2, short title UFOB, signed by order of the Secretary of the Air Force by N. F. Twining, supersedes AFL 200-5 of 29 April 1952. It designates ATIC as the analysis authority, directs that all evidence be forwarded there, and restricts public discussion to cases conclusively identified as conventional. Identified cases could be discussed; unidentified ones could not. On the node Air Force Regulation 200-2. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1954

- **1954-03-10, JANAP 146(C) issued.** Unidentified flying objects become a mandatory CIRVIS report for airborne personnel, with unauthorised disclosure of report contents prohibited under 18 U.S.C. 793 and 794. On the node JANAP 146 (CIRVIS/MERINT reporting). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1954-08-30, Atomic Energy Act of 1954.** Restricted Data is created as a category classified by statute rather than executive order. Any material placed under this authority becomes permanently beyond the reach of presidential declassification directives. On the node United States Department of Energy. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1954-12-01, Battelle's authorship of Special Report No. 14 concealed.** Battelle completes the statistical study issued as Special Report No. 14 and, per AARO's own history, 'released a report under the cover of ATIC to maintain its anonymity.' Contractor performs, government takes the byline. On the node Project BEAR (Battelle contract to Project GRUDGE). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1955

- **1955-02-15, ATIC and WADC report jointly on a disc-shaped supersonic aircraft.** TR-AC-47, Project No. 9961, is issued at Wright-Patterson Air Force Base on the Avro Canada Project Y2 flat-riser. Its stated purpose is to correct the distorted picture presented in previous releases and to alert the intelligence community to any Soviet interest, and its Summary says the project should in no way be associated with any science fiction or flying saucer stories because of its external appearance. It was declassified on 29 March 1995. On the node TR-AC-47, Joint ATIC-WADC Report on Project Silver Bug, 15 February 1955. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1955-04-01, The CIA establishes the Groom Lake facility.** Project AQUATONE selects the site because U-2 testing cannot be conducted at Edwards or Palmdale given the secrecy of the project. The first U-2 arrives 24 July 1955. On the node Groom Lake, Nevada. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1955-05-05, Blue Book Special Report No. 14.** ATIC issues the largest statistical study of UFO reports ever produced by the US government: 434 of 2,199 objects unknown, with unknown rates rising with report quality. On the node Project Blue Book Special Report No. 14. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1955-05-05, Blue Book Special Report No. 14 issued.** Battelle delivers the largest statistical study of UFO reports ever performed for the US government, Project No. 10073. The proportion of unknowns rises with the quality of the sighting; the Air Force publicises the opposite conclusion. On the node Project Blue Book Special Report No. 14. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1955-06-10, An aerospace prime proposes an institute to study gravity.** George S. Trimble of the Glenn L. Martin Company writes to Bryce DeWitt that 'our industry was vitally concerned with gravity' and that respectable researchers 'do not wish to get mixed up in the field of gravity research'. He proposes an 'industrial version of the Institute for Advanced Study'. RIAS, Inc. of Baltimore is the result; Louis Witten is its first researcher. On the node RIAS, Inc. (Research Institute for Advanced Studies). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1955-09-07, The Institute of Field Physics is incorporated, with an anti-gravity disclaimer written into its publicity.** The Institute receives its certificate of incorporation and is placed inside the physics department at the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill under Bryce and Cecile DeWitt, funded principally by Agnew Bahnson. George Trimble and the Glenn L. Martin Company buy a Founder's Membership for 5,000 dollars. On John Wheeler's insistence a Protection Clause is attached to every publicity statement declaring that the work 'has no connection with so-called "anti-gravity research" of whatever kind and for whatever purposes'. On the node Institute of Field Physics (UNC Chapel Hill). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1956

- **1956, NICAP incorporated in the District of Columbia.** The National Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena states in its own 1964 volume that it is a non-profit organization incorporated in the District of Columbia in 1956. A Washington Daily News report of 23 October 1956, filed in the FBI's NICAP file, records the organisation forming at 1536 Connecticut Avenue NW under T. Townsend Brown of Leesburg, Virginia, executive vice chairman pro tempore, with a nine-man board of governors still to be elected on 1 November. The District's own corporate register was not opened. On the node National Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena (NICAP). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1957

- **1957-01-18, RIAS at the Chapel Hill gravitation conference.** The participants list of the conference on the role of gravitation in physics prints 'Witten, L. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore' and 'Bass, R.W. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore'. The conference report is WADC Technical Report 57-216, produced under a funding agreement with the Wright Air Development Center. The participants record their own concern to avoid identification with anti-gravity research. On the node RIAS, Inc. (Research Institute for Advanced Studies). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1958

- **1958-04-04, CIA resolves to route Keyhoe's UFO inquiries to the Air Force, and to make it a precedent.** A CIA memorandum for record on 'Comments on Letters Dealing with Unidentified Flying Objects' notes that the Air Force was 'most insistent' on answering his letters itself, that 'the Air Force is endeavoring to impress upon Major Keyhoe and others in a similar position, that his office is the proper place to come for such information', and concludes: 'I think we can use this procedure as a precedent henceforth in all inquiries regarding UFO's.' On the node Maj. Donald E. Keyhoe, USMC (Ret.). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1958-07-21, MITRE incorporated.** The MITRE Corporation is incorporated as a nonprofit, taking Robert Everett and 485 Lincoln Laboratory staff to continue SAGE systems engineering under Air Force sponsorship rather than university administration. On the node The MITRE Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1959

- **1959, Canada-US joint UFO reporting system.** Library and Archives Canada records that an agreement was finalised between the United States and Canada to institute a joint reporting system for UFOs under CIRVIS/MERINT, 'to extend the early warning coverage for the defence of North America'. Posters depicted a saucer-like object alongside missiles and submarines as examples of objects to be reported. On the node Library and Archives Canada - UFO records collection. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1959-06-19, A Study of Lunar Research Flights delivered to the Air Force Special Weapons Center.** AFSWC TR-59-39 Vol I, by L. Reiffel of the Armour Research Foundation with contributions including C. E. Sagan, is dated 19 June 1959 under Contract AF 29(601)-1164. Its introduction states that the motivation for detonating a nuclear weapon on or near the Moon is threefold, scientific, military and political, and that positive effects would accrue to the nation first performing the feat. Copies went to ARPA at the Pentagon, to the AEC for the Division of Military Application, to Sandia, to Los Alamos, to the Lawrence Radiation Laboratory and to WADD at Wright-Patterson. The string A119 appears nowhere in it. On the node AFSWC TR-59-39 Vol I, A Study of Lunar Research Flights, 19 June 1959. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1960

- **1960-01-01, JASON established under IDA administration.** The government's standing panel of elite scientists is created - and housed inside an FFRDC operator rather than inside the government. It has never been anywhere else since. On the node JASON. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1960-06-01, The first DCI asks Congress to investigate.** A Worcester Gazette report, filed in CIA records and released in 2014, quotes Adm. Roscoe H. Hillenkoetter, DCI from May 1947 to October 1950: 'The unknown objects are operating under intelligent control. It is imperative that we learn where the UFO's come from and what their purpose is.' He is by then a NICAP board member and has referred his conclusions to the Senate Space Committee. On the node Roscoe H. Hillenkoetter. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1960-06-03, The Aerospace Corporation founded.** Announced by Lt. Gen. Bernard Schriever, Aerospace is created to provide dedicated technical planning for military space and ballistic missile programmes, with charter restrictions the Air Force had not imposed on earlier FFRDCs. On the node The Aerospace Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1961

- **1961-01-01, MOON DUST / BLUE FLY stood up.** The Air Force establishes a named worldwide capability to collect and exploit unidentified objects and debris surviving re-entry, with a quick-reaction airlift arm routing material to technical intelligence at Wright-Patterson. On the node Project MOON DUST. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1961-07-01, ATIC is discontinued and the Foreign Technology Division is constituted the same day.** The Air Force organizational-history record gives a discontinuation and a new constitution rather than a renaming: 'Air Technical Intelligence Center established 21 May 1951; redesignated Aerospace Technical Intelligence Center, 21 September 1959; discontinued 1 July 1961. Foreign Technology Division constituted, activated, and organized 1 July 1961', with the mission and resources absorbed by FTD on that date. The board's prose in more than one node says ATIC 'became' FTD; the record calls it a discontinuation and an absorption. Carried at Tier 2 because the compilation is a private site citing the Air Force Historical Research Agency, whose own pages return 403 to this environment. On the node Foreign Technology Division, Wright-Patterson AFB. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1961-09-06, NRO established as a classified entity.** The National Reconnaissance Office is created as a joint DoD-CIA activity under a classified directive. Its existence will be an official secret for the next 31 years - the working proof that concealment at agency scale is achievable. On the node National Reconnaissance Office. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1961-09-06, An agency whose existence is a secret.** The National Reconnaissance Office is established with its very existence classified - a status maintained until September 1992. For thirty-one years the legally required answer to whether the organisation existed was no, which is the documented proof that a categorical official denial can be false and lawful at the same time. On the node Official Denial. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 1962

- **1962-03-13, The Joint Chiefs forward fabricated pretexts for invading Cuba to the Secretary of Defense.** A memorandum for the Secretary of Defense, subject 'Justification for US Military Intervention in Cuba (TS)', serial JCS 1969/321, forwards an annex headed 'PRETEXTS TO JUSTIFY US MILITARY INTERVENTION IN CUBA' and recommends that responsibility for both overt and covert military operations under the resulting plan be assigned to the Joint Chiefs of Staff. It is signed 'For the Joint Chiefs of Staff, L. L. LEMNITZER, Chairman'. On the node Justification for US Military Intervention in Cuba (Operation Northwoods), 13 March 1962. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1962-04-18, Corso becomes Chief, Foreign Technology Division, OCRD.** Philip J. Corso's own DA Form 66 records him as 'Chief Foreign Technology Div, OCRD USA' from 18 April to 18 July 1962, after a year as a staff officer in the same division. Three months. The same form records him on the Operations Coordinating Board from 1954 to 1957, which his later affidavit describes as membership of the National Security Council. On the node Philip J. Corso. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1962-10-25, Bluegill Triple Prime detonates over Johnston Island.** The Defense Nuclear Agency's Operation DOMINIC I report records the shot at 25 October 1962, 2359 local, the fourth and first successful attempt to launch the BLUEGILL device on a Thor missile, submegaton, tens of kilometres over Johnston Island, with 22 Air Force aircraft arrayed and nine Navy ships positioned. The report carries no unidentified object, no recovery and no debris, and the photographic aircraft were three KC-135s under the call sign KETTLE. On the node Bluegill Triple Prime. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
### 1965

- **1965-12-09, Kecksburg.** A fireball crosses eight states at about 4:44pm EST. Kecksburg residents report an object down in the woods. The Greensburg Tribune-Review reports the area 'immediately sealed off on the order of U.S. Army and State Police officials'. A later edition: 'Searchers Fail to Find Object.' On the node Kecksburg incident, 9 December 1965. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1966

- **1966-03-01, Michigan sightings and 'swamp gas'.** Mass sightings near Dexter and Hillsdale are met with J. Allen Hynek's provisional swamp gas explanation, reported as a blanket dismissal and met with public fury. On the node J. Allen Hynek. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1966-04-05, First congressional hearing.** After House Minority Leader Gerald Ford demands an inquiry, the House Armed Services Committee holds a one-day hearing on unidentified flying objects, the first in nineteen years of official investigation. On the node House Armed Services Committee UFO Hearing. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1966-08-09, The Low memo.** Two months before the Air Force contract is signed, project coordinator Robert Low writes that 'the trick would be' to appear a totally objective study to the public while presenting the image of nonbelievers to scientists. On the node Low Memorandum ("the trick would be..."). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1966-08-09, The conclusion is written before the study.** Robert Low's memorandum proposes a study 'conducted almost entirely by non-believers' with 'almost zero expectation of finding a saucer'. McDonald finds it in the project files in July 1967. On the node Boulder, Colorado. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1966-08-25, Minot AFB documents.** Three Air Force documents record unexplained aerial activity around Minot AFB, North Dakota, home of the 91st Strategic Missile Wing. Published in facsimile by Robert Hastings. On the node Minot AFB incursions, 1966. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1966-09-19, AFR 80-17 assigns the Blue Book effort to the Foreign Technology Division by name.** Air Force Regulation 80-17 provides that 'The Air Force Systems Command will support the program with current resources within the Foreign Technology Division (FTD) at Wright-Patterson Air Force Base, Ohio, to continue Blue Book effort', requires FTD to 'Analyze and evaluate all information and evidence reported to bases on those UFOs which are not identified at the base level', and requires FTD to 'prepare a final case report on each sighting reported to it'. Six months earlier the USAF Scientific Advisory Board's ad hoc committee had recorded the resources behind the whole programme as 'only one officer, a sergeant, and secretary'. On the node Foreign Technology Division, Wright-Patterson AFB. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1966-10-06, Condon Committee established.** The University of Colorado UFO Project is formed under USAF contract 44620-67-C-0035 with Edward Condon as scientific director. On the node University of Colorado UFO Project (Condon Committee). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1967

- **1967-03-16, Malmstrom Echo Flight shutdown.** All ten Minuteman missiles of Echo Flight, 341st Strategic Missile Wing, go off alert status. An Air Force teletype the following day concerns loss of strategic alert. Robert Salas would later attribute the cause to an object over the front gate. On the node Malmstrom AFB missile shutdowns, March 1967. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1967-06-30, The 1127th's own history names the recovery pipeline.** The 1127th USAF Field Activities Group's semiannual history, doc. AFNIABA 014-67, lists Project BLUE FLY, MIG-17 Exploitation and Project MOON DUST together under FOREIGN MATERIEL PROGRAM. It records exploitation personnel from FTD Detachment 3 deployed to crash sites and about three tons of parts airlifted to Wright-Patterson, and SPADATS notifying the unit of 37 Soviet deorbits. On the node 1127th USAF Field Activities Group. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1967-10-04, Shag Harbour.** An object enters the water off Shag Harbour, Nova Scotia at about 11:20pm ADT before at least eleven witnesses. RCC Halifax telexes the RCAF Air Desk in Ottawa having eliminated all aircraft; the Navy tasks Fleet Diving Unit Atlantic to a three-day underwater search. Nothing is found. On the node Shag Harbour incident, 4 October 1967. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1968

- **1968-01-01, McDonnell Douglas funds an internal study.** Robert M. Wood begins a company-funded study of unidentified craft and their propulsion at McDonnell Douglas Astronautics, Huntington Beach. No government contract, no reporting obligation, no released record. On the node McDonnell Douglas Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1968-05-01, Look publishes 'Flying Saucer Fiasco'.** John G. Fuller publishes the Low memo, calling the project a $500,000 trick. Condon fires investigators David Saunders and Norman Levine. On the node Low Memorandum ("the trick would be..."). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1968-07-09, Chile's first official UAP circular.** Colonel Sergio Bravo Flores, Director of the Chilean Meteorological Office, sends the first official circular to 43 aeronautical installations from Arica to the Chilean Antarctic Territory requesting reports. Part of the resulting dozen reports are forwarded to the University of Colorado, where the Condon Committee is working. On the node Coronel de Aviación Sergio Bravo Flores. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1968-07-29, House Science Committee UFO symposium.** Hynek, James McDonald, Carl Sagan and others testify. McDonald tells Congress that 'credible observers are observing relatively incredible objects'. No legislation follows. On the node James E. McDonald. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1968-10-24, Minot B-52 incident.** Ground radar, base weather radar, airborne radar and visual observation converge on an object near B-52H JAG 31; the bomber's transmitter fails for four minutes; Minuteman sites of N, O, J and M Flights report alarms and an inner door combination lock moved. Blue Book sends no field investigators. On the node Minot AFB B-52 incident, 24 October 1968. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1968-11-01, RAND publishes 'UFOs: What To Do?'.** George Kocher's draft DRU-1571 examines UFO brightness, size and manoeuvres and recommends a course of action - two months before the Condon report tells the public the subject is closed. On the node George Kocher. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1968-11-01, The Condon Report closes the official file.** The University of Colorado study directed by Edward Condon publishes 'Scientific Study of Unidentified Flying Objects', including Martin Altschuler's chapter on atmospheric electricity and plasma interpretations. Project Blue Book is terminated the following year, ending official American investigation for the next four decades. On the node Historiography. Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 1969

- **1969-01-08, Condon Report released.** 'Scientific Study of Unidentified Flying Objects' concludes further study is unwarranted, while its Section V Chapter 2 documents the 1948 Estimate of the Situation and its destruction. On the node University of Colorado UFO Project (Condon Committee). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1969-10-20, Bolender memo.** Brig. Gen. Carroll Bolender recommends terminating Blue Book, explaining that UFO reports affecting national security go via JANAP 146 or AFM 55-11 and 'are not part of the Blue Book system'. On the node Bolender Memorandum. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1969-12-17, Secretary of the Air Force terminates Project BLUE BOOK.** After 12,618 reported sightings of which 701 remain unidentified, the Air Force closes its public UFO channel on the strength of the Condon Report and a National Academy of Sciences review. No document terminating classified collection is ever produced. On the node United States Air Force. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1969-12-17, Project Blue Book terminated.** Secretary of the Air Force Robert C. Seamans Jr. closes the program citing the Condon Report. Final tally: 12,618 sightings, 701 never identified. On the node Project Blue Book. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1969-12-17, Blue Book is terminated on the Condon findings.** The Air Force closes its public reporting channel citing the University of Colorado report. The Bolender memo of the same period records that reports affecting national security were never in that channel. On the node Boulder, Colorado. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.

## 1970-1999: Post-Blue Book

172 events sit in this era.

### 1970

- **1970-01-01, SCANATE begins.** The CIA initiates 'scan by coordinate' in response to assessments of Soviet psychotronic spending. SRI research under Puthoff and Targ follows in 1972. On the node STAR GATE and predecessor remote viewing programs, 1970-1995. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1972

- **1972-01-01, SRI psychoenergetics.** Harold Puthoff and Russell Targ begin the SRI International programme that becomes the government's Stargate effort - the template for outsourcing an unwanted phenomenon to a private laboratory. On the node Harold E. Puthoff. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1972-01-01, CIA funds SRI psychoenergetics.** Harold Puthoff and Russell Targ begin CIA-funded remote-viewing research at Stanford Research Institute - the start of a 23-year compartmented programme in a publicly ridiculed domain. On the node SRI International (Stanford Research Institute). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1972-01-01, Federal funding of anomalous cognition begins.** Harold Puthoff and Russell Targ begin remote viewing work at Stanford Research Institute under federal sponsorship. The programme runs through Grill Flame, Center Lane, Sun Streak and Star Gate until 1995, establishing beyond argument that the US government funds research of this kind for decades at a time before terminating it and denying it produced anything. On the node Remote Viewing & Anomalous Cognition. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 1973

- **1973-01-01, JASON moves from IDA to SRI International.** The advisory panel's administration transfers between FFRDC-adjacent operators for the first time, establishing the pattern of contractor custody of the government's most sensitive scientific advice. On the node JASON. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
### 1974

- **1974-10-18, Targ and Puthoff publish in Nature.** 'Information transmission under conditions of sensory shielding' appears in Nature vol. 251, pp. 602-607 - the open-literature face of a classified programme. On the node Harold E. Puthoff. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1974-10-23, The USIB charters a standing committee tasked with anomalies by name.** The charter of the Scientific and Technical Intelligence Committee records the renaming from the Scientific Intelligence Committee and lists among its Priority Group II objectives the 'Assessment and establishment of an Intelligence Community position regarding certain unidentified foreign activities and anomalies in the S&T area'. The committee advises the United States Intelligence Board and coordinates collection and production in those areas. Nothing in the charter mentions the Robertson Panel. On the node Scientific and Technical Intelligence Committee (USIB). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1975

- **1975-01-01, Ben Rich takes over Skunk Works.** Rich succeeds Kelly Johnson as director of Lockheed's Advanced Development Projects, holding the deepest black-aerospace access available to a private citizen for the next sixteen years. On the node Ben R. Rich. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1975-10-27, Loring AFB overflights begin.** Unidentified aerial incursions are logged over Loring AFB, Maine, opening a three-week sequence across SAC nuclear installations. On the node 1975 SAC Nuclear-Base Overflights (Loring, Wurtsmith, Malmstrom, Minot). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1975-11-07, Malmstrom AFB and Minuteman fields overflown.** DoD records later released under FOIA case 97-F-1677 describe numerous unidentified craft over Malmstrom AFB and its missile fields, which multiple F-106 interceptors could not acquire. On the node Malmstrom AFB and Minuteman Missile Fields, Montana. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1975-11-08, NMCC memorandum for the record.** At 0253 EST Malmstrom takes seven height-finder radar cuts between 9,500 and 15,500 feet with simultaneous ground witnesses; FAA cross-tell shows no jet within 100NM; two F-106s are scrambled at 0254 and airborne at 0257; at 0344 the objects cannot be intercepted. On the node 1975 SAC Nuclear-Base Overflights (Loring, Wurtsmith, Malmstrom, Minot). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1975-11-11, NORAD summary document.** A NORAD document records that reliable military personnel at Loring, Wurtsmith, Malmstrom, Minot and CFS Falconbridge had visually sighted suspicious objects during October and early November 1975. On the node 1975 SAC Nuclear-Base Overflights (Loring, Wurtsmith, Malmstrom, Minot). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1976

- **1976, IHEDN committee opens the French file.** A committee of the Institut des Hautes Études de Défense Nationale chaired by General Blanchard of the Gendarmerie Nationale opens the unidentified flying objects file with the objective of proposing how to organise research and data collection. Its recommendations are adopted. On the node Institut des Hautes Études de Défense Nationale (IHEDN). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1976-04-26, Church Committee files Book I and records that it never examined an agency file itself.** Senate Report 94-755, Book I, Foreign and Military Intelligence, is filed. Its section on the obstacles the committee met states that despite its legal Senate mandate and the issuance of subpoenas, in no instance had the committee been able to examine the agencies' files on its own. Its publication note records that three chapters, Cover, Espionage and Budgetary Oversight, were deleted at the request of the agencies. On the node Senate Select Committee to Study Governmental Operations with Respect to Intelligence Activities (Church Committee). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1977

- **1977, GEPAN created inside CNES.** The French national space agency establishes GEPAN under Claude Poher with the authority of director general Yves Sillard - the world's first and still only permanent civilian government UAP office with a public case archive. The Gendarmerie Nationale routes sighting reports to it by teletype. On the node GEPAN - Groupe d'Étude des Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non-identifiés. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1977-01-01, CAUS founded.** Citizens Against UFO Secrecy is established under attorney Peter Gersten and begins FOIA litigation against the CIA. On the node Citizens Against UFO Secrecy (CAUS). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1977-05-17, JANAP 146 still lists UFOs, eight years after Blue Book closed.** Change No. 2 to JANAP 146(E) is promulgated. Unidentified flying objects remain a reportable CIRVIS category at Article 201, and Article 208 continues to incorporate 18 U.S.C. 793 and the Canadian Official Secrets Act. Project Blue Book closed in December 1969. The document was released under FOIA on 28 February 1994. On the node JANAP 146 (CIRVIS/MERINT reporting). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1977-09-20, The Petrozavodsk phenomenon.** A luminous object is reported from Copenhagen and Helsinki to Vladivostok. Northern European governments write to the president of the USSR Academy of Sciences asking whether it was Soviet weapons testing. The Academy's own preliminary report concludes that 'based on the available data, it is unfeasible to satisfactorily understand the observed phenomenon'. On the node Petrozavodsk phenomenon, 20 September 1977. Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **1977-10-20, Operação Prato begins on Colares.** The Brazilian Air Force and the National Information Service open the first Brazilian military operation mounted solely to investigate UAP, at the request of the mayor of Colares after residents report burns and puncture wounds from descending lights. Captain Uyrangê Hollanda commands. On the node Operação Prato (Operation Saucer). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **1977-12-01, HAVE BLUE flies at Groom Lake.** Lockheed's stealth demonstrator first flies from a facility whose existence the government will not acknowledge for another twenty-one years. On the node Groom Lake, Nevada. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1977-12-17, Council Bluffs fall.** Five independent witness groups see a luminous mass descend near Big Lake Park, Council Bluffs, Iowa; police photograph a four-by-six-foot molten metal mass. The sample survives into the modern literature. On the node Council Bluffs, Iowa molten metal fall, 17 December 1977. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1977-12-26, CAUS FOIA request.** Citizens Against UFO Secrecy files for the Loring, Wurtsmith and Malmstrom case files. On 6 February 1978 the Office of the Assistant Secretary of Defense identifies twenty-four Joint Chiefs documents; after an internal dispute Assistant Secretary Thomas Ross orders the withheld fifteen released. On the node Clear Intent: The Government Coverup of the UFO Experience (1984). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1978

- **1978-01-01, GRILL FLAME formalised.** Army INSCOM and DIA formalise operational remote viewing at Fort Meade using SRI methodology - the first of four codenames for one continuous seventeen-year effort. On the node GRILL FLAME. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1978-01-26, First Council Bluffs analysis.** The Des Moines Register reports the initial materials analyses identifying the metal as high-carbon steel and slag - the baseline the 2022 Stanford work would revisit. On the node Council Bluffs, Iowa molten metal fall, 17 December 1977. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1978-02, Friedman meets Marcel.** Stanton Friedman is put in touch with retired Lt. Col. Jesse Marcel Sr. in Baton Rouge. Marcel states the weather balloon account was a cover and that the material was not of this earth. Roswell restarts after thirty-one years. On the node Stanton T. Friedman. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1978-02-01, Jesse Marcel breaks silence.** The 509th's intelligence officer tells researcher Stanton Friedman that the 1947 weather balloon explanation was a cover story, restarting the Roswell inquiry after thirty-one years. On the node Jesse A. Marcel Sr. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1978-06, GEPAN's classified five-volume report.** GEPAN reports to its scientific council in five volumes and about 500 pages, limited to 120 or 140 copies and secret. Of eleven high-strangeness cases studied by four-person teams including psychologists, ten were concluded to be material phenomena not explicable as natural or human. A document in the US NSA's declassified UFO collection assesses that its method makes 'the bulk of the Condon Report seem very poor by comparison'. On the node GEPAN - Groupe d'Étude des Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non-identifiés. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1978-07-29, Retrievals of the Third Kind.** Leonard Stringfield delivers Status Report I at the ninth annual MUFON Symposium in Dayton, Ohio. The crash-retrieval literature begins as a research field. On the node UFO Crash/Retrieval Status Reports I-VII (Stringfield, 1978-1994). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1978-10-18, Founding meeting of SETKA at the USSR Academy of Sciences.** Migulin and Platov of IZMIRAN, Narimanov and Petrovskaya of the Institute for Space Studies, Balashov and Volga of secret military unit 67947, Gindilis of the Shternberg Institute and others meet. SETKA-AN and SETKA-MO are established on the order of the Military-Industrial Commission. The official history later gives as one reason for secrecy the possibility 'that some of the UFO characteristics could be used for military purposes'. On the node SETKA-AN / SETKA-MO (later Galaktika, then Gorizont) - Soviet state anomalous phenomena programme. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
### 1979

- **1979-06-01, THE COUNTERWEIGHT: TSgt Fouche assigned temporary duty to AFFTC Detachment 3, Nellis AF Range.** An AF Form 77a signed 24 August 1979 by MSgt Brewer of 57 CRS, Nellis AFB Nevada records: 'This out of cycle report is generated because TSgt Fouche was assigned TDY to the AFFTC-DET 3, Nellis AF Range from 1 June 79 to 14 August 79. His duties and responsibilities for this period have been verified via a separate report.' Peter Merlin's published reference work identifies Detachment 3 AFFTC as the unit operating the Groom Lake facility. The form does not say what he did there, and the separate report it refers to is not in the file. On the node AF Form 77a, Letter of Evaluation, 24 August 1979. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1979-07-01, Bennewitz begins his Manzano study.** The Albuquerque businessman starts a self-funded study of lights and signals over the Kirtland/Manzano nuclear weapons storage area. He contacts the base 24 October 1980 and briefs officials 10 November 1980. On the node Kirtland Air Force Base and Albuquerque, New Mexico. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1980

- **1980, The Roswell Incident.** Berlitz and Moore publish, combining Marcel's account with the second-hand Barney Barnett material and creating the two-site structure that no later Roswell scenario has reconciled. Haut is described in it as 'not a witness'. On the node The Roswell Incident (Berlitz and Moore, 1980). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-01, Status Report II.** 'The UFO Crash/Retrieval Syndrome: New Sources, New Data', published by MUFON, introduces the letter-number case coding and the first Del Rio and Wright-Patterson entries. On the node UFO Crash/Retrieval Status Reports I-VII (Stringfield, 1978-1994). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-03-07, Soviet Navy anomalous phenomena reporting instruction.** Guidelines for the Soviet Navy on collecting anomalous phenomena data are signed by the Deputy Commander of the Main Headquarters of the Navy, Vice Admiral Saakyan. On the node SETKA-AN / SETKA-MO (later Galaktika, then Gorizont) - Soviet state anomalous phenomena programme. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **1980-06-01, CAUS sues NSA.** Civil Action No. 80-1562 is filed. NSA locates UFO-related records, withholds them, and defends the withholding with a classified affidavit by its Chief of Policy, Eugene Yeates. On the node Citizens Against UFO Secrecy v. National Security Agency (Civil Action No. 80-1562). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-08-08, Manzano intrusions and the Bennewitz operation.** AFOSI at Kirtland AFB investigates intrusions at the Manzano Weapons Storage Area adjacent to Sandia while simultaneously feeding fabricated crash-retrieval material to the physicist Paul Bennewitz through Special Agent Richard Doty. On the node Air Force Office of Special Investigations. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-08-08, Kirtland AFB / Manzano incident.** Three 1608 SPS airmen watch an object make stop-and-go manoeuvres near the Manzano restricted test range, descend into Coyote Canyon and depart vertically at high speed; a Sandia security guard reports a disc near an alarmed structure hours later. On the node Kirtland AFB / Manzano Weapons Storage Area incident. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-09-09, AFOSI complaint form filed by Richard Doty.** District File No. 8017D93-0/29 documents the Kirtland sightings. The same AFOSI office would shortly begin running fabricated material at Albuquerque physicist Paul Bennewitz. On the node Richard C. Doty. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-11-10, AFOSI Kirtland record on Coyote Canyon.** An AFOSI document records Kirtland AFB security police observing unexplained aerial objects over the Coyote Canyon / Manzano Weapons Storage Area, and notes Paul Bennewitz's reporting and photography. On the node AFOSI Kirtland AFB UFO File (1980-1983). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-12-26, Rendlesham Forest incident begins.** USAF security patrolmen at the RAF Woodbridge east gate report lights descending into the forest; three enter on foot and report a metallic triangular object. On the node Rendlesham Forest Incident, RAF Woodbridge/Bentwaters. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1980-12-29, Cash-Landrum encounter.** Betty Cash, Vickie Landrum and Colby Landrum stop on FM 1485 near Huffman, Texas before a bright diamond-shaped object expelling flame, followed by a formation of tandem-rotor helicopters. All three report illness; Cash is hospitalised from 2 January 1981. On the node Betty Cash. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1981

- **1981-01-13, Halt signs the 'Unexplained Lights' memo.** The deputy base commander documents the object, three ground depressions and beta/gamma readings of 0.1 milliroentgens in an official memorandum to the RAF station commander. On the node Halt Memorandum, 'Unexplained Lights', 13 January 1981. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1981-11-03, D.C. Circuit affirms NSA's total withholding.** The Court of Appeals sustains the withholding on the classified showing; the Supreme Court denies certiorari on 8 March 1982. A federal court has now confirmed that a US intelligence agency holds UFO material it cannot describe in public. On the node National Security Agency. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1981-11-03, D.C. Circuit affirms NSA withholding.** The court of appeals affirms judgment for NSA on the basis of an affidavit the plaintiffs were never permitted to see. On the node Citizens Against UFO Secrecy v. National Security Agency (Civil Action No. 80-1562). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1981-11-03, The courts sustain the NSA wall.** The D.C. Circuit upholds NSA's withholding of its UFO-related holdings in Citizens Against UFO Secrecy v. NSA, on the strength of a classified affidavit the plaintiffs never see; certiorari is denied in March 1982. This sets the practical ceiling on FOIA litigation in this field. On the node Freedom of Information Practice. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 1982

- **1982, Army IG investigation.** Lt. Col. George Sarran of the Office of the Inspector General of the US Army conducts the only thorough formal government investigation of the Cash-Landrum encounter. He finds no evidence the helicopters were US armed forces aircraft, while stating the witnesses were credible and no one appeared to be exaggerating. On the node Cash v. United States, Civil Action No. H-84-348 (S.D. Tex.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1982-02, Northrop TACIT BLUE first flight.** The U.S. Air Force fact sheet states 'The aircraft made its first flight in February 1982, and by the conclusion of the program in 1985, had flown 135 times.' This is what a real black aircraft programme of the era looks like in the primary record: 48 ft 2 in span, curved surfaces, two Garrett turbofans, no armament. On the node Northrop TACIT BLUE. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1982-03-08, Supreme Court denies certiorari.** The withholding of UFO-related signals intelligence becomes settled law. The government's position has never been that the records do not exist. On the node Citizens Against UFO Secrecy v. National Security Agency (Civil Action No. 80-1562). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1982-05-17, The F-117 moves to Tonopah.** The 4450th Tactical Group relocates from Groom Lake to the Tonopah Test Range. A manned operational combat squadron will fly from a hidden base for six years before acknowledgement in November 1988. On the node Tonopah Test Range, Nevada. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1982-06-27, Los Alamos Monitor front page calls Lazar a physicist at LAMPF.** The Los Alamos Monitor of Sunday 27 June 1982, pages A1 and A8, carries 'LA Man Joins The Jet Set - At 200 Miles An Hour' by staff writer Terry England, about a jet-powered Honda Lazar had built, and says in the paper's own voice: 'To Lazar, a physicist at the Los Alamos Meson Physics Facility, the important thing is the jet engine.' The earliest dated artefact in the cluster, seven years before the UFO claim. On the node Los Alamos Monitor, 27 June 1982, 'LA Man Joins The Jet Set - At 200 Miles An Hour'. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1982-06-28, Edward Teller lectures at Los Alamos the day after the jet-car story.** Tom Mahood's 2018 retrospective places Teller's Los Alamos lecture on the day after the Monitor article, calling the timing an 'amazing display of totally fucked synchronicity'. Lazar's own 1991 narration instead places the newspaper story and the Teller speech on the same day. The newspaper's date is fixed, so both cannot be right, and the board carries the anecdote as 'within a day'. No newspaper notice of the lecture was opened. On the node Robert "Bob" Lazar. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
### 1983

- **1983-01-01, CUFON goes online.** The Computer UFO Network begins publishing full-text FOIA releases electronically from Seattle, establishing the document-with-case-number standard of evidence. On the node Computer UFO Network (CUFON). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1983-04-09, Doty shows Howe the AQUARIUS paper.** AFOSI Special Agent Richard Doty shows documentary producer Linda Moulton Howe a purported government briefing paper at Kirtland AFB. She may read but not copy it; the promised film footage never materialises. On the node Project AQUARIUS (alleged). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1983-06-09, INSCOM puts a written assessment of the Monroe Institute's Gateway Process on the record.** A twenty-nine page memorandum from the US Army Operational Group, US Army Intelligence and Security Command, Fort George G. Meade, analyses the Gateway Experience on tasking from its own commander and states that it is meant as 'a useful guide for other USAINSCOM personnel who are required to take the Gateway training or work with Gateway materials'. It names remote viewing as a follow-on technique inside the method and concludes that there is 'a sound, rational basis in terms of physical science parameters for considering Gateway to be plausible in terms of its essential objectives'. On the node The Monroe Institute. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1984

- **1984, Clear Intent.** Fawcett and Greenwood publish the FOIA-sourced counterweight to the anonymous-testimony literature, reproducing the NORAD, NMCC and Joint Chiefs documents on the 1975 overflights. On the node Clear Intent: The Government Coverup of the UFO Experience (1984). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1984-01-18, Cash v. United States filed.** Betty Cash, Vickie Landrum and Colby Landrum sue the United States in the Southern District of Texas under the Federal Tort Claims Act, Civil Action No. H-84-348. Peter A. Gersten is attorney in charge. On the node Cash v. United States, Civil Action No. H-84-348 (S.D. Tex.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1984-07-10, The letter Fouche later calls the 'SAC/DARPA Classified Document' is written.** A Headquarters Strategic Air Command letter of 10 July 1984, 'REPLY TO ATTN OF: DARPA', addressed to office code LGMQ, states 'HQ AFMPC has approved an AAR 51[redacted] on MSgt (32674) Fouche as requested. MSgt Fouche's date of availability is Aug 87.' It is signed by DALE E. HAGERMAN, TSgt, USAF, NCOIC, Amn Acft Maint Asgns Sec, Dir of Asgns, DCS/Pers. The page image carries no classification marking of any kind, and all redactions on it were applied by Fouche himself on his own caption's admission. On the node Edgar Fouche's Military, and DOD contractors reference Documents. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1984-12, The MJ-12 film arrives.** William L. Moore and Jaime Shandera receive an anonymous roll of undeveloped 35mm film containing the Eisenhower Briefing Document and the Truman-Forrestal memorandum. The delivery falls inside the period Moore later says he was cooperating with AFOSI. On the node William L. Moore. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1984-12-11, MJ-12 film arrives postmarked Albuquerque.** An anonymous roll of undeveloped 35mm film reaches Jaime Shandera in Los Angeles, containing the purported 1952 Eisenhower briefing on Operation Majestic-12 - mailed from the city where the AFOSI operation was running. On the node MJ-12 / Operation Majestic-12 Documents. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1984-12-11, The Majestic-12 film arrives.** Unsolicited film purporting to show an Eisenhower-era briefing document on recovered craft is delivered to Jaime Shandera. The FBI investigates and its file records an AFOSI assessment that the documents are bogus. Whatever their origin, their circulation imposes a permanent evidentiary tax on every genuine document in the field. On the node Majestic-12 Corpus. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 1985

- **1985-01-01, IASA founded.** Puthoff establishes the Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin, incorporated under EarthTech International in 1991. On the node Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1985-01-01, USAF fact sheet denies Wright-Patterson holdings.** Wright-Patterson's public affairs office states: 'There are not now nor ever have been, any extraterrestrial visitors or equipment on Wright-Patterson Air Force Base.' On the node Operation BLUE FLY. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1985-02-08, Aurora appears in the Pentagon's unclassified procurement list.** The Washington Post reports that the Pentagon's own unclassified P-1 document shows spending on an Air Force programme called Aurora rising from zero to $80 million in FY1986 and $2.27 billion in FY1987. Reminded that its own department had published the figures, an Air Force spokesman said his orders were to say only 'It's a classified program.' Five years before the Aviation Week story. On the node Aurora (FY1986-FY1987 procurement line). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1985-03-02, INSCOM deactivates CENTER LANE and attaches its assets to DIA.** INSCOM notifies the Department of the Army of 'the deactivation of the INSCOM CENTER LANE Project, the attachment OPCON of CENTER LANE assets and resources to the Defense Intelligence Agency, and the retirement of CENTER LANE as an active project nickname', and asks HQDA to officially discontinue CENTER LANE 'as a Secretary of the Army designated Special Access Program'. The initiative was INSCOM's own: its commanding general had recommended discontinuing the work in July 1984. On the node US Army Intelligence and Security Command (INSCOM). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1985-06, Status Report IV.** 'The Fatal Encounter at Ft. Dix-McGuire' opens with a prologue titled 'The Burden of Proof' in which Stringfield states that public research does not have proof and that affidavits, leaked documents and photographs are not proof. On the node UFO Crash/Retrieval Status Reports I-VII (Stringfield, 1978-1994). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1986

- **1986-01-01, Remote viewing transfers to DIA as SUN STREAK.** The programme moves from Army INSCOM to the Defense Intelligence Agency - the same agency that will contract BAASS twenty-two years later. On the node SUN STREAK. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1986-05-19, The Brazilian 'Night of the UFOs'.** Twenty-one unidentified radar plots over four Brazilian states. Five Brazilian Air Force fighters are scrambled from Santa Cruz and Anápolis. One Mirage closes to two miles of a contact that zigzags and then accelerates away from a supersonic interceptor; the pilot later estimates Mach 15. Thirteen plots appear astern of an F-5 which turns 180 degrees and sees nothing. On the node Noite Oficial dos OVNIs - the Brazilian 'Night of the UFOs', 19 May 1986. Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1986-05-23, Moreira Lima's press conference.** At 16:30 the Brazilian Minister of Aeronautics convenes the press with the five intercept pilots and the air traffic controllers and says: 'It is not a question of believing or not believing. We can only give technical information. There are several hypotheses. Technically, I would tell you gentlemen that we have no explanation.' He promises a full dossier within thirty days. It never appears. On the node Brigadeiro Octávio Júlio Moreira Lima. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **1986-06-02, Brazilian Air Defence Command records solid, intelligent objects.** Brigadier José Pessoa Cavalcanti de Albuquerque signs a CONFIDENCIAL Relatório de Ocorrências concluding that 'the phenomena are solid and reflect in a certain manner intelligence, by the capacity to follow and to maintain distance from observers as well as to fly in formation, not necessarily crewed'. It stays classified for twenty-three years. On the node Relatório de Ocorrências, Comando Aéreo de Defesa Aérea, 2 June 1986 (Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1986-07-24, GAO tells Congress a carve-out contractor lost control of its documents.** GAO testifies to the House Energy and Commerce Subcommittee on Oversight and Investigations that Lockheed reported 1,460 discrepancies in a 40,000-item special-access inventory, 11 of them still unresolved. In the same statement it explains that since 1965 special access programs have been excluded from Defense Investigative Service supervision, and that this is why they are called carve-out contracts. On the node GAO accession 130581 - Controls Over Classified Documents for a Special Access Program at Lockheed Corporation. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1986-08-21, Cash-Landrum dismissed.** Judge Ross N. Sterling enters an Order of Dismissal under Federal Rules of Civil Procedure 12(b)(1), 12(b)(6) and 56 on the United States' motion. The only US UFO personal-injury case to reach federal court ends without trial. On the node Cash v. United States, Civil Action No. H-84-348 (S.D. Tex.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1987

- **1987-05-29, MJ-12 documents released publicly.** Moore, Shandera and Friedman publish the material. NARA's subsequent searches produce nothing but a single problematic memorandum in Record Group 341. On the node MJ-12 / Operation Majestic-12 Documents. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1987-08-31, End of Fouche's Strategic Air Command liaison post at Kelly AFB; active service concludes.** The Meritorious Service Medal citation with oak leaf cluster reads 'as the Strategic Air Command Liaison Officer, San Antonio Air Logistics Center, Kelly Air Force Base, Texas, from 17 July 1983 to 31 August 1987', and closes 'The singularly distinctive accomplishments of Sergeant Fouche culminate a distinguished career in the service of his country.' The certificate is signed by John T. Chain, Jr., General, USAF, Commander in Chief. On the node Edgar Fouche. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1987-12-04, 10 U.S.C. 119 enacted, and the waiver with it.** Pub. L. 100-180, div. A, title XI, sec. 1132(a)(1), 101 Stat. 1151 adds section 119 to title 10. Subsection (e) -- the waiver that lets the Secretary of Defense strip content from the SAP reports and deliver it only to the chairman and ranking minority member of each defense committee -- is present in the original enactment and has never been amended. Sec. 1132(b)-(c) required the first reports to cover all EXISTING SAPs and five prior fiscal years of budget history. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1987-12-04, Congress first requires DoD to report special access programs.** President Reagan signs Public Law 100-180.
