Section 1132(a)(1) adds section 119 to title 10, creating for the first time a statutory duty to tell the defense committees that a special access program exists. Every alleged legacy activity from the 1940s to this date ran under no SAP reporting obligation at all. The same section creates the subsection (e) waiver in the same breath. On the node 10 U.S.C. 119, Special Access Programs: Congressional Oversight. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1987-12-29, John Lear's statement is dated, eighteen months before Lazar reaches KLAS.** A statement by John Lear dated 29 December 1987, originally a physical letter to the Borderland Sciences Research Foundation and preserved in the BSRA/BSRF holdings at the Archives for the Unexplained, circulated on the ParaNet bulletin board in early 1988. It precedes Lazar's KLAS appearance by roughly eighteen months and set the frame the later account fitted into. Only the archive item record was opened; the statement's text was not rendered and no content from it is asserted. On the node John Olsen Lear. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
### 1988

- **1988-01-01, Bennewitz hospitalised.** After eight years of fabricated material fed to him through AFOSI channels, Paul Bennewitz is hospitalised for psychiatric care and withdraws from the field. On the node Paul Frederick Bennewitz. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1988-06-23, Christic Institute sanctioned.** Judge James Lawrence King dismisses Avirgan v. Hull and orders Daniel Sheehan's Christic Institute to pay $955,000 in fees and $79,500 in costs under Rule 11, criticising reliance on witnesses who were dead, unwilling, or one person removed from the facts. Recorded because the same evidentiary pattern recurs in the UAP context and hostile reviewers will reach for this first. On the node Daniel P. Sheehan. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1988-11-12, CLAIMED ONLY: a classified exhibit in the Big Hangar with three saucer-shaped craft.** The load-bearing claim of the whole thread, and it rests on one second-hand chain. McCandlish's December 2000 testimony gives the date and the day: Sorensen 'told me how he had seen these three flying saucers in this hangar at Norton Air Force Base on November 12, 1988 - it was a Saturday.' The 1990 primary document, taken from the claimed eyewitness himself, places it only 'in early Nov. 88'. No record of any exhibit was opened. On the node Alien Reproduction Vehicle (ARV) / Flux Liner. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1988-11-12, Air show at Norton AFB; SR-71A 61-7962 flies and departs.** A private photographic collection of Blackbird aircraft carries a section headed 'Norton Air Force Base Airshow, November 12, 1988', with captions including 'SR-71A 61-7962 at Norton AFB on November 12, 1988' and 'The crew of SR-71A 61-7962 shake hands before departing the November 12, 1988 Norton Air Force Base airshow'. This is the only opened record that dates the air show independently of the ARV account. On the node Norton AFB air show, 12 November 1988, and the alleged classified exhibit. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1988-12, Lazar's claimed employment at S-4 begins.** In his 1991 narration Lazar says that 'between December of 88 and April of 89' he worked at 'a facility at an area known as S4 on the Nellis Air Force range in central Nevada', and that 'I was hired in December of 1988 to replace one of these men'. This row records the claim and its date, not any employment. On the node S-4 / Papoose Lake, Nevada. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1989

- **1989-01, Status Report V.** 'Is the Coverup Lid Lifting?' surveys Roswell, Aztec, Kingman, El Yunque and Brown County, Ohio, and argues that confirming a single retrieval would compel eventual sharing of alien propulsion and materials technology. On the node UFO Crash/Retrieval Status Reports I-VII (Stringfield, 1978-1994). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1989-03, George Knapp begins interviewing Lazar.** Tom Mahood's timeline records that Knapp began conducting initial interviews with Lazar in March 1989 'for safekeeping purposes'. No 1989-dated artefact was opened and this row is Tier 3 evidence carried on a Tier C node. On the node George Knapp. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1989-03-22, First Wednesday desert viewing in Tikaboo Valley.** Lazar, his wife, Gene Huff and John Lear watched lights in Tikaboo Valley. Mahood's timeline records that only Lear saw an elliptical shape through a telescope. Huff's own synopsis of 12 March 1995 gives the same date. On the node Gene Huff. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1989-04-05, Third viewing ends with the group detained by the Lincoln County Sheriff.** On the third Wednesday viewing the group, which included Lazar, Gene Huff and John Lear, was discovered by guards and detained by the Lincoln County Sheriff. Both the claimants' own accounts and the principal sceptical account agree this happened; they disagree about what the group was watching. Tom Mahood's counter-explanation is that the whole alien-craft narrative began as a cover story after this detention. On the node Gene Huff. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1989-05, KLAS-TV airs the silhouetted interviews under the pseudonym 'Dennis'.** TIER 3 DATE, CARRIED HONESTLY. Tom Mahood's timeline records that in May 1989 Lazar's silhouetted interviews with George Knapp aired on KLAS-TV using the pseudonym 'Dennis'. NO 1989-DATED ARTEFACT WAS OPENED BY THIS BOARD, and KLAS's own retrospectives returned HTTP 403 three times on 18 September 2026. On the node The KLAS-TV Lazar broadcasts, 1989. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1989-05-01, Lazar goes on television in Las Vegas.** George Knapp broadcasts the Robert Lazar interviews on KLAS-TV, creating the public reverse-engineering narrative attached to Area 51 and S-4. No primary record has ever corroborated it. On the node Area 51 (Homey Airport / Groom Lake operating location). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1989-07, Moore's AFOSI admission.** At the MUFON symposium in Las Vegas, William L. Moore states publicly that he had cooperated with Air Force Office of Special Investigations personnel from about 1980 and had knowingly passed disinformation to Paul Bennewitz. On the node William L. Moore. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1989-07-01, Moore admits the AFOSI collaboration.** At the MUFON symposium Bill Moore tells the UFO community that he worked with Air Force counterintelligence and knowingly passed disinformation to Bennewitz in exchange for access. On the node William L. Moore. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1989-07-01, Moore admits the Bennewitz deception.** William Moore states publicly that he attempted to push Bennewitz toward a breakdown by feeding him false information. Bennewitz had been hospitalised in August 1988; he dies 23 June 2003. On the node Kirtland Air Force Base and Albuquerque, New Mexico. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1989-11-01, Knapp airs Lazar.** George Knapp's KLAS-TV broadcast reveals Bob Lazar's identity and his S-4 claims, putting Area 51 into global consciousness and establishing the template of the insider-plus-local-reporter disclosure. On the node George Knapp. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1989-11-01, Knapp broadcasts the S-4 reverse-engineering claim.** George Knapp reports Bob Lazar's account of working on recovered craft at S-4 near Groom Lake, employed through EG&G. The modern contractor-custody narrative starts here. On the node EG&G (Edgerton, Germeshausen and Grier). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1989-11-10, KLAS-TV identifies and interviews Lazar under his own name.** TIER 3 DATE, CARRIED HONESTLY. Mahood's timeline gives 10 November 1989; some accounts give 11 November. This is the broadcast that put Area 51 into worldwide public consciousness and no artefact of it was opened by this board. The only contemporaneous documentary confirmation opened is the Las Vegas Review-Journal of 19 June 1990 recording that Lazar 'was the focal point for much of KLAS-TV, Channel 8 reporter George Knapp's award winning series'. On the node The KLAS-TV Lazar broadcasts, 1989. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1990

- **1990-03-31, The Belgian Air Force scrambles F-16s during the Belgian wave.** The National Archives' guide to the MoD UFO files opened in August 2009 states that the files 'reveal how in March 1990 the Belgian Air Force scrambled F-16 fighters to intercept UFOs reported by police officers and others', and places a four-page account by General Wilfried de Brouwer, Chief of Operations, Belgian Air Staff, in DEFE 24/1960, confirming that the F-16 pilots obtained radar lock-ons but could not explain the phenomena. Malcolm Rifkind later told Lord Hill-Norton that the MoD was not informed at the time. On the node Belgian Air Force F-16 intercept and the 1989-90 Belgian wave. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1990-06-05, Lazar arrested for pandering in Las Vegas.** Mahood's timeline records the arrest on 5 June 1990. The offence, per the police account printed in the Review-Journal a fortnight later, was recruiting a prostitute, operating her business from an apartment complex, maintaining computer records of customers and their licence plate numbers, and taking at least half her earnings. On the node Clark County, Nevada felony pandering file, 1990 and 1996. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1990-06-18, Guilty plea to felony pandering.** Lazar pleaded guilty to felony pandering in District Court. The Las Vegas Review-Journal reported it the next day under the headline 'Source in Channel 8's UFO series pleads guilty to pandering charge', by Warren Bates, page 8B, noting the exposure as 'one to six years, with a possible fine of $5,000'. On the node Clark County, Nevada felony pandering file, 1990 and 1996. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1990-07-27, Pre-Sentence Report records the Pacifica, MIT and Caltech credential list.** THE SPINE OF THE CREDENTIAL QUESTION. Lazar's Pre-Sentence Report of 27 July 1990 lists his own reported education: '1978, Bachelor of Science Degree in Physics and Electronic Technology, Pacifica University'; '1982, Masters of Science in Physics, MIT'; '1985, Masters of Science in Electronic Technology, Cal Tech'. Because this is a court document rather than a television statement, the credential question is Tier 1 evidence and not gossip. No institution has confirmed any of the three. Gene Huff's rebuttal of 15 May 1995, that the probation officer misread two Pacifica correspondence courses, is carried beside it and is not adopted. On the node Clark County, Nevada felony pandering file, 1990 and 1996. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1990-08-13, Scott's handwritten sheet headed 'Mark - 8/13/90'.** One of the handwritten sheets on Aviation Week & Space Technology letterhead in the 31 page scan is headed, circled, 'Mark - 8/13/90'. It is the earliest dated page in the document. On the node William B. Scott's interview notes on 'Brad', 3 December 1990. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1990-08-20, Sentenced by Judge Jack Lehman to probation, community service and psychotherapy.** District Judge Jack Lehman sentenced Lazar to probation rather than prison, with 150 hours of community service, an order to 'stay away from brothels and undergo psychotherapy', and a year of psychotherapy, saying he 'could not make a determination regarding his claims'. The same report describes Lazar's claim as of that date as being that 'the U.S. government was testing alien spacecraft at the Nevada Test Site, 65 miles northwest of Las Vegas', which is a different named place from the one the claim later settled on. On the node Clark County, Nevada felony pandering file, 1990 and 1996. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1990-10, Chief Financial Officers Act; FASAB created.** The CFO Act imposes audited financial statements on major federal agencies. In the same month the Secretary of the Treasury, the Director of OMB and the Comptroller General create the Federal Accounting Standards Advisory Board to set federal accounting standards. The Department of Defense will not attempt a full-scope audit for twenty-seven years. On the node Federal Accounting Standards Advisory Board (FASAB). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1990-10-01, YEAR INFERRED: Aviation Week prints McCandlish's paintings of two aircraft.** The 1990 interview notes state 'Brad is the source of artwork done by Mark McCandlish and featured in AVWK's Oct. 1 issue.' The year is 1990 only by the document's own December 1990 dating, and the issue itself was NOT OPENED. It is the highest-value gap on this lead. On the node Mark McCandlish. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1990-12-03, Scott interviews 'Brad' for one hour in his Pasadena office, on two tapes.** William B. Scott, senior engineering editor of Aviation Week & Space Technology, interviewed the claimed eyewitness for one paid hour: 'He agreed to a one-hr. session in his Pasadena office, pointedly telling me that he gets $200/hr. for his time, and one hr. was all he could give me.' Internal markers show two tapes. On the node William B. Scott's interview notes on 'Brad', 3 December 1990. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1990-12-10, Handwritten addendum: 'As of 12/10/90 - additional info from Brad'.** Page 16 of the transcript carries a handwritten addendum in the same hand, followed by notes on a diamond-shaped aircraft slowing to subsonic speeds to dispense weapons. The interview was followed up before the notes were compiled. On the node William B. Scott's interview notes on 'Brad', 3 December 1990. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1990-12-11, The 16 page transcript is compiled: 'COMPILED 12-11-90; SCOTT/AVWK LANCASTER'.** The typed transcript is hand-numbered 1 to 16 and headed 'NOTES FROM INTERVIEW ON 12-3-90 WITH BRAD' and 'COMPILED 12-11-90; SCOTT/AVWK LANCASTER'. Nothing in the 31 pages is a published article; Aviation Week printed the artwork, not the interview. On the node William B. Scott's interview notes on 'Brad', 3 December 1990. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1991

- **1991, The Lazar Tape fixes the whole claim in datable form.** Tri-Dot Productions publishes 'The Lazar Tape and Excerpts from the Government Bible', forty minutes, created by Gene Huff and Bob Lazar. It is the strongest artefact in the cluster and the one opened by this board: its English auto-generated captions, 1,046 segments to 38:39, were read in full on 18 September 2026. Every element-115 claim, every S-4 description and the Teller anecdote carry timestamps in it. On the node The Lazar Tape and Excerpts from the Government Bible (1991). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1991, UFO Crash at Roswell.** Randle and Schmitt publish the book that drives Roswell into congressional attention. Haut, described as 'not a witness' in 1980, now appears as a witness, though not to the debris. On the node Kevin D. Randle. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1991-01-01, Senate Appropriations opens the first contractor inquiry.** Richard D'Amato, staffer to Senate Democratic Leader Robert C. Byrd, is tasked to inquire into the New Mexico crash allegations. He visits the Nevada facility and reports back that nothing merits further Senate investigation. On the node Richard D'Amato. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1991-10-01, The 30-day pre-initiation bar takes effect.** 10 U.S.C. 119(f) -- 'A special access program may not be initiated until (1) the defense committees are notified of the program; and (2) a period of 30 days elapses' -- was added by Pub. L. 101-510 sec. 1482(a)(2) and took effect 1 October 1991 per sec. 1482(d). Before that date no statutory waiting period applied. Subsection (f) is not among the subsections that (e)(1) permits the Secretary to waive. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 1992

- **1992, The Plains of San Agustin Controversy.** Mark Rodeghier of CUFOS and Fred Whiting of the Fund for UFO Research publish an investigation concluding against Gerald Anderson, the only claimed first-hand witness to the second 1947 crash site. The field audits its own star witness and publishes against interest. On the node Gerald Anderson. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1992-09-18, NRO's existence declassified.** After 31 years the Deputy Secretary of Defense authorises acknowledgement that the National Reconnaissance Office exists. The precedent cuts both ways: it proves concealment at scale is possible, and it proves it eventually ends. On the node National Reconnaissance Office. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1993

- **1993-01-01, O'Donnell says nothing to the Senate investigator.** Knapp arranges a meeting between Al O'Donnell of EG&G and D'Amato with the blessing of EG&G co-founder Herbert Grier. O'Donnell, who had privately described a recovered saucer and a live being, tells the investigator nothing. On the node Alfred 'Al' O'Donnell. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1993-01-01, The Rich remark.** Ben Rich is reported to have told a UCLA alumni audience that the means to travel among the stars already exist but are locked inside black projects. No recording or transcript has ever been produced. On the node Ben R. Rich. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1993-01-01, Rockefeller Initiative begins.** Laurance Rockefeller begins funding a coordinated campaign to obtain presidential declassification of US government UFO holdings. On the node The Rockefeller UFO Disclosure Initiative. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1993-01-06, E.O. 12829 creates the National Industrial Security Program.** 58 FR 3479. Section 101: 'There is established a National Industrial Security Program. The purpose of this program is to safeguard classified information that may be released or has been released to current, prospective, or former contractors, licensees, or grantees.' The Secretary of Defense as Executive Agent issues the NISPOM; the DCSA carve-out is a carve-out from this program. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1993-01-06, Executive Order 12829 creates the National Industrial Security Program.** The order directs the Secretary of Defense to issue and maintain the NISP Operating Manual and designates the Defense Security Service as the DoD Cognizant Security Office - creating the inspection function that a carve-out would later switch off. On the node Executive Order 12829, National Industrial Security Program (6 Jan 1993). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1993-03-31, More than thirty sightings over central England in six hours, then a rocket re-entry.** The MoD's UFO desk had the RAF replay radar tapes and 'found nothing unusual had been detected'; the Assistant Chief of the Air Staff was nonetheless briefed 'that there was evidence a UFO had evaded UK defences'. The same official guide then records that 'It soon emerged that the majority of sightings were caused by the Russian rocket that launched the Cosmos 2238 satellite, re-entering the earths atmosphere.' Both halves are in one paragraph of one guide. On the node Cosford incident, 30-31 March 1993. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1993-05-14, Haut's sworn affidavit.** Walter Haut signs an affidavit stating that Col. Blanchard saw the material, that there is no chance Blanchard would have mistaken it for a weather balloon and none that Marcel would have been mistaken. On the node Walter Haut. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1993-11-30, The government-wide SAP statute -- with DoD, NNSA and the IC written out of it.** Sec. 1152 of the FY1994 NDAA, Pub. L. 103-160, 107 Stat. 1758, now 50 U.S.C. 3348. Subsection (g)(2)(B) excludes the Department of Defense, the National Nuclear Security Administration, and every element of the Intelligence Community from the definition of 'covered department or agency'. Its waiver at (e)(2) keeps the audience at the full oversight committees rather than reducing it to chairs and ranking members. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1993-11-30, The government-wide SAP statute and the only statutory definition.** Section 1152 of the FY1994 NDAA, Public Law 103-160, imposes the same reporting regime on every federal agency that runs a SAP other than DoD, the nuclear weapons complex and the intelligence community, and supplies the only statutory definition of 'special access program' in the United States Code, now at 50 U.S.C. 3348(g)(3). On the node 50 U.S.C. 3348, Reports Relating to Certain SAPs and Similar Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1993-12-25, Baltimore Sun reports the Testors SR-75 Penetrator and XR-7 Thunderdart on sale.** 'Testor Corp. released the SR-75 Penetrator and XR-7 Thunderdart, the latest in a series of highly speculative military miniature models', designed by 'John Andrews, an aviation historian in San Diego who designed the new models, as well as the F-19'. The Air Force spokeswoman Capt. Mary Dillon is quoted: 'We're not saying no comment, we're saying such a plane does not exist.' Fouche's presentation of August 1998 presents the same SR-75 Penetrator designation and the same mothership architecture as inside knowledge. On the node Testor Corp. SR-75 Penetrator and XR-7 Thunderdart model kits, 1993. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
### 1994

- **1994-01-01, The 1994 SAP restructuring.** Gerb argues the Special Access Program enterprise was restructured in 1994 to gatekeep US government access and vest permanent control of the portfolio in a quasi-industry and government control group - the moment, on this account, at which the government ceased to be the controlling party in its own programme. On the node 'Manhattan Project 2.0' (alleged secrecy architecture). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1994-01-01, Rep. Schiff directs GAO to search the records.** After DoD refers his Roswell inquiry to the National Archives rather than answering it, Representative Steven Schiff orders a government-wide GAO records audit. On the node Steven H. Schiff. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1994-01-01, Groom Lake contractors sue.** Kasza v. Browner and Frost v. Perry allege exposure to hazardous waste burned in open pits. Judge Philip Pro rejects the state secrets claim; the suits are dismissed for lack of evidence and the Ninth Circuit affirms. On the node Groom Lake, Nevada. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1994-01-05, The Deputy Secretary of Defense stands up the SAPOC, sixteen years before the directive the board cites.** 'On January 5th, 1994, the Deputy Secretary of Defense (DEPSECDEF) significantly enhanced changes in the DoD Special Access Program (SAP) management and control structures. ... The plan standardized and formalized the SAP approval, termination, revalidation and restructuring process through the Special Access Program Oversight Committee (SAPOC). A Senior Review Group (SRG) was established to support the SAPOC principals.' The Joint Security Commission called the committee a 'recent formation' on 28 February 1994. The board's sapoc node dated the committee to DoD Directive 5205.07 of 1 July 2010; the 2010 issuance re-established a body that had run since 1994. On the node DoD special access programme governance change, 5 January 1994. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1994-02, Status Report VII.** 'Search for Proof in a Hall of Mirrors', Stringfield's last, opens with Walter Scott's 'I say the tale as 'twas told to me' and acknowledges the Fund for UFO Research, MUFON, CUFOS and the Mid-Ohio Research Associates. He dies ten months later. On the node UFO Crash/Retrieval Status Reports I-VII (Stringfield, 1978-1994). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1994-06-09, The Navy codifies the NCIS title, and it is not 1992.** A Department of the Navy final rule amends 32 CFR 701.5(e)(2)(iii)(B) by removing 'Commander, Naval Investigative Service Command' and adding in its place 'Director, Naval Criminal Investigative Service'. A Navy document published eight days earlier still lists the old title among the officers it designates. This is the earliest federal record of the new name reached by this pass; the commonly cited 1992 redesignation date is supported by nothing opened. On the node Naval Criminal Investigative Service. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1994-07, Air Force report to GAO.** Col. Richard L. Weaver, Director of Security and Special Program Oversight in the Office of the Secretary of the Air Force, and 1st Lt. James McAndrew deliver the report concluding the debris was Project Mogul. It also records that the Secretary released former airmen and contract scientists from nondisclosure obligations in writing. On the node The Roswell Report: Fact Versus Fiction in the New Mexico Desert (1995). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1994-09-08, Air Force abandons the weather balloon story.** Under pressure from Rep. Steven Schiff the Air Force concedes the 1947 statement was false and attributes the debris to the then-classified Project MOGUL. On the node The Roswell Reports (USAF, 1994-1997). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1995

- **1995-01-01, 'The Best Available Evidence' distributed.** Marie Galbraith's Rockefeller-funded briefing document goes to legislators, senior officials and heads of state. No policy response follows. On the node UFO Briefing Document: The Best Available Evidence (1995). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1995-01-01, STARGATE declassified and terminated.** CIA releases roughly 12,000 pages of the remote-viewing programme record and the effort is closed - the one case where a compartmented programme in a ridiculed domain was fully documented after the fact. On the node STAR GATE and predecessor remote viewing programs, 1970-1995. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-01-01, The President exempts a place that officially does not exist.** Clinton issues a determination exempting 'the Air Force's operating location near Groom Lake, Nevada' from environmental disclosure law - the only formal recognition to that date that Groom Lake is more than part of the Nellis complex. It is reissued annually. On the node Groom Lake, Nevada. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-01-01, NIDS founded.** Robert Bigelow founds the National Institute for Discovery Science in Las Vegas, assembling the staff and methodology later inherited by BAASS. On the node National Institute for Discovery Science (NIDS). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-01-01, STAR GATE is terminated.** CIA's AIR evaluation finds remote viewing information 'vague and ambiguous' and unusable for actionable intelligence. The roughly $20 million programme ends; the records are published to CREST in January 2017. On the node STAR GATE and predecessor remote viewing programs, 1970-1995. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-07-28, GAO reports Roswell message traffic destroyed.** GAO/NSIAD-95-187, produced at Rep. Steven Schiff's request, finds that Roswell Army Air Field outgoing messages for March 1945 to December 1949 were destroyed with no destruction certificate located to authorise it. On the node Government Accountability Office. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-07-28, GAO: Roswell records destroyed, authority unknown.** GAO/NSIAD-95-187 finds RAAF administrative records (Mar 1945-Dec 1949) and outgoing messages (Oct 1946-Dec 1949) destroyed, with the disposition form recording neither who destroyed them nor under what authority. On the node GAO/NSIAD-95-187, Government Records: Results of a Search for Records Concerning the 1947 Crash Near Roswell, New Mexico. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-07-28, GAO finds the records destroyed.** GAO reports that RAAF outgoing messages (Oct 1946 - Dec 1949) and administrative records (Mar 1945 - Dec 1949) were destroyed, and that the disposition form does not record who destroyed them, when, or under what authority. On the node GAO/NSIAD-95-187, Results of a Search for Records Concerning the 1947 Crash Near Roswell. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-07-28, GAO finds the Roswell records destroyed.** GAO/NSIAD-95-187 reports that RAAF outgoing messages from October 1946 to December 1949 were destroyed with no disposition authority located. No agency has ever rebutted it. On the node Roswell Incident. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-08-21, Clintons visit Rockefeller's JY Ranch.** The President and First Lady visit Laurance Rockefeller in Jackson, Wyoming - documented in Clinton Library FOIA collection 2006-0546-F, and the occasion on which Rockefeller is reported to have pressed the disclosure case directly. On the node Laurance S. Rockefeller. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1995-10-10, First presidential Groom Lake exemption published.** A Presidential Determination exempting 'the Air Force's Operating Location Near Groom Lake, Nevada' from federal environmental disclosure requirements appears in the Federal Register. It is repeated annually thereafter. On the node Presidential Determinations on the Air Force's Operating Location Near Groom Lake. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
### 1996

- **1996, Petition to withdraw the 1990 guilty plea is refused.** Lazar petitioned to withdraw the plea, citing post-traumatic stress, hidden federal records and inadequate legal representation. The District Attorney opposed, arguing 'that Lazar's new claims were irrelevant to his admitted and documented criminal activity'. The judge denied the petition and the conviction stands. On the node Clark County, Nevada felony pandering file, 1990 and 1996. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1996-01-01, Dark Skies airs.** Bryce Zabel creates and showruns Dark Skies, a prime-time dramatisation of a Majestic-12-style control group - a dateable marker of how long the control-group narrative has been in mass circulation. On the node Bryce Zabel. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1996-01-01, Knapp joins NIDS.** Knapp works with Robert Bigelow's National Institute of Discovery Science alongside Colm Kelleher; this period produces the Skinwalker Ranch material and the Reid-Bigelow introduction that leads to AAWSAP. On the node National Institute for Discovery Science (NIDS). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1996-01-01, Bechtel Nevada takes the Nevada Test Site contract.** Bechtel Nevada becomes DOE management and operating contractor for the Nevada Test Site, running the Remote Sensing Laboratory and Special Technologies Laboratory under Atomic Energy Act authorities outside the DoD SAP regime. On the node Bechtel Nevada Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1996-01-01, Carnegie tests the Howe samples.** Linda Moulton Howe says the bismuth/magnesium-zinc laminate was tested by Carnegie Science's Department of Terrestrial Magnetism, the first documented laboratory in that chain of custody. On the node ADAM Research Project (Acquisition and Data Analysis of Materials). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1996-01-01, Bigelow buys Skinwalker Ranch.** The 480-acre Sherman property in Uintah County, Utah is purchased with a condition that the sellers keep quiet about what they had witnessed; a research team and surveillance equipment move in. On the node Skinwalker Ranch, Uintah Basin, Utah. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1996-01-01, Bigelow buys the Utah ranch.** Bigelow purchases the Uintah Basin property later known as Skinwalker Ranch and begins instrumented NIDS study under Colm Kelleher. On the node Skinwalker Ranch, Uintah Basin, Utah. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1996-01-20, The Varginha encounter.** Three young women in Varginha, Minas Gerais report a crouching creature with red eyes and three fingers; a week earlier an ultralight pilot reported a cylindrical craft coming down outside the town. Rumours of military recovery and of an American removal follow. A 2010 Brazilian military inquiry attributes the sighting to a mentally disabled man and routine vehicle movements. On the node Varginha incident, Minas Gerais, January 1996. Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **1996-05, TACIT BLUE placed on public museum display.** 'The aircraft was placed on display at the museum in May 1996.' A real compartmented aircraft programme of the Groom Lake era ends in a public exhibition hall with a published fact sheet and a cockpit plaque naming its five pilots. On the node Northrop TACIT BLUE. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1996-08-26, Physicist David L. Morgan publishes the physics critique.** Dr David L. Morgan, a particle physicist, publishes 'A Physicist's Critique', revised April 2001. Three objections: that the strong nuclear force 'has NOTHING TO DO WITH GRAVITY'; that the nuclear force 'extends out to about the same distance away from a nucleus NO MATTER HOW MASSIVE THE NUCLEUS IS'; and, crucially without needing any model of gravity, that if the antimatter must be made on board then 'your system produces NO NET ENERGY AT ALL!!' On the node Element 115: the Lazar claim and moscovium. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
### 1997

- **1997, First intelligence budget disclosure in fifty years.** Following FOIA litigation brought by the Federation of American Scientists with Steven Aftergood as plaintiff, assisted by Kate Martin of the Center for National Security Studies, the Director of Central Intelligence declassifies the total intelligence budget: $26.6 billion for FY1997. It is the first such disclosure since the CIA was created. On the node Steven Aftergood. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1997, The Roswell Report: Case Closed.** James McAndrew publishes the bodies report through HQ USAF and the GPO. Section 2.1 works the 427th AAFBU Squadron 'M' morning reports and National Personnel Records Center holdings to show Glenn Dennis's 'Naomi Maria Selff' never existed, and identifies 1st Lt. Eileen M. Fanton as matching every other particular. On the node The Roswell Report: Case Closed (1997). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1997-01-01, CIA publishes its own UFO history.** Gerald K. Haines' 'CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-1990' appears in Studies in Intelligence, acknowledging the Office of Scientific Intelligence effort, the Robertson Panel and the later attribution of sightings to U-2 and OXCART overflights. On the node Central Intelligence Agency. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1997-01-01, CIA publishes the U-2 cover-story admission.** Gerald Haines' Studies in Intelligence history states that Blue Book investigators knowingly attributed U-2 sightings to ice crystals and temperature inversions and that 'this led the Air Force to make misleading and deceptive statements to the public.' On the node A Die-hard Issue: CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-1990. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1997-01-01, CIA admits official deception.** CIA's Studies in Intelligence publishes Gerald Haines's history, stating that reconnaissance secrecy 'led the Air Force to make misleading and deceptive statements to the public' and that mention of CIA sponsorship of the Robertson Panel was forbidden. On the node A Die-hard Issue: CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-1990. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1997-01-01, CIA concedes the false explanations.** CIA historian Gerald K. Haines publishes 'CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-90' in Studies in Intelligence, recording that U-2 and OXCART flights accounted for over half of late-1950s and 1960s reports and that Air Force investigators knowingly gave false explanations to the public. A documented admission of deliberate deception, from the deceiving agency. On the node The Cover-Up. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1997-04-09, Wilson's alleged SAP inquiry begins.** Per the Davis notes, Joint Staff J-2 Vice Admiral Thomas Wilson - prompted by the Disclosure Project's Washington briefings - begins trying to locate a special access programme holding recovered non-human material. On the node Thomas R. Wilson. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1997-06-24, 'The Roswell Report: Case Closed'.** The Air Force attributes reported bodies to anthropomorphic test dummies dropped in parachute tests conducted principally between 1953 and 1959. On the node The Roswell Reports (USAF, 1994-1997). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1997-09-01, DoD FOIA case 97-F-1677 released.** The Office of the Assistant Secretary of Defense releases both the Halt memo and the November 1975 Malmstrom overflight records to The Black Vault in a single case. On the node John Greenewald Jr. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1997-10-03, CEFAA created in Chile.** Gonzalo Miranda Aguirre, Director of Chile's Dirección General de Aeronáutica Civil, creates the Comité de Estudios de Fenómenos Aéreos Anómalos after a sighting wave over Arica and the expressed interest of the former Air Force Commander-in-Chief. Ricardo Bermúdez takes the directorship and holds it until 2016. On the node CEFAA / SEFAA - Chilean anomalous aerial phenomena office, Dirección General de Aeronáutica Civil. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1997-12, Stanton Friedman publishes 'The Bob Lazar Fraud'.** Friedman, a nuclear physicist who spent his career arguing that UFOs are real craft, publishes the credential investigation, updated January 2011. He located the professor Lazar named as a Caltech instructor: 'I located Dr. Duxler. He's a Pierce Junior College physics prof, and never taught at Caltech.' He records the directory page's own scope statement and describes the W-2 as totalling 'under $1000.00'. The heaviest denial in the cluster comes from a proponent, not a debunker. On the node Robert "Bob" Lazar. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1998

- **1998, Second and last DCI disclosure, then nine years of silence.** The DCI discloses $26.7 billion for FY1998 and then stops. No intelligence budget figure is published again until 2007, and then only because Congress compels it by statute. On the node The intelligence budget disclosure record, FY1997-FY2026. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1998, Alien Rapture: The Chosen published by Galde Press.** First edition, Lakeville, Minnesota, Galde Press, 1998, xii and 275 pages, 23 cm, ISBN 1880090503, by Edgar Rothschild Fouche and Brad Steiger. The Library of Congress record gives only the year; no primary record for the month was opened. No copy of the text was opened, so how the TR-3B appears inside it is unestablished. On the node Alien Rapture: The Chosen, Galde Press, 1998. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1998-01-01, The Air Force concedes the site exists.** Forty-three years after establishment, the USAF acknowledges the existence of the operating location at Groom Lake - without describing anything done there. On the node Groom Lake, Nevada. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1998-01-08, Ninth Circuit dismisses the Groom Lake workers' suit.** Kasza v. Browner, 133 F.3d 1159, sustains dismissal on state secrets and statutory exemption grounds. The Supreme Court denies certiorari at 525 U.S. 967. The facility's contents remain legally unspeakable. On the node Presidential Determinations on the Air Force's Operating Location Near Groom Lake. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1998-02-01, The 7th Annual International UFO Congress Convention runs at Laughlin, and Fouche is not on its programme.** A contemporaneous page for the '7th Annual International UFO Congress Convention Film Festival & EBE Awards' gives the dates 'February 1 thru 7, 1998' and the venue 'Gold River Resort, Laughlin, Nevada', and lists 24 speakers by name. Edgar Fouche is not among them. His own transcript is dated August 1998 and his own text says he spoke at Laughlin 'in August', which the Congress's separate summer sessions make consistent rather than contradictory, but no contemporaneous record placing him on any podium in 1998 was opened. On the node Fouche's presentation transcript, August 1998. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **1998-02-04, THE LIBRARY OF CONGRESS CATALOGUES ALIEN RAPTURE AS SCIENCE FICTION.** Library of Congress record LCCN 98002961, recordContentSource DLC, recordCreationDate 980204, carries the gsafd genre element 'Science fiction.', the subject heading 'Alien abduction - Fiction', the classification PS3556.O779 A79 1998, which is American literature, individual authors, 1961 onward, and the Dewey number 813/.54, American fiction. The cataloguing predates both the book's publication and the August presentation. On the node Alien Rapture: The Chosen, Galde Press, 1998. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1998-08, THE ANCHOR: the presentation transcript that originates the TR-3B.** A 62 page slide-cued speaking script headed 'Presentation to IUFOR by / Edgar Rothshild Fouche / Copyright Aug. 1998 / Fouche Media Associates'. Slide 5 names the book it was given to promote. Slides 69 to 72 give the TR-3B its designation and code name Astra, attribute it to 'the Top Secret, Aurora Program with SDI and black budget monies', name the NRO, NSA and CIA as funders and taskers, name Sandia and Livermore as developers of the propulsion, specify a mercury-based plasma 'at a temperature of 150 degrees Kelvin', and give the operational craft two different sizes two slides apart, 600 feet at slide 69 and 300 feet at slide 70. On the node Fouche's presentation transcript, August 1998. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **1998-08-10, Bigelow's first NIDS interview.** Bigelow tells the Deseret News that anomalous aerial activity and animal deaths have continued around the ranch and that 'we wouldn't be there just for the weather,' while declining to discuss specifics. On the node Robert T. Bigelow. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **1998-10-01, The Defense Threat Reduction Agency is established, heir to AFSWP.** DTRA is established in the Department of Defense as a combat support agency by DoD Directive 5105.62 of 30 September 1998, consolidating the Defense Special Weapons Agency, the On-Site Inspection Agency, the Defense Technology Security Administration and OSD arms control elements. Its own history states the line: 'The nation's oldest defense agency thus lives on; MED to AFSWP to DAS A to DNA to DSWA to DTRA.' The Atomic Energy Commission's own records are a separate National Archives group, RG 326, not RG 374. On the node Defense Threat Reduction Agency. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
### 1999

- **1999-07, COMETA publishes 'Les OVNI et la Défense'.** A committee of French retired general officers, weapons engineers, an admiral and a national police chief, almost all IHEDN auditors, concludes that the physical reality of unknown flying objects is 'almost certain', that 'a single hypothesis sufficiently takes into account the facts ... the hypothesis of extraterrestrial visitors', and recommends that France 'initiate diplomatic demarches to the United States ... to urge the superpower to collaborate and, if necessary, exert useful pressure'. On the node Les OVNI et la Défense: à quoi doit-on se préparer? (COMETA report, July 1999). Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1999-10-05, NNSA gets its own SAP statute with the DoD-style waiver.** Sec. 3236 of Pub. L. 106-65, 113 Stat. 962, creates 50 U.S.C. 2426. The waiver mirrors 10 U.S.C. 119(e): case-by-case adverse-national-security determination by the Administrator, after which the information goes jointly to the chairman and ranking minority member of each congressional defense committee. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **1999-10-05, NNSA gets a SAP statute with a broader waiver than DoD's.** Section 3236 of the FY2000 NDAA, Public Law 106-65, creates 50 U.S.C. 2426. Its subsection (e)(1) lets the NNSA Administrator waive not only the reports but the 30-day report-and-wait itself - the most permissive SAP provision in the Code, governing the agency that holds Restricted Data and runs Los Alamos, Sandia and Livermore. On the node 50 U.S.C. 2426, Congressional Oversight of DOE/NNSA Special Access Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.

## 2000-2016: AAWSAP / AATIP

146 events sit in this era.

### 2000

- **2000, Project Condign reports.** The UK Defence Intelligence Staff completes 'Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region' as Scientific & Technical Memorandum 55/2/00. Its key recommendation is that DI55 should no longer monitor UAP reports; its paragraph on 'Related Military Applications' is withheld under the defence exemption; its chapter on other countries denies any US or Canadian collaboration 'of any sort' and then redacts four lines. On the node Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region (DIS Scientific & Technical Memorandum 55/2/00). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2000-03-01, NNSA established by Congress.** The nuclear weapons complex is placed under a semi-autonomous agency inside DOE, consolidating the most capable custodial architecture in the government outside the DoD SAP system. On the node National Nuclear Security Administration. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2000-09, Lt Col John Williams's Disclosure Project testimony on a compartmented facility at Norton.** Printed in the same document as McCandlish's. Williams says he 'was responsible for that base's facilities from 1981-82' and that 'I was never allowed in that area when I was there at Norton Air Force Base'. Greer cross-references the two testimonies to each other in bracketed notes signed 'SG'. On the node Disclosure Project Briefing Document, April 2001. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2000-12, McCandlish's Disclosure Project testimony is taken.** Printed at section 3.8.5 under the heading 'Testimony of Mr. Mark McCandlish, US Air Force / December 2000'. It is where the claimed craft acquires the names Alien Reproduction Vehicle and Flux Liner in print, where the sizes are given as 24 / 60 / 'about 120 to 130 feet', and where the Frank Carlucci identification is published in his own voice and labelled as his own speculation. On the node Mark McCandlish. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
### 2001

- **2001-04, The Disclosure Project Briefing Document is prepared by Greer and Loder.** The roughly 500 page compilation prepared by Steven M. Greer MD and Theodore C. Loder III PhD, printing summarised witness testimony including McCandlish's of December 2000. It is the custody point for dozens of this board's witness claims. On the node Disclosure Project Briefing Document, April 2001. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2001-05-09, Greer's National Press Club event.** Steven Greer stages the Disclosure Project press conference with twenty retired Air Force, FAA and intelligence officers, creating the format every subsequent disclosure event has followed. On the node Steven M. Greer. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2001-05-09, Disclosure Project press conference.** Steven Greer presents about twenty former military and government witnesses at the National Press Club and demands congressional hearings with subpoena power. Global coverage; no hearing. On the node The Disclosure Project. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2001-11-14, CAUS still litigating against DoD.** The Ninth Circuit decides Citizens Against UFO Secrecy, Inc. v. United States Department of Defense - twenty-four years after the organisation was founded. On the node Citizens Against UFO Secrecy (CAUS). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
### 2002

- **2002, UFOs and the National Security State vol. I.** Richard Dolan publishes 'Chronology of a Cover-Up, 1941-1973', moving the field's argument from witness testimony to the documented architecture of American secrecy. On the node UFOs and the National Security State, vols. I-II (Dolan, 2002 and 2009). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2002-01-01, MITRE takes over JASON administration.** The JASON contract passes to MITRE, placing the government's most sensitive standing scientific advisory panel inside the corporation that would be interrogated about UAP records 24 years later. On the node JASON. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2002-10-16, Alleged Wilson meeting.** The date attributed to Eric Davis's meeting with Vice Adm. Thomas R. Wilson recorded in the notes that surfaced in 2019 among Edgar Mitchell's papers, describing a special access programme that refused a former DIA director access. On the node Eric W. Davis. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2002-10-16, The Wilson-Davis meeting.** Eric Davis takes fifteen pages of handwritten notes recording Admiral Thomas Wilson describing being refused access, on need-to-know grounds, to a SAP holding recovered non-human material in contractor custody. On the node The Wilson-Davis Notes (16 October 2002). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2002-10-22, Podesta calls for declassification.** Six days after the Wilson meeting, the former White House Chief of Staff appears at the National Press Club for the Coalition for Freedom of Information and demands the government open its UFO files. On the node John D. Podesta. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2002-12, The sealed Haut affidavit.** Donald Schmitt composes and emails a further affidavit which Haut signs at the International UFO Museum before his daughter and a notary, embargoed until after his death. It asserts first-hand sight of a craft and bodies, contradicting his 1979, 1989 and 1993 statements. On the node Walter Haut. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
### 2003

- **2003, Vandenberg incursions during NRO launches (2003-2005).** Jeffrey Nuccetelli's testimony describes objects 'like flying buildings' over a space launch range during National Reconnaissance Office missions, each incident witnessed by multiple personnel, documented, investigated and reported up the chain. No Air Force series exists in RG 615 to hold those reports. On the node Jeffrey Nuccetelli. Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2003-01-31, Kean's FOIA request.** Leslie Kean files with NASA for records on Kecksburg, the 9 December 1965 incident, the Fragology Files 1962-1967, Richard M. Schulherr, Project Moon Dust and Cosmos 96. NASA answers 'no records' on 11 April 2003. On the node Kean v. NASA, No. 1:03-cv-02509 (D.D.C.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2003-10-14, Vandenberg red square.** Boeing contractors at Vandenberg AFB report a massive glowing red square hovering silently over two missile defense sites; security guards report a rectangular craft larger than a football field the same night. On the node Vandenberg AFB Incidents (2003-2005). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2003-10-14, A red square over the missile defense sites.** Boeing contractors at Vandenberg report a massive glowing red square hovering silently over two missile defense sites; security personnel the same night describe a rectangular craft larger than a football field. A witness a week later is threatened and told to keep quiet. On the node Vandenberg Space Force Base, California. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2003-12-09, Kean v. NASA filed.** Thirty-eight years to the day after Kecksburg, Kean sues NASA in the District of Columbia. The case is assigned to Judge Emmet G. Sullivan. On the node Kean v. NASA, No. 1:03-cv-02509 (D.D.C.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
### 2004

- **2004-01-01, USS Reagan log pages removed.** Multiple USS Ronald Reagan watchstanders observe an orange self-luminous object above the flight deck; a Boatswain's Mate of the Watch is ordered to 'rip it out' of the deck logbook and another witness sees an officer order pages removed. On the node USS Ronald Reagan Encounter and Logbook Tampering (2003-2004). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2004-01-01, NIDS closes; ranch retained.** Bigelow shuts the institute but keeps Skinwalker Ranch. The cleared cadre remains intact and available. On the node National Institute for Discovery Science (NIDS). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2004-02-02, Element 115 synthesis published in Physical Review C.** Oganessian et al., 'Experiments on the synthesis of element 115 in the reaction 243Am(48Ca,xn)291-x115', Physical Review C 69, 021601(R), a Dubna and Lawrence Livermore collaboration. The isotopes are 287 and 288, at cross sections of roughly 1 and 3 picobarn, observed as chains of consecutive alpha decays running to completion within about twenty seconds and about half a second. Nothing about them is stable, machinable or storable. On the node Element 115: the Lazar claim and moscovium. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2004-03-03, GAO reports it cannot vouch for DoD contractor security oversight.** GAO-04-332, 'Industrial Security: DOD Cannot Provide Adequate Assurances That Its Oversight Ensures the Protection of Classified Information', prepared in response to a Senate report accompanying the FY2004 NDAA. DSS monitored 11,000-plus contractor facilities; GAO issued seven recommendations. Facilities under an approved carve-out sit outside the population DSS inspected and therefore outside GAO's audit universe. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2004-03-03, GAO audits contractor security and is steered around special access programs.** GAO-04-332 finds that DoD 'cannot provide adequate assurances that its oversight ensures the protection of classified information' at more than 11,000 cleared contractor facilities. Footnote 2 records that, by agreement with committee staff, the review 'did not include DSS's oversight of special access programs at contractor facilities.' On the node GAO-04-332, Industrial Security: DOD Cannot Provide Adequate Assurances That Its Oversight Ensures the Protection of Classified Information. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2004-11-10, Princeton tracks descents from 80,000 ft.** USS Princeton's upgraded SPY-1B tracks objects descending from above 80,000 feet to 20,000 feet over roughly two weeks before the intercept. On the node USS Princeton (CG-59). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2004-11-14, Nimitz Tic Tac intercept.** VFA-41 CO David Fravor and Lt. Cmdr. Alex Dietrich intercept a white 46-foot object with no control surfaces about 30 miles off Baja California; a follow-on aircraft records the ATFLIR footage later released as FLIR1. On the node USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2004-11-14, The Nimitz intercept.** Princeton vectors two VFA-41 F/A-18Fs to a contact it has held on Aegis for two weeks. Fravor, Dietrich and their WSOs watch a 46-foot white object for about five minutes; it departs and reappears at the CAP point 60 miles away in under a minute. A follow-on sortie records FLIR1. On the node USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2004-11-15, Princeton data collection alleged.** Princeton crewmen Kevin Day and Gary Voorhis say unidentified personnel came aboard and removed or wiped recording media. No visit log, message or chain-of-custody document has been located. On the node USS Princeton (CG-59). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2004-11-16, Reporting terminates at Third Fleet.** The Navy range fouler report on the incident records: 'Pilots reported the incident through Intelligence Personnel. Reporting terminated at 3rd Fleet, there was no process to share the reporting.' On the node Navy Range Fouler Reports (72 reports, 2004-2021). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2004-11-16, The Princeton data goes missing.** Three Princeton crewmen in different divisions - Day, Voorhis and Turner - describe unidentified personnel coming aboard within roughly 48 hours and removing or ordering wiped the recording media. No FOIA release, visit log or message traffic has ever identified them. On the node USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier C.
### 2005

- **2005, Military Intelligence Program created.** The Joint Military Intelligence Program and Tactical Intelligence and Related Activities aggregations are consolidated into the Military Intelligence Program under the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence, formalising the split between DNI-controlled and SECDEF-controlled intelligence money and creating the oversight seam between the intelligence and armed services committees. On the node Military Intelligence Program (MIP). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2005, Majic Eyes Only.** Ryan S. Wood publishes the retrieval literature's first indexed catalogue, with dates, locations, source trails and credibility weightings. On the node Majic Eyes Only: Earth's Encounters with Extraterrestrial Technology (2005). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2005-01-01, 'Hunt for the Skinwalker' published.** Kelleher and Knapp publish the account of the NIDS ranch work; James Lacatski of DIA reads it. On the node National Institute for Discovery Science (NIDS). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2005-12, NASA's satellite statement.** Before the fortieth anniversary NASA states that experts examined metallic fragments from Kecksburg and determined they came from a Soviet satellite, but that the records of those findings were lost in the 1980s. NASA's own later position is that Cosmos 96 could not have been responsible. On the node Kecksburg incident, 9 December 1965. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
### 2006

- **2006, FAS wins FOIA suit against the NRO.** Aftergood, for FAS, secures release of unclassified National Reconnaissance Office budget records - the only occasion on which any component-level intelligence budget material has been prised loose by litigation. On the node Federation of American Scientists. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2006-01-01, Northrop-led consortium takes over the Nevada complex.** National Security Technologies LLC succeeds Bechtel Nevada as Nevada Test Site management and operating contractor, inheriting the classified support infrastructure adjacent to Groom Lake. On the node Northrop Grumman Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2006-05-15, Condign released under FOI.** The Ministry of Defence releases the Condign report following a September 2005 Freedom of Information Act request by David Clarke and Gary Anthony. The publication scheme entry names the Directorate of Air Staff as author organisation. On the node Dr David Clarke. Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 2007

- **2007-01-01, FLIR1 leaks without authorization.** DoD's 2020 release statement acknowledges the videos circulated publicly following unauthorized disclosures in 2007 and 2017. On the node FLIR1 (Nimitz ATFLIR video, 14 Nov 2004). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-01-01, DIA contacts Bigelow about Skinwalker.** A senior Defense Intelligence Agency official writes to Bigelow about the ranch; Bigelow connects him to Senator Harry Reid. The AAWSAP contract follows in 2008 - the private capability is re-federalised without a new office being created. On the node National Institute for Discovery Science (NIDS). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-01-01, $22M directed into the black budget.** Reid, Inouye and Stevens direct roughly $22 million into the classified defense appropriation for DIA aerospace threat work, bypassing the authorizing committees. On the node Defense Appropriations Acts FY2008 and FY2010, the $22M AAWSAP appropriation. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-01-01, Reid initiates AATIP.** Senator Harry Reid, prompted by Robert Bigelow, sets in motion the program that becomes AATIP. Funding of roughly $22 million flows from late 2008 through 2011, most of it to Bigelow Aerospace. On the node Advanced Aerospace Threat Identification Program (AATIP / AAITP). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-03-27, Court finds NASA's searches inadequate.** Judge Sullivan holds that NASA 'has not yet met its burden of demonstrating that the search for documents was adequate', denies summary judgment in part, and orders the parties to propose search and documentation procedures creating a proper record. NASA had already admitted its first two searches were inadequate. On the node Kean v. NASA, No. 1:03-cv-02509 (D.D.C.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-06-19, Lacatski writes to Bigelow.** Per 'Skinwalkers at the Pentagon', Lacatski writes to Robert Bigelow on DIA letterhead, the act that originates the contract. DIA later reports no records of the letter. On the node James T. Lacatski. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2007-07-20, CPSC: United Nuclear, the company, fined $7,500 after pleading guilty to three counts.** CORRECTS THE STANDARD FRAMING. It is widely repeated that 'in 2006, Lazar pleaded guilty' to a Federal Hazardous Substances Act violation. The CPSC's own release of 20 July 2007 says the COMPANY pled guilty, to three criminal counts, before U.S. Chief Magistrate Lorenzo F. Garcia: 'United Nuclear Scientific Supplies LLC, of Edgewood, N.M., founded and operated by Robert Lazar, was fined $7,500 and received three years probation.' Robert Lazar and accountant Joy White appear in the separate civil consent decree. On the node Robert "Bob" Lazar. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-08-03, Section 601 compels annual NIP disclosure.** Section 601 of the Implementing Recommendations of the 9/11 Commission Act, codified at 50 U.S.C. 3306, requires the DNI to disclose the aggregate NIP appropriation within 30 days of each fiscal year's end, subject to a presidential waiver. The FY2007 figure is $43.5 billion. This remains, with the later request-side requirement, the entire statutory transparency regime for the intelligence budget. On the node 50 U.S.C. 3306 - Public disclosure of NIP aggregate amounts. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-10-16, Stipulated settlement in part.** Judge Sullivan signs the Stipulation of Settlement in Part and orders joint status reports every sixty days. NASA searches under court supervision for the next twenty-two months. On the node Kean v. NASA, No. 1:03-cv-02509 (D.D.C.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2007-11-01, 'Probable ET Generated' proposal to Puthoff.** A proposal to analyse 'Probable ET Generated Specific Near-field Electromagnetic Emissions' is addressed to Puthoff and Hathaway - quoted verbatim in the preface of Kit Green's later DIRD, nine months before the AAWSAP contract. On the node DIRD: 'Clinical Medical Acute & Subacute Field Effects on Human Dermal & Neurological Tissues'. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 2008

- **2008-01-01, DIA money flows to a private contractor.** Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies wins the DIA AAWSAP contract on a special appropriation obtained through Sen. Harry Reid. Government-funded UAP research moves onto private premises. On the node Bigelow Aerospace. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2008-03-10, A public AFIT thesis defines 'fastwalker'.** AFIT/GA/ENY/08-M06, distribution unlimited, defines the term in its own words as 'any orbital object seen passing through the field of view of an Earth observing sensor which is suspected of being in orbit'. The words UFO and unidentified appear nowhere in its 112 pages. It names a fastwalker observation database with data from as far back as 1972, and a 1989 predecessor thesis by R. Osedacz. On the node AFIT/GA/ENY/08-M06 - Space Based Satellite Tracking and Characterization Utilizing Non-Imaging Passive Sensors. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2008-05, The National Archives UFO release programme begins.** The first of ten tranches of MoD UFO files is published at nationalarchives.gov.uk/ufos with a highlights guide by David Clarke. The programme runs to June 2013 and covers DEFE 24 and DEFE 31. On the node The National Archives / MoD UFO File Release Programme (2008-2013). Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-07, FY2008 Defense Supplemental Appropriation Act carries Reid/Inouye $10M add directing DIA to assess far-term foreign advanced aerospace threats out to 40 years.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-08-18, DIA/Virginia Contracting Activity posts solicitation HHM402-08-R-0211, 'Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program', Total Small Business set-aside, NAICS 541330.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-08-18, DIA posts solicitation HHM402-08-R-0211.** The DIA Virginia Contracting Activity publicly solicits advanced aerospace weapon system studies across eleven areas out to 2050, naming Dr. James T. Lacatski as Government Project Leader. On the node DIA Solicitation HHM402-08-R-0211. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2008-08-18, DIA buys the physics.** The Defense Intelligence Agency issues solicitation HHM402-08-R-0211 for the Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program. The resulting contract produces thirty-eight Defense Intelligence Reference Documents on metric engineering, wormholes, antigravity and field effects on human tissue - the tier-1 evidence that a US agency paid to study the physics a recovered craft would require. On the node Exotic Technology & Physics. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-09-01, AAWSAP awarded.** The DIA awards the Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications contract to Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies; Colm Kelleher serves as deputy administrator. On the node AAWSAP Defense Intelligence Reference Document series. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2008-09-05, Solicitation response deadline; DIA receives only one bid, from BAASS.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-09-22, Contract HHM402-08-C-0072 signed with Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies LLC.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-09-22, AAWSAP awarded to BAASS.** DIA awards firm-fixed-price contract HHM402-08-C-0072 to Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies LLC, $10,000,000 base with four option years. On the node DIA Contract HHM402-08-C-0072 (BAASS). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2008-11-13, DIA Director meets Senator Reid on the program.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-11-21, BAASS submits 12 technical-area program management plans; DIA rates them 'very good to excellent'.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-12-16, DIA 'Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Contract Status' briefing.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2008-12-16, First DIA contract status briefing.** DIA prepares its first Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications contract status briefing three months after award. On the node Defense Intelligence Agency. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2008-12-17, Defense Security Service conducts facility clearance site inspection at BAASS.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 2009

- **2009-01-01, Stratton reopens the Nimitz case.** Fravor testifies that Jay Stratton contacted him in 2009 to investigate the 2004 sighting as part of a DoD program - five years after the Navy declined to investigate it. On the node USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-01-01, The alleged Lockheed material negotiation.** Per George Knapp's later sworn testimony, Robert Bigelow and an AAWSAP colleague negotiate with senior Lockheed Martin executives for a quantity of material held at a California facility. AARO separately records an allegation that an organisation possessed off-world material in 2009 and 2010. On the node Lockheed Martin Corporation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-01-01, DIRD series produced.** The FY2009 Defense Intelligence Reference Documents are produced under the DIA Defense Warning Office with James T. Lacatski as programme manager - propulsion physics, exotic materials and human injury pathology commissioned as one set. On the node AAWSAP Defense Intelligence Reference Document series. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-01-01, BAASS funds MUFON's Star Team Impact Project.** A DIA-contracted firm funds rapid-response investigation by the largest civilian UFO network in the United States, routing case data back to BAASS. The arrangement collapses in 2010. On the node BAASS–MUFON Star Team Impact Project (SIP). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2009-01-05, DIA talking paper for Reid visit.** DIA prepares a talking paper for a Senator Reid visit on AAWSAP contract status, the first of three surviving Reid briefing papers from 2009. On the node Harry M. Reid. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2009-05-08, DIA status briefing: 'On Track, On Schedule, and Within Cost'; SCI clearance processing completed for all key BAASS personnel.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-05-08, May 2009 Reid briefing.** DIA briefs Reid on contract status; the meeting generates the request that produces the October deliverables quality review. On the node Harry M. Reid. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2009-06-24, Sen. Reid writes DEPSECDEF Lynn requesting restricted Special Access Protection for the AAITP.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-06-24, Reid requests a Restricted SAP.** Reid asks Deputy Secretary of Defense William Lynn III for a Restricted Special Access Program with a bigoted access list over methodologies for engineering advanced technologies and contractor involvement. On the node Reid Letter to DEPSECDEF Requesting Restricted SAP Status. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-06-30, BAASS delivers 26 detailed research reports, twice the contracted minimum.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-07-01, BAASS delivers the 494-page Ten Month Report.** The contractor delivers a report integrating exotic physics research with field investigation, a case database, ranch operations and human physiological effects. DIA has never released it. On the node BAASS Ten Month Report (2009). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2009-07-24, Reid seeks SAP status.** Reid writes to Deputy Secretary of Defense William Lynn III requesting Special Access Program status for AATIP. The request is denied. Elizondo's and Puthoff's names in the memo are originally redacted. On the node Advanced Aerospace Threat Identification Program (AATIP / AAITP). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-08-24, Kean v. NASA dismissed.** On the parties' joint motion and NASA's representation that it had met its settlement obligations, the case is dismissed. The only federal finding of an inadequate UFO-related records search stands undisturbed. On the node Kean v. NASA, No. 1:03-cv-02509 (D.D.C.). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-09-25, Brazil declassifies the 1986 report.** The Air Defence Command report of 2 June 1986 is released to the Arquivo Nacional. The Brazilian Air Force begins transferring its whole UAP holding to the National Archives; by 2022 it is the second most consulted collection there, behind only the records of the military dictatorship. On the node Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection. Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2009-10-30, DIA Info Memo U-429-09 reviews deliverables; all reviews positive; five reviews by outside reviewers incl. three Sandia National Laboratories staff.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-10-30, DIA finds the deliverables excellent.** DIA's Defense Warning Office records that of thirteen reviews of the 26 FY09 reports, including three by Sandia National Laboratories staff, 'all of the reviews were positive, some exceptionally so'. On the node DIA Memorandum: Review of Advanced Aerospace Contract Deliverables (U-429-09-DWO-IM). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-11-01, The conversion is considered.** An Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence information memorandum records review of a special access programme request relating to AATIP. This is the documented moment at which a FOIA-visible programme was considered for conversion into an invisible one. On the node Special Access Programs. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-11-13, DIA Info Memo U-09-2660: 'DIA cannot find adequate justification to establish a restricted SAP'.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-11-13, DIA: insufficient grounds to classify.** DIA finds the program cannot be classified, the material is basic scientific research not owned or controlled by the government and no report is sufficiently sensitive to require extra protection. On the node DIA Memorandum: Review of Special Access Program Request (U-09-2660CE-IM). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-11-17, USD(I) recommends against SAP status.** James Clapper's recommendation closes out Senator Reid's June 2009 request for restricted special access program status for the DIA advanced aerospace effort. The one documented SAP decision in the modern record goes against concealment. On the node Waived unacknowledged special access program (waived USAP). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-11-17, Clapper recommends against a SAP.** USD(I) James R. Clapper Jr. recommends against establishing a Special Access Program in the packet presented to Deputy Secretary Lynn. Nine months later he becomes Director of National Intelligence. On the node Final Packet Presented to the Deputy Secretary of Defense on the SAP Request. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-11-19, DEPSECDEF Lynn and DIA meet Sen. Reid at Senate Visitor Center SVC-217, 1645; DIA position is that deliverables do not meet DoD 5200.1-R criteria for a SAP.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-11-19, Lynn refuses Reid in person.** At 4:45pm in Senate Visitor Center room SVC-217, the Deputy Secretary of Defense tells the Majority Leader the AAWSAP deliverables do not meet DoD 5200.1-R criteria for SAP protection. On the node William J. Lynn III. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-12-01, The MoD closes the UFO desk.** Britain terminates its public UAP reporting channel with effect from December 2009, in the same period in which it was handling the largest volume of sighting reports in thirty years. The National Archives records that the closure 'sparked' a disclosure campaign. On the node Closure of the MoD UFO Desk, 1 December 2009. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-12-14, First DIRD publication in the released set: DIA-08-0911-016, 'Aerospace Applications of Programmable Matter'.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-12-29, E.O. 13526 fixes who may create a SAP and caps ISOO's access.** Sec. 4.3(a) limits SAP creation to the Secretaries of State, Defense, Energy and Homeland Security, the Attorney General and the DNI, or the principal deputy of each. Sec. 4.3(b)(3) permits an agency head to limit access to a SAP to the Director of ISOO 'and no more than one other employee' or, 'for special access programs that are extraordinarily sensitive and vulnerable, to the Director only.' Sec. 4.3(c): 'Nothing in this order shall supersede any requirement made by or under 10 U.S.C. 119.' No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2009-12-29, Executive Order 13526 fixes the six officials who may create a SAP.** Section 4.3 limits SAP creation to the Secretaries of State, Defense, Energy and Homeland Security, the Attorney General and the DNI, or their principal deputies, requires the number to be kept at an absolute minimum, and affords the ISOO Director access 'consistent with the policies and procedures directed by the President'. On the node Executive Order 13526, Classified National Security Information. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2009-2012, Reid SAP request (2009, denied by DIA) and later KONA BLUE PSAP to DHS (post-2012, rejected by DHS leadership), two formal attempts to move the effort into special-access channels.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 2010

- **2010, MIP topline published for the first time.** Military Intelligence Program figures are disclosed alongside the NIP for FY2010: $27.0 billion MIP against $53.1 billion NIP, a combined $80.1 billion. No breakdown of either accompanies the figures, then or since. On the node The intelligence budget disclosure record, FY1997-FY2026. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2010, Brazilian Army inquiry on Varginha.** The inquiry concludes the 1996 witnesses encountered Luiz Antonio de Paula, a mentally disabled man, and that the observed military vehicle movements were routine maintenance and Escola de Sargentos das Armas transport. The underlying unit movement logs are not published. On the node Varginha incident, Minas Gerais, January 1996. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2010-01-01, The transfer is blocked.** The divestment never occurs. Elizondo testifies that a memorandum from the Secretary of the Air Force required to complete it never issued; the reported cause is a CIA veto, which AARO records as a former senior CIA official quashing the proposal to remove the material from the corporation. On the node Central Intelligence Agency. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2010-01-01, Elizondo assumes AATIP role.** On TTSA's account Elizondo takes over AATIP as Director in 2010, running it from OUSD(I). Neill Tipton, his superior in this period, later states he brought no AATIP responsibilities with him. On the node Luis D. Elizondo. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2010-04-06, DIA-08-1004-004 issued.** 'Traversable Wormholes, Stargates, and Negative Energy' is issued with Eric W. Davis of EarthTech International, 11855 Research Blvd, Austin TX, named as author. On the node DIA-08-1004-004: 'Traversable Wormholes, Stargates, and Negative Energy'. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
