- **2010-07-01, DoDD 5205.07 puts acknowledged / unacknowledged / waived into DoD policy.** The directive cancels DoDD 5205.07 of 5 January 2006, establishes the SAPOC and DoD SAPCO, defines the three-state taxonomy and the carve-out, restricts waived-SAP access to committee chairs, ranking minority members and staff directors, and provides at para 4.h that 'All waived SAP material submitted to Congress, to include annual reports and other products, shall remain in DoD custody.' Change 2 issued 4 February 2020. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2010-07-01, DoD Directive 5205.07 defines acknowledged, unacknowledged and waived.** The directive establishes the SAPOC and DoD SAPCO and puts the three-state taxonomy into DoD policy in terms, together with the rule that committee members get access to DoD SAPs 'except for waived programs' and that waived material submitted to Congress 'shall remain in DoD custody'. On the node DoDD 5205.07, Special Access Program (SAP) Policy (1 Jul 2010, Chg 2 of 4 Feb 2020). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2010-09-02, Contract modification P00001 to HHM402-08-C-0072 (FY10 option, $12M add in FY2010 Defense Appropriations Act).**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2010-09-27, UFO-Nukes Connection press conference.** Robert Hastings convenes seven named USAF veterans at the National Press Club. CNN streams it live. Signed affidavits from Salas, Halt, McDonough, Jamison, Arneson, Fenstermacher and Nelson are on file and published in facsimile. Anonymous testimony becomes on-the-record testimony. On the node UFO-Nukes Connection press conference, National Press Club, 27 September 2010. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2010-12-01, DIA proposes moving AATIP out.** A DIA info memo to USD(AT&L) says the reports were of limited value to DIA but could merit other organisations, and that the project could be transitioned to a better-suited component. On the node DIA Info Memo to USD(AT&L) on AATIP (U-10-2552CE-IM-ATL). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
### 2011

- **2011-01-01, DHS Under Secretary for S&T establishes KONA BLUE as a prospective SAP.** On the government's own account, a DHS component head creates a prospective special access program that supporters intended to use to reverse-engineer recovered craft and exploit non-human biologics. Senators Lieberman and Reid ask that it be established. On the node DHS Science and Technology Directorate. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2011-01-01, Alleged 2011 restriction on historical briefings.** UAP Gerb alleges a legacy program briefing restriction initiated in 2011 to cease historical briefings on program incumbents - i.e. a policy act specifically designed to prevent newly cleared personnel from learning the programme's own history. If real, it is documentable as a memorandum or classification guide change. On the node UAP Gerb - The MITRE Corporation investigation. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2011-01-01, KONA BLUE proposed to DHS.** AAWSAP's advocates propose a prospective special access program at DHS to restart the work and reverse-engineer any recovered craft they hoped to acquire. DHS rejects it. AARO notes it was never reported to Congress because it never met the SAP threshold. On the node KONA BLUE. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2011-01-01, DIA withholds the BAASS contract.** DIA withholds the AAWSAP contract in full in response to a FOIA request; it will not release it for another eleven years. On the node DIA Contract HHM402-08-C-0072 (BAASS). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2011-01-01, KONA BLUE proposed at DHS.** A successor special access program to house UAP recovery and reverse-engineering work is proposed to the Department of Homeland Security and subsequently rejected by DHS leadership. On the node KONA BLUE. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2011-01-11, Last DIRD publication in the released set: DIA-08-1102-007, 'Quantum Tomography of Negative Energy States in the Vacuum'.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2011-02, First publication of the NIP budget request.** Under section 364 of the Intelligence Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2010, the aggregate NIP budget request is published for the first time, in February 2011. Two numbers a year is now the settled floor and ceiling of public knowledge. On the node 50 U.S.C. 3306 - Public disclosure of NIP aggregate amounts. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2011-07-01, DHS Deputy Secretary terminates KONA BLUE.** Approximately six months after it is established, the Deputy Secretary disapproves the prospective program. Neither the establishing nor the terminating official is named in the released paper. On the node United States Department of Homeland Security. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2011-07-26, The most-reproduced black triangle photograph is confessed as a hoax on Belgian television.** Robert Sheaffer records that on 26 July 2011 the Belgian broadcaster RTL carried the photographer's confession about the Petit-Rechain image of the 1989-90 Belgian wave: 'The UFO of Petit-Rechain is not a spaceship from a distant galaxy but a panel of painted styrofoam with three spots affixed', and, from the maker, 'One has managed to fool the whole world with a silly model made of styrofoam.' Fouche's 1998 presentation had attached his craft to 'the country of Belgium' at slide 6. This does not touch the eyewitness reports and it decides nothing about Fouche; it records that the visual corpus the claim borrows plausibility from has a confessed hoax at its centre. On the node TR-3B 'Astra', claimed black triangular aircraft. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2011-12-30, CAPCO Register v5.1 gives SAPs their own marking category.** Volume 5 Edition 1 of the IC Authorized Classification and Control Markings Register moves the Special Access Required marking into a dedicated Special Access Program Markings category with the indicator 'SAR-'. Its title page records that certain markings were removed for classification and 'compiled in separate classified addenda'. On the node CAP Registry / CAPCO Register of control systems, compartments and sub-compartments. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.

Sapp is sworn in as Director of the National Reconnaissance Office.** The Congressional Research Service's intelligence-community leadership roster records 'Director of the National Reconnaissance Office (D/NRO) Betty J. Sapp (sworn in July 6, 2012)', with the appointment authority at 50 U.S.C. 3041(b)(2)(B) and 50 U.S.C. 3602(a)(2), and gives Christopher Scolese as nominated on 7 February 2019 in the same row. IMDb is user-maintained and the film itself was not opened. The UAP Gerb episode description calls it the '2014 Zero Point Documentary'. On the node Zero Point: The Story of Mark McCandlish and the Flux Liner. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2013-06-25, The CIA names Area 51.** Answering a 2005 FOIA request by Jeffrey Richelson of the National Security Archive, the CIA releases the U-2 and OXCART history and acknowledges specific activity at the site - fifty-eight years after establishment. On the node Area 51 (Homey Airport / Groom Lake operating location). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2013-08-29, The only public view inside the black budget.** The Washington Post publishes excerpts from the 'National Intelligence Program Summary for FY2013', classified TS/SI/TK/NOFORN, from the Snowden material. For the same year the officially disclosed figure was a single aggregate: $52.7 billion requested, $49.0 billion after sequestration. Thirteen years later this remains the only occasion on which the interior of the intelligence budget has been seen outside the cleared community. On the node National Intelligence Program Summary for FY2013 (excerpts) - the 2013 black budget disclosure. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.

Richelson, on the CIA history 'The Central Intelligence Agency and Overhead Reconnaissance: The U-2 and OXCART Programs, 1954-1974' by Gregory Pedlow and Donald E. Welzenbach, declassified that summer. This is the documented half of the hub's geography and it is a statement about Groom Lake, NOT about Papoose Lake. On the node S-4 / Papoose Lake, Nevada. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.

- **2014-11, Chilean Navy helicopter FLIR case.** A Chilean Navy helicopter films an object in daylight on infrared during a coastal patrol. Two radar stations cannot see it although they can see the helicopters; air traffic control confirms no authorised aircraft in the area. CEFAA analyses the case for two years. On the node Chilean Navy helicopter FLIR case, November 2014 (released January 2017). Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
### 2015

- **2015-01-01, Fleet Forces safety message recalled.** A US Fleet Forces Command 'URGENT SAFETY OF FLIGHT ISSUE' email carrying GO FAST and citing 'multiple near-midair collisions' is sent to all subordinate commanders and deleted from every recipient's account the next day. On the node Fleet Forces Command 'URGENT SAFETY OF FLIGHT ISSUE' Email (Jan 2015, recalled). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2015-01-01, GIMBAL and GO FAST recorded.** Two ATFLIR recordings are made in the Atlantic operating areas; both are formally released by DoD five years later as authentic and unidentified. On the node GIMBAL (Navy ATFLIR video, January 2015). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2015-01-01, W-72 near-midair.** Two F/A-18Fs 100 feet apart take evasive action around a dark gray cube inside a clear sphere holding station against the wind at the entry point of Warning Area W-72. The squadron files a safety report and receives no acknowledgement. On the node Warning Area W-72, off Virginia Beach. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2015-03-06, Invention original date recorded for the Inertial Mass Reduction Device.** The NAVAIR invention-disclosure briefing for Navy Case PAX 205 records the invention original date as 06/03/2015 and states that the concept is theoretical with no prototype in existence. On the node NAVAIR Invention Disclosure Briefing, Navy Case PAX 205 (Inertial Mass Reduction Device). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2015-06-18, DoDM 5205.07 Volume 1 states exactly what a carve-out removes.** Enclosure 2 para 2: unless a carve-out is approved by the Secretary or Deputy Secretary of Defense, DSS/DCSA performs SAP security inspections at cleared contractor locations, authorizes contractor SAP information systems, and coordinates CI threat and incident cross-sharing. A carve-out removes all three. Volume 1 also incorporates and cancels the DoD Overprint to the NISPOM Supplement, and confines fraud/waste/abuse reporting to SAP channels. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2015-06-18, DoD SAP Security Manual Volume 1 issued.** DoDM 5205.07-V1 states what a carve-out removes - contractor SAP security inspections, information system authorisation and counterintelligence cross-sharing - and defines carve-out in its glossary as a provision approved by the Secretary or Deputy Secretary that relieves DSS of its NISP obligation. On the node DoDM 5205.07 Volume 1, DoD SAP Security Manual: General Procedures. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2015-09, DoD FMR Volume 3 Chapter 6 reissued.** The Department's reprogramming regulation sets the four action types and the congressional approval thresholds - $10 million for military personnel, $15 million for operation and maintenance, $20 million or 20 percent for procurement, $10 million or 20 percent for RDT&E - and preserves Below-Threshold Reprogramming, which requires no approval and no notification at all. On the node Reprogramming of DoD Appropriated Funds (DoD FMR Volume 3, Chapter 6; DD Form 1415 series). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2015-10-17, ICD 906 signed, governing IC Controlled Access Programs.** The current ICD 906 states at B.2: 'This ICD supersedes ICD 906, Controlled Access Programs, signed 17 October 2015.' The 2015 text is preserved in an Internet Archive capture of dni.gov. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2015-10-17, ICD 906 signed, governing Controlled Access Programs.** The DNI's directive establishes the IC's counterpart to the DoD SAP regime, defines CAPs as control systems with compartments and sub-compartments, permits unacknowledged CAPs on DNI or PDDNI approval, and creates the CAP Oversight Committee and Senior Review Group. This version was later superseded by a reissue released in December 2024. On the node ICD 906, Controlled Access Programs (ODNI). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2015-10-22, NRO declassifies almost the entire DORIAN collection.** The NRO's Center for the Study of National Reconnaissance held a panel at the National Museum of the United States Air Force on the occasion of the NRO declassifying almost its entire collection of records on the Dorian program: over 850 documents with some 22,000 pages and 280 photographs. Line-by-line review had taken over a year, and the collection was posted on the NRO public web page the day after the panel. On the node Manned Orbiting Laboratory and the DORIAN / KH-10 reconnaissance payload. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2015-12-30, IUPAC assigns discovery priority for element 115.** Priority went to 'the Dubna-Livermore-Oak Ridge collaborations', on the basis of work 'starting in 2010, and subsequently confirmed in 2012 and 2013'. A precision point usually got wrong in both directions: the priority statement rests on the 2010 to 2013 work, not on the 2003 experiment. The Joint Working Party report is Karol, Barber, Sherrill, Vardaci and Yamazaki, Pure and Applied Chemistry 88 (2016) 155. On the node Element 115: the Lazar claim and moscovium. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.

- **2016-01-01, 'Exemplary' performance evaluation.** Elizondo's annual evaluation rates him exemplary; his supervisor writes that it 'cannot be overstated the importance of Mr. Elizondo's portfolio to national security' and credits him with neutralising six insider threats. General McCasland was in charge of that exact laboratory up to a couple years ago.' On the node Peter W. Merlin. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.

On the node US Patent 10,144,532 - Craft using an inertial mass reduction device. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2016-05-02, Pais craft application referred for third-level security review.** USPTO events record the inertial mass reduction craft application referred to Level 2 (LARS) and then by L&R for third-level security review, the screening that decides whether a filing receives a secrecy order. It cleared. On the node US Patent 10,144,532 - Craft using an inertial mass reduction device. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2016-07-26, DODIG-2016-113: $6.5 trillion in unsupported year-end adjustments.** The DoD Inspector General finds that the Army Comptroller's office and DFAS Indianapolis 'did not adequately support $2.8 trillion in third quarter journal voucher (JV) adjustments and $6.5 trillion in yearend JV adjustments made to AGF data during FY 2015 financial statement compilation'. Footnote 2 states the amounts are not cumulative. The report will become the load-bearing citation of the entire missing-money literature, usually misquoted. On the node DODIG-2016-113 - Army General Fund Adjustments Not Adequately Documented or Supported. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2016-08, DoD asks FASAB for a classified-reporting standard.** Two months after the Army adjustments report, the Department of Defense identifies to the Federal Accounting Standards Advisory Board the need for a standard reconciling generally accepted accounting principles with classification obligations under Executive Order 13526. That request becomes SFFAS 56. On the node SFFAS 56 - Classified Activities (FASAB, 4 October 2018). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2016-11-28, Element 115 formally named moscovium.** The name moscovium, symbol Mc, was formally approved on 28 November 2016 and announced on 30 November 2016, proposed by the discoverers at Dubna, Oak Ridge, Vanderbilt and Lawrence Livermore, in recognition of the Moscow region. This event is regularly presented as vindicating the Lazar claim. It does not: his claim requires a STABLE isotope that can be machined and stored, and no such isotope exists. On the node Element 115: the Lazar claim and moscovium. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.

## 2017-2026: Post-disclosure

551 events sit in this era.

### 2017

- **2017-01, Chile releases the Navy footage.** CEFAA publishes the Chilean Navy infrared video without an explanation - the first release of state military sensor footage of an unresolved object by a Latin American government, three years before the US Department of Defense authorised release of the three Navy videos. On the node Chilean Navy helicopter FLIR case, November 2014 (released January 2017). Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2017-05-11, Edgar Albert Fouche dies, aged 69; buried Coffey Cemetery, Mason County, Texas.** Find a Grave memorial 180498305, created 18 June 2017 and citing an obituary from Mason Funeral Home, gives the death date and age and the burial at Coffey Cemetery, Streeter, Mason County, Texas, with the inscription 'Saved by the blood of Jesus Christ'. A death date of 6 May 2018 at age 82 also circulates and is wrong on both counts; no opened record supports it. On the node Edgar Fouche. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2017-05-28, Bigelow on 60 Minutes.** Robert Bigelow tells CBS he is 'absolutely convinced' of an extraterrestrial presence on Earth, seven months before the program he was paid to run becomes public. On the node Robert T. Bigelow. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2017-07-19, FBI searches United Nuclear Scientific in a Michigan homicide investigation.** A two-page Laingsburg, Michigan police report records an 'Agency Assist' supporting an FBI search of United Nuclear Scientific in aid of 'a homicide investigation out of Houghton, MI involving poison', and records that Lazar consented to the search. The underlying case is the thallium death of Janel Sturzl, 31, on 22 December 2015. Lazar is not recorded as a suspect. The report was obtained by Tim McMillan under a local open-records request. On the node Robert "Bob" Lazar. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2017-08-09, DD Form 1910 submitted.** Elizondo, as Director of the National Programs Special Management Staff, submits a clearance request for three .mpg files, GoFast, Gimbal and FLIR, for research and analysis only, expressly 'not for publication'. On the node DD Form 1910 for GoFast, Gimbal and FLIR video files. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2017-08-24, DD Form 1910 approved.** The clearance request is approved. Forty-one days later Elizondo resigns; six weeks after that the files appear in the New York Times. On the node DD Form 1910 for GoFast, Gimbal and FLIR video files. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2017-09, Skidmore and Fitts publish the unsupported-adjustment compilation.** Mark Skidmore of Michigan State University and Catherine Austin Fitts publish a summary report compiling unsupported journal voucher adjustments reported in public OIG and GAO documents for FY1998-FY2015: roughly $21 trillion at DoD and $350 billion at HUD. The authors state that the public reports 'only provide summary information; thus it is impossible for us to conduct a detailed assessment of the nature of these unsupported adjustments and errors'. On the node Summary Report on 'Unsupported Journal Voucher Adjustments' (Skidmore and Fitts, 2017). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2017-09-01, Elizondo drafts AATIP transition memo for Tipton.** Emails later released under FOIA show Elizondo drafting a memorandum to help Neill Tipton 'assume the new responsibilities for AATIP', five years after the Department says the program ended. On the node Neill Tipton. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2017-10-03, Resignation letter #1.** 'Effective 4 October 2017, I humbly submit my resignation as Director, National Programs Special Management Staff... I respectfully request I receive a deferred retirement.' Hand-delivered. On the node Elizondo Resignation Letter #1, 3 October 2017. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2017-10-04, Resignation memorandum to the Secretary.** A memorandum saluted 'Mr. Secretary' protesting that 'certain individuals in the Department remain staunchly opposed to further research on what could be tactical threats,' and asking the Secretary to 'ask the hard questions'. On the node Elizondo Resignation Memorandum for Record, 4 October 2017. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2017-10-11, To The Stars Academy formed.** Puthoff co-founds TTSA with Tom DeLonge, Jim Semivan and Steve Justice, creating a public-company vehicle for materials acquisition and analysis. On the node ADAM Research Project (Acquisition and Data Analysis of Materials). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2017-10-11, To The Stars Academy launches.** DeLonge, Puthoff, Semivan, Justice, Elizondo and Mellon appear on one masthead, with a Regulation A+ offering targeting $50 million and divisions for Science, Aerospace and Entertainment. - **2017-12, First Kotlikoff-Skidmore Forbes column.** 'Has Our Government Spent $21 Trillion of Our Money without Telling Us?' puts the aggregate in front of a financial-markets readership. Two further columns follow in July 2018 and January 2019, the second asking directly whether the concealment is intentional. On the node Laurence J. Kotlikoff. Strand: oversight. - **2017-12-14, FASAB issues the exposure draft for SFFAS 56.** The exposure draft of what becomes SFFAS 56 is issued on 14 December 2017 with comments requested by 16 March 2018. FASAB receives seventeen responses from preparers, auditors and professional associations. Seventeen comment letters is the entirety of the public deliberation on a standard authorising modified public financial reporting across the federal government. On the node SFFAS 56 - Classified Activities (FASAB, 4 October 2018). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2018-10-15, TTSA's $37.4 million deficit reported.** Vice reports SEC filings showing a $37.4 million accumulated deficit, $129,000 cash on hand, roughly $1 million raised against a $50 million target, and a going-concern warning. On the node To The Stars Academy of Arts & Science Inc. (later To The Stars Inc.). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2018-11, First full-scope DoD audit: disclaimer of opinion.** David L. Norquist, Under Secretary of Defense (Comptroller) and Chief Financial Officer, writes in the FY2018 Agency Financial Report: 'In FY 2018, the Department underwent its first full-scope financial statement audit. As anticipated, our independent auditor, the Department of Defense Office of Inspector General, issued a Disclaimer of Opinion as the result of the audit.' It will be the first of eight. On the node David L. Norquist. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2018-11-01, Bob Lazar: Area 51 and Flying Saucers released digitally in the United States.** Jeremy Corbell's 97-minute documentary, narrated by Mickey Rourke, featuring Lazar, George Knapp and Joy White. Letterboxd lists United States digital release on 1 November 2018, United Kingdom physical on 4 December 2018 and United States physical on 8 December 2018. The widely cited '4 December 2018' is the UK physical date; IMDb returned HTTP 403 and no distributor record was opened. On the node Bob Lazar: Area 51 and Flying Saucers (2018). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2018-11-06, Former PDDNI Donald Kerr elected MITRE board chairman.** MITRE's trustees elect a former director of Los Alamos and of the National Reconnaissance Office as chairman, and former House Intelligence Committee chairman Mike Rogers as vice chairman. On the node Donald M. Kerr. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2018-12-04, US 10,144,532 granted.** The inertial mass reduction craft patent issued to the Secretary of the Navy. Reporting states the application had first been rejected and was granted after the Naval Aviation Enterprise chief technology officer asserted operability to the USPTO and cited Chinese development of comparable technology. On the node US Patent 10,144,532 - Craft using an inertial mass reduction device. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2018-12-21, House Oversight and Senate HSGAC written into the SAP reporting statute.** Sec. 103 of Pub. L. 115-390 amends 50 U.S.C. 3348 to substitute 'the congressional oversight committees' for 'Congress' throughout subsections (a)-(e), replaces 'appropriate oversight committees' in (f)(1), and adds the (g)(1) definition naming the Senate Committee on Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs and the House Committee on Oversight and Government Reform alongside leadership and jurisdictional committees. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2018-12-21, House Oversight named a statutory recipient of SAP reports.** Section 103 of Public Law 115-390 amends 50 U.S.C. 3348 to substitute 'the congressional oversight committees' for 'Congress' throughout and defines that term to include the House Committee on Oversight and Government Reform and the Senate Committee on Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs. On the node 50 U.S.C. 3348, Reports Relating to Certain SAPs and Similar Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
### 2019

- **2019-01-01, Read-on request denied.** Grusch testifies he was informed in the course of official duties of a multi-decade UAP crash retrieval and reverse engineering program and was denied the additional read-ons when he requested them. No official account has ever explained the denial or its authority. On the node David Charles Grusch. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-01-01, Grusch joins UAPTF; tasked to inventory SAPs.** As NRO's representative to the UAP Task Force, Grusch is asked by the Task Force Director to identify all special access programs and controlled access programs needed to satisfy the Task Force's congressionally mandated mission. He is, in his sworn words, cleared to literally all relevant compartments. On the node David Charles Grusch. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-01-01, A private company signs a federal exploitation agreement over claimed exotic material.** To The Stars Academy executes CRADA 19-15, 'Novel & Emerging Technology Exploitation,' with the US Army CCDC Ground Vehicle Systems Center. It is filed with the SEC the following year, making it the only fully documented private-custody chain in the file. On the node CRADA between U.S. Army Ground Vehicle Systems Center and To The Stars Academy. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-01-16, DIA releases the 38-title list.** DIA responds to Steven Aftergood's FOIA request with the list of 38 AATIP research titles funded 2007-2012 at approximately $22 million, previously provided to Congress in January 2018. On the node DIA letter to Steven Aftergood listing 38 AATIP reference products (16 January 2019). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-01-16, The 38 DIRD titles enter the public record.** Nick Pope publishes the DIA transmittal letter; researchers begin extracting the individual reports through FOIA, eventually obtaining 37 of 38. On the node The 38 Defense Intelligence Reference Documents (DIRDs). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-04-01, Wilson-Davis notes surface.** The fifteen pages enter public circulation. Davis confirms the notes are his; Wilson denies the meeting and its substance. No agency has attempted to reconcile the two. On the node The Wilson-Davis Notes (16 October 2002). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2019-04-01, Elizondo's evaluation published.** Keith Kloor publishes the 'exemplary' rating and the supervisor's language on the importance of Elizondo's portfolio in Issues in Science and Technology. On the node Keith Kloor. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2019-04-11, Pentagon moves to kill JASON.** OUSD(R&E) confirms it is ending the JASON contract managed by MITRE. Two weeks later NNSA posts a notice of intent to award a short-term sole source contract to MITRE, and the panel survives. On the node JASON. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-05-07, Tipton originates the denial formula.** Internal email: Elizondo 'had no assigned responsibilities for this program.' A colleague warns the Department risks 'contradicting previous statements'; the chain closes with 'we will have to keep our messages in synch.' On the node Neill Tipton. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-05-13, Friedman dies.** Stanton Friedman dies of a heart attack at Toronto Pearson Airport returning from a lecture. He had donated his research files to the Provincial Archives of New Brunswick in the months before his death. On the node Stanton T. Friedman. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-05-20, The five observables reach print.** The earliest dated publication of the five as a set located for this board, attributing them to Luis Elizondo from his AATIP work. The Navy's PAX 205 briefing, which itemises four of them as design goals, predates it by four years. On the node The five observables (UAP performance taxonomy). Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2019-05-22, Pentagon confirms AATIP studied UAP.** Christopher Sherwood tells the New York Post that AATIP 'did pursue research and investigation into unidentified aerial phenomena'. On the node Christopher Sherwood. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-05-31, 'Unidentified' premieres on History.** The TTSA-produced series presents Elizondo as having run AATIP for eight years, prompting The Intercept to test the Department's position. On the node 'Unidentified: Inside America's UFO Investigation' (History Channel). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2019-06-01, 'No responsibilities with regard to the AATIP program'.** Christopher Sherwood's statement to The Intercept becomes the Department's fixed position on Elizondo and remains so for the next seven years. On the node Christopher Sherwood. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-06-05, Elizondo writes to DoD Public Affairs.** His 'final effort to avoid seeing this unfortunate situation escalate', with attached substantiating documents the Department did not release. On the node OSD FOIA Case 21-F-0938, Elizondo/Gough correspondence. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-06-13, Gough authenticates the Reid memo and reaffirms the denial.** 'It makes no change to previous statements. Mr. Elizondo had no assigned responsibilities for AATIP while he was in OUSD(I).' She adds that DIA administered AATIP and Elizondo was never assigned to DIA. On the node Susan Gough. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-06-13, Pentagon authenticates Reid's letter, denies its meaning.** Susan Gough confirms the 2009 SAP request is genuine while restating that Elizondo 'had no assigned responsibilities for AATIP while he was in OUSD(I)'. On the node Susan Gough. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-06-18, US 10,322,827 granted.** The high frequency gravitational wave generator patent issued to the Secretary of the Navy. On the node US Patent 10,322,827 - High frequency gravitational wave generator. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2019-07-14, USS Kidd first sights drones.** USS Kidd spots UAVs around 2200; USS Rafael Peralta and USS John Finn also report activity, with a white light hovering over Peralta's flight deck for over 90 minutes at 16 knots in low visibility. On the node July 2019 Drone Swarms, USS Kidd / USS Russell and CSG ships off California. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-07-15, 'Multiple UAVs around ship'.** USS Kidd's deck log records the phrase at 2120; USS Russell reports objects changing elevation and direction; the cruise ship Carnival Imagination reports five or six drones. The event runs nearly three hours. On the node July 2019 Drone Swarms, USS Kidd / USS Russell and CSG ships off California. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-07-25, TTSA announces metamaterials acquisition.** The ADAM Research Project announces possession of material reportedly from an advanced aerospace vehicle of unknown origin; Steve Justice says its structure and composition match no known military or commercial application. On the node ADAM Research Project (Acquisition and Data Analysis of Materials). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2019-07-26, ADAM Research Project announced.** TTSA announces acquisition of 'potentially exotic materials'; COO Steve Justice concedes they came from sources 'with varying levels of chain-of-custody documentation'. On the node ADAM Research Project (Acquisition and Data Analysis of Materials). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-07-30, Further swarm events.** Additional incidents are logged on 25 and 30 July 2019. The Navy, Coast Guard and FBI investigate and the matter reaches the Chief of Naval Operations without any attribution being made. On the node July 2019 Drone Swarms, USS Kidd / USS Russell and CSG ships off California. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-08-17, DD Form 1910 released via FOIA.** The unredacted form is published, identifying Elizondo by name and OUSD(I) title as the officer who cleared the three video files in August 2017. On the node DD Form 1910 for GoFast, Gimbal and FLIR video files. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-09-10, Navy designates the objects UAP.** Joseph Gradisher: 'The Navy designates the objects contained in these videos as unidentified aerial phenomena', while stating the Navy never released them and that no DOPSR record of an unrestricted-release request exists. On the node Joseph Gradisher. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-09-25, Entropy kinematics paper.** Knuth, Powell and Reali publish 'Estimating Flight Characteristics of Anomalous Unidentified Aerial Vehicles' - the first quantitative UAP performance analysis in a peer-reviewed physics journal. On the node Knuth, Powell & Reali (2019), Entropy 21(10):939. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-10-04, $35,000 SEC filing.** Vice reports TTSA's SEC filing recording $35,000 paid for six pieces of bismuth/magnesium-zinc metal and a piece of aluminium previously held by Linda Moulton Howe. On the node ADAM Research Project (Acquisition and Data Analysis of Materials). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-10-10, TTSA-Army CRADA signed.** Jeffery Langhout for the Army GVSC and Kari DeLonge for TTSA sign a five-year cooperative research agreement covering inertial mass reduction, metamaterials, quantum communications, beamed energy propulsion and active camouflage. On the node CRADA between U.S. Army Ground Vehicle Systems Center and To The Stars Academy. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-10-10, The Army signs for the material.** GVSC Director Jeffery Langhout signs a CRADA with To The Stars Academy at Detroit Arsenal covering inertial mass reduction and metamaterial waveguides. The text is released 18 October; all twenty-nine emails of correspondence are later withheld in full. On the node Detroit Arsenal, Warren, Michigan. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-10-17, US Army CRADA signed.** TTSA enters a five-year cooperative research and development agreement with the US Army Combat Capabilities Development Command covering inertial mass reduction, metamaterials, quantum physics and beamed energy propulsion. On the node To The Stars Academy - US Army CCDC Cooperative Research and Development Agreement (17 October 2019). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-10-17, CRADA announced publicly.** Dr. Joseph Cannon of Army Futures Command calls TTSA 'an exciting, non-traditional source for novel materials'. The Army releases the agreement text the next day. On the node CRADA between U.S. Army Ground Vehicle Systems Center and To The Stars Academy. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-10-21, Davis briefs SASC staff.** Eric Davis gives a classified briefing on retrievals of unexplained objects to staff members of the Senate Armed Services Committee. On the node Eric Davis classified briefings to Senate Armed Services and Senate Intelligence, 21 and 23 October 2019. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-10-23, Davis briefs SSCI members.** Two days later Davis briefs members of the Senate Intelligence Committee on the same subject. On the node Eric Davis classified briefings to Senate Armed Services and Senate Intelligence, 21 and 23 October 2019. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2019-12-06, Gough reverses: 'Neither AATIP nor AAWSAP were UAP related'.** The Department repudiates its own May 2019 acknowledgement, saying Sherwood was repeating an earlier spokesperson who 'is no longer with my organization', and that AATIP 'did not pursue research and investigation into unidentified aerial phenomena'. On the node Susan Gough. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
### 2020

- **2020-02-14, Kit Green DIRD released.** Popular Mechanics publishes Green's AAWSAP medical study, revealing the Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin imprint, the November 2007 Puthoff/Hathaway proposal and the human-injury reverse-engineering hypothesis. On the node DIRD: 'Clinical Medical Acute & Subacute Field Effects on Human Dermal & Neurological Tissues'. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-02-21, Army withholds all CRADA correspondence.** CCDC FOIA Officer Joy L. Linton locates 29 emails totalling 100 pages of TTSA-Army correspondence and denies all of them under exemptions (b)(4) and (b)(5). On the node U.S. Army Combat Capabilities Development Command, Ground Vehicle Systems Center. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-03-01, Davis briefs a DoD agency.** Davis gives a classified briefing to a Defense Department agency about retrievals from 'off-world vehicles not made on this earth'. The agency has never been identified. On the node Eric Davis classified briefings to Senate Armed Services and Senate Intelligence, 21 and 23 October 2019. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-04-13, Papoose Lake photographed from the air on a legal overflight: nothing there.** Tyler Rogoway of The War Zone publishes aerial images taken on a pilot's authorised flight directly over Papoose Lake: 'nothing appears to be there, just as satellite images show. There isn't even an access road and, as you can see, the lakebed is flooded, which is a fairly common occurrence.' On the node S-4 / Papoose Lake, Nevada. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2020-04-27, DoD releases the three Navy videos.** The Department authorizes release of FLIR1, GIMBAL and GO FAST, stating 'The aerial phenomena observed in the videos remain characterized as unidentified.' On the node FLIR1 (Nimitz ATFLIR video, 14 Nov 2004). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-04-27, DoD authorises release of the three videos.** The Department releases FLIR1, Gimbal and GoFast officially, calls their earlier appearance the product of 'unauthorized releases in 2007 and 2017', and states the phenomena 'remain characterized as unidentified'. On the node DoD Statement on the Release of Historical Navy Videos, 27 April 2020. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-04-27, The Department releases FLIR1.** DoD authorises release of the three Navy videos, stating the phenomena 'remain characterized as unidentified' - the government authenticating the artefact and declining to explain it. On the node USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-04-27, The Pentagon authenticates the imagery.** The Department of Defense formally releases FLIR1, GIMBAL and GOFAST, confirming the videos are genuine while stating the phenomena in them remain unidentified. The government thereby authenticates the evidence and declines to explain it - the exact posture the disclosure movement has been arguing against ever since. On the node Military Aviators & Operators. Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2020-06-03, 'The truth... is that I ran the program'.** Elizondo writes to Susan Gough noting she corrected the AATIP/UAP statement but never the statement about him. The letter sits in the Department's own FOIA reading room. On the node OSD FOIA Case 21-F-0938, Elizondo/Gough correspondence. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-07-23, 'We couldn't make it ourselves'.** The New York Times publishes Davis's account of his briefings and his statement about the analysed material, alongside Harry Reid's call for information on retrieved craft to come out. On the node Eric Davis classified briefings to Senate Armed Services and Senate Intelligence, 21 and 23 October 2019. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-08-04, UAP Task Force established under Navy lead.** The Deputy Secretary of Defense formalises the government's UAP effort under USD(I&S) cognisance with the Department of the Navy leading, placing it in ONI's institutional orbit and out of the hands of offices with acquisition equity. On the node Office of Naval Intelligence. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-08-04, UAP Task Force approved.** Deputy Secretary of Defense David Norquist approves the UAP Task Force under the Department of the Navy with OUSD(I&S) cognizance. The Navy announces it on 14 August. On the node Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force (UAPTF). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2020-12-01, Elizondo, Mellon and Justice leave TTSA.** The three government-side principals depart; the aerospace and materials work winds down and the entity later renames itself To The Stars Inc. On the node To The Stars Academy of Arts & Science Inc. (later To The Stars Inc.). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2020-12-21, The NISPOM is published as a federal rule.** 32 CFR Part 117 published at 85 FR 83312, 21 December 2020, with the carve-out written into 117.6(b): 'The Director, DCSA, will be relieved of this oversight function for DoD special access programs (SAPs) when the Secretary of Defense or the Deputy Secretary of Defense approves a carve-out provision in accordance with DoDD 5205.07.' No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 2021

- **2021-01-01, An IC controlled access program is expanded to include reverse engineering.** AARO confirms that one intelligence community CAP was 'unnecessarily expanded in 2021 to include a UAP reverse-engineering mission' without evidence or mission need, and was later disestablished. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-02-24, 32 CFR Part 117 takes effect; the carve-out becomes regulation.** With the NISPOM's conversion from DoD 5220.22-M into a codified rule, the DCSA carve-out ceased to be an internal departmental arrangement and became a provision of the Code of Federal Regulations, citing DoDD 5205.07 by URL. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2021-02-24, The NISPOM becomes federal regulation at 32 CFR Part 117.** The carve-out provision moves from a DoD manual into the Code of Federal Regulations at 117.6(b)(3): the Director, DCSA 'will be relieved of this oversight function for DoD special access programs (SAPs) when the Secretary of Defense or the Deputy Secretary of Defense approves a carve-out provision'. On the node 32 CFR Part 117, NISPOM Rule. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-04-13, McCandlish declared deceased at 1946 Trumpet Drive, Redding, California.** Shasta County Sheriff-Coroner case C21-0324, Verdict of Death (without jury): 'In the matter of the death of Mark Edward McCandlish, a 68 year old male ... he was declared deceased on the 13th day of April 2021.' Cause of death 'Gunshot wound of head'; other significant conditions 'None'. On the node Mark McCandlish. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2021-04-19, External examination at 0713 by Dan Purtzer MD; no internal examination performed.** The Report of External Examination, dated 04/19/21 at 0713, gives the cause of death as 'central nervous system damage caused by self-inflicted gunshot wound to the head', records an entrance wound '1 cm above the right ear' whose edges 'have powder burns' and an exit wound above the left ear, and states on page 2: 'Due to the obvious cause of death, internal examination is not performed.' On the node Mark McCandlish. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2021-05-01, Elizondo files DoD IG complaint.** Alleging 'malicious activities, coordinated disinformation, professional misconduct, whistleblower reprisal and explicit threats', naming Garry Reid, Susan Gough and Neill Tipton. On the node Elizondo complaint to the DoD Inspector General, May 2021. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2021-05-16, 60 Minutes brings the aviators forward.** Fravor, Dietrich, Graves, Mellon and Elizondo appear on CBS; Dietrich gives her first public account: 'I never wanted to be on national TV, no offense.' On the node Alex Dietrich. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-05-16, 60 Minutes segment.** Elizondo, Mellon and Navy aviators appear in a CBS 60 Minutes report on UAP in restricted U.S. airspace, weeks before the ODNI preliminary assessment. On the node Luis D. Elizondo. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2021-05-20, Doty personnel file published.** A 321-page New Mexico State Police personnel file for Richard Doty is released under state public-records law - documentation federal FOIA never produced. On the node Richard Doty New Mexico State Police Personnel File [321 pages]. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-05-21, Gough's partial re-reversal.** The Department concedes the AATIP contract 'allowed for research drawn from a wide variety of sources, including reports of UAPs' while insisting UAP examination 'was not the purpose of AATIP'. The denial of Elizondo's role does not move. On the node Susan Gough. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-06-14, Nolan says he was approached.** Nolan tells KQED that representatives of the government and an aerospace corporation asked him to assess medical harm from 'supposed interactions with an anomalous craft'. On the node Garry Nolan. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-06-17, Nine transients on a 1950 plate.** Villarroel and colleagues report nine simultaneously occurring transients on a single Palomar plate from 12 April 1950 in Scientific Reports. On the node VASCO - Vanishing and Appearing Sources during a Century of Observations. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-06-21, Verdict of Death signed by Chief Deputy Coroner Logan Stonehouse; manner of death Suicide.** The verdict gives manner of death as Suicide and records that 'Phillip Wacker, Deputy Coroner, conducted the investigation and established the identity of the decedent. A postmortem examination was conducted by Dr. Dan Purtzer, M.D., Pathologist.' The postmortem it rests on is an external examination that expressly declined an internal one. On the node Mark McCandlish. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2021-06-25, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2021-06-25, ODNI Preliminary Assessment published.** Nine pages, 144 reports, 80 with multiple sensors, five explanatory categories - and a contributor list naming NRO, NGA, NSA, DIA, Navy/ONI, DARPA, FAA and NOAA. The list is the most useful part of the document. On the node Office of the Director of National Intelligence. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-06-25, ODNI preliminary assessment: 144 reports.** 144 reports from USG sources spanning November 2004 to March 2021; only one resolved with high confidence; 18 incidents in 21 reports showed unusual movement; aviators face 'disparagement' for reporting. On the node ODNI Preliminary Assessment: Unidentified Aerial Phenomena (25 June 2021). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-06-25, ODNI Preliminary Assessment.** ODNI delivers the 9-page Preliminary Assessment to Congress: 144 incidents, one explained, and the data-limitation formula that every AARO annual report will reuse through FY2025. On the node ODNI Preliminary Assessment: Unidentified Aerial Phenomena (25 June 2021). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-07-01, First disclosure to the DoD Inspector General.** Grusch provides classified information to the DoD IG regarding the improper withholding and concealment of UAP-related material from Congress, ten months before the ICIG filing. On the node Grusch PPD-19 Urgent Concern Complaint to the ICIG. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-07-26, Galileo Project founded.** Avi Loeb founds the Galileo Project four weeks after the ODNI preliminary assessment, on the premise that unclassified instrumentation is the way past a classified impasse. On the node The Galileo Project. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-08-01, 'Skinwalkers at the Pentagon' published.** The DIA program manager, the contractor's deputy administrator and the reporter who broke the story publish a DOPSR-cleared insider account including Lacatski's claim of entering a craft of unknown origin. On the node Skinwalkers at the Pentagon (Lacatski, Kelleher, Knapp, 2021). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-10-18, CEFAA becomes SEFAA.** By Resolución Exenta N° 04/3/0113/1385 the Chilean office is moved in the DGAC organisation chart to the Subdepartamento Comunicacional and renamed the Sección de Estudios de Fenómenos Aéreos Anómalos. On the node CEFAA / SEFAA - Chilean anomalous aerial phenomena office, Dirección General de Aeronáutica Civil. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2021-11-01, Grusch becomes NGA co-lead for UAP analysis.** Joins NGA as a GS-15 Senior GEOINT Capabilities Requirements Officer and becomes the agency's co-lead for UAP and trans-medium object analysis, reporting to the UAPTF and later to AARO. Reviewing overhead collection of such events is one of his primary tasks. On the node David Charles Grusch. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-11-23, AOIMSG stood up.** Deputy Secretary Hicks directs establishment of the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group, a coordinating layer with no independent authority, overseen by AOIMEXEC. On the node Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group (AOIMSG). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2021-12-27, Sec. 1683 enacted.** The FY2022 NDAA is signed as P.L. 117-81. Section 1683, Gillibrand's provision, orders a permanent UAP office stood up within 180 days and is codified at 50 U.S.C. 3373. On the node FY2022 NDAA Sec. 1683 (P.L. 117-81; 50 U.S.C. 3373). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-12-27, FY2022 NDAA signed; UAP office written into statute.** Public Law 117-81 is approved. Section 1683, codified at 50 U.S.C. 3373, requires DoD and ODNI to stand up a standing UAP office within 180 days, designate line organizations able to 'rapidly respond to, and conduct field investigations of' UAP incidents, and run a science plan aimed at performance 'that exceed the known state of the art in science or technology' and at providing 'the foundation for potential future investments to replicate any such advanced characteristics and performance.' On the node FY2022 NDAA Sec. 1683 (P.L. 117-81; 50 U.S.C. 3373). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-12-27, FY2022 NDAA enacted.** P.L. 117-81 creates the statutory requirement for a permanent UAP office and the reporting regime that becomes 50 U.S.C. 3373. On the node All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-12-28, Harry Reid dies.** The senator who created the funded lineage and was refused the SAP he asked for dies in Henderson, Nevada. On the node Harry M. Reid. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2021-12-31, The statutory annual DoD SAP report to Congress lapses.** Per the 'Termination of Reporting Requirements' note carried with 10 U.S.C. 119 in the U.S. Code: the provisions of subsecs. (a) and (b) requiring submittal of an annual report to Congress terminated effective 31 December 2021 under sec. 1061 of Pub. L. 114-328. The annual SAP census and the annual new-SAP notice are the two pieces that lapsed; the (c), (d) and (f) notices did not. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 2022

- **2022-01-01, Americans for Safe Aerospace founded.** Ryan Graves builds the aircrew reporting channel the Navy did not provide; by July 2023 it has nearly 5,000 members and is working with more than 30 UAP witnesses. On the node Americans for Safe Aerospace. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-01-01, First peer-reviewed UAP materials analysis.** Nolan, Vallee, Jiang and Lemke publish the SIMS and MIBI analysis of the Council Bluffs mass in Progress in Aerospace Sciences: no isotopic anomaly, but metals mixed incompletely by an industrial process of unknown origin. On the node Nolan, Vallee, Jiang & Lemke (2022), Progress in Aerospace Sciences 128:100788. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-01-01, AARO contracts Oak Ridge to test the magnesium specimen.** The Pentagon's UAP office sends its one publicly acknowledged material sample to a laboratory operated by UT-Battelle - a partnership including the same Battelle Memorial Institute that analysed the Air Force's UFO data in the 1950s. On the node Oak Ridge National Laboratory (UT-Battelle, LLC). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-03-01, DIA cannot find the Utah ranch report.** DIA tells a FOIA requester it can locate no report matching 'Utah Ranch Investigation: August 2009-February 2010' as described by the program's own manager. On the node BAASS Ten Month Report (2009). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-03-15, Congress creates a no-waiver oversight regime for IC controlled access programs.** Sec. 504 of the FY2022 Intelligence Authorization Act (Pub. L. 117-103, div. X, 136 Stat. 986) adds sec. 501A to the National Security Act, codified at 50 U.S.C. 3091a: semiannual DNI briefings on every CAP in effect, a bar on establishing any CAP, compartment or subcompartment before notifying the intelligence and appropriations committees and the four congressional leaders, and annual reports listing every active and terminated compartment and subcompartment. There is no waiver provision. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2022-03-15, Congress creates a statutory oversight regime for IC Controlled Access Programs.** Section 504 of the FY2022 Intelligence Authorization Act, Public Law 117-103, adds section 501A to the National Security Act: semiannual briefings on every CAP, a prohibition on establishing a CAP or compartment before notification, and annual reports listing every active and terminated compartment with a DNI certification for each. On the node 50 U.S.C. 3091a, Congressional Oversight of Controlled Access Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-03-15, IAA FY2022 sec. 413 compels immediate handover of UAP data.** Public Law 117-103 is approved, carrying the Intelligence Authorization Act for FY2022 as Division X. Section 413 (50 U.S.C. 3373a) requires the DNI and SecDef jointly to make every IC element and DoD component with UAP data 'make such data available immediately' to the UAP Task Force and NASIC, with classified quarterly reports to the intelligence and armed services committees. On the node Intelligence Authorization Act for FY2022, Sec. 413 (Div. X of PL 117-103; 50 U.S.C. 3373a). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-03-29, John Lear dies.** The Museum of Flight Archives authority record states: 'John Lear died on March 29, 2022.' He was born 3 December 1942, the son of William Powell Lear and Moya Olsen Lear, and his materials sit within the William P. and Moya Olsen Lear Family Collection. On the node John Olsen Lear. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2022-04-01, Classified conversation with Sean Kirkpatrick.** Three months before Kirkpatrick takes over AARO, Grusch briefs him in a classified setting and passes him concerns. Grusch later testifies he was happy to give counsel on where to look; Kirkpatrick did not follow up. On the node Sean M. Kirkpatrick. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-04-01, Garry Reid removed.** Reid is removed as Director for Defense Intelligence after DoD IG investigations substantiate ethics violations, 65 instances of official business on personal email, and unwelcome physical contact with subordinates. On the node Garry P. Reid. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-05, Grusch files the ICIG urgent-concern complaint.** Charles McCullough III, himself a former Intelligence Community Inspector General, files a Disclosure of Urgent Concern(s) and Complaint of Reprisal with the ICIG on behalf of David Grusch, covering information Grusch had gathered from 2019. On the node The misappropriation allegation: Grusch's ICIG complaint and sworn testimony. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-05-17, Congress asks DoD about the Wilson-Davis memo.** At the House Intelligence subcommittee UAP hearing, Ronald Moultrie and Scott Bray are asked directly about the Wilson-Davis memo and the Malmstrom missile events and state they are unaware of them. On the node The Wilson-Davis Notes (16 October 2002). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-05-17, First open congressional UAP hearing in over fifty years.** The House Permanent Select Committee on Intelligence, Subcommittee on Counterterrorism, Counterintelligence, and Counterproliferation met at 9:00 a.m. in Room HVC-210 with Rep. Andre Carson presiding, taking testimony from Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence and Security Ronald S. Moultrie and Deputy Director of Naval Intelligence Scott W. Bray. Carson opened by saying the subcommittee would 'bring that organization out of the shadows', referring to AOIMSG. On the node House Intelligence subcommittee hearing, 17 May 2022: 'Unidentified Aerial Phenomena'. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-05-24, NASA commissions its panel.** NASA commissions the sixteen-member UAP Independent Study Team under David Spergel - with access to unclassified data only. On the node NASA Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Independent Study Team. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-05-25, PPD-19 urgent-concern complaint filed with the ICIG.** Charles McCullough III, the first Inspector General of the Intelligence Community, files Grusch's urgent-concern complaint with the office he once ran. On the node Grusch PPD-19 Urgent Concern Complaint to the ICIG. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-07, ICIG finds the complaint credible and urgent.** The Intelligence Community Inspector General determines the complaint credible and urgent - the statutory finding that obliges transmission to the congressional intelligence committees. On the node The misappropriation allegation: Grusch's ICIG complaint and sworn testimony. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-07-01, ICIG finds the complaint credible and urgent.** The statutory determination that obliges transmission to the congressional intelligence committees. It has never been withdrawn, and no executive-branch document reconciles it with AARO's later findings. On the node Inspector General of the Intelligence Community. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-07-15, AARO established.** Hicks signs the memorandum creating AARO, disestablishing AOIMSG, and ordering the Navy to disestablish the UAPTF and transfer its data and materials. Announced 20 July. On the node Memorandum: Establishment of the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (15 July 2022). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-07-20, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2022-08-17, FOIA 18-F-0324 released.** Four years and eight months after the request, DoD releases the two resignation letters and the two Garry Reid memoranda, eight pages. On the node OSD/JS FOIA Case 18-F-0324. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-10-13, DIA finally releases the AAWSAP contract.** Fourteen years after award and eleven years after withholding it, DIA releases contract HHM402-08-C-0072 under FOIA-00349-2018, with all government personnel names redacted. On the node DIA Contract HHM402-08-C-0072 (BAASS). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-12-23, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2022-12-23, AARO named; Historical Record Report ordered.** P.L. 117-263 carries IAA FY2023 Sec. 6802, restating Sec. 1683 to name the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, and Sec. 1673, creating the authorized reporting channel that voids nondisclosure agreements. On the node FY2023 NDAA Sec. 1673 (P.L. 117-263; 50 U.S.C. 3373b). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-12-23, FY2023 NDAA UAP whistleblower protections enacted.** The FY2023 National Defense Authorization Act includes provisions permitting authorized UAP disclosures without retaliation regardless of prior nondisclosure agreements. The Debrief reports Grusch helped draft the language. On the node FY2023 NDAA, Public Law 117-263 - Sec. 1673 (50 U.S.C. 3373b) UAP reporting/whistleblower protection; Div. F secs. 6802-6803 establishing AARO. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-12-23, FY2023 NDAA creates AARO and the whistleblower channel.** Public Law 117-263 is approved. Section 1673 (50 U.S.C. 3373b) creates a secure authorized-reporting mechanism covering federal and contractor activity involving 'material retrieval, material analysis, reverse engineering', voids nondisclosure agreements as against authorized disclosures, overrides EO 13526 and Atomic Energy Act chapter 18 restrictions, and bans reprisal including clearance revocation. Division F sec. 6802 rewrites 50 U.S.C. 3373 to establish the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office and inserts the statutory definitions of 'unidentified anomalous phenomena' and 'transmedium objects or devices'. On the node FY2023 NDAA, Public Law 117-263 - Sec. 1673 (50 U.S.C. 3373b) UAP reporting/whistleblower protection; Div. F secs. 6802-6803 establishing AARO. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-12-23, AARO codified in statute.** The FY2023 NDAA (P.L. 117-263) codifies AARO and its public reporting mandate at 50 U.S.C. 3373, including the annual report deadline AARO later misses. On the node All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2022-12-26, US 10,135,366 expires for non-payment of maintenance fees.** The first of the two granted Pais patents lapsed for non-payment of the first maintenance fee, five weeks before the inertial mass reduction patent did the same. USPTO had mailed a maintenance fee reminder on 11 July 2022. On the node US Patent 10,135,366 - Electromagnetic field generator. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 2023

- **2023-01-09, US 10,144,532 expires for non-payment of maintenance fees.** Four years after fighting an appeal to obtain the grant, the Navy did not pay the first maintenance fee on the inertial mass reduction craft patent. USPTO records it as expired under 37 CFR 1.362, placing the invention in the public domain. On the node US Patent 10,144,532 - Craft using an inertial mass reduction device. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2023-01-10, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2023-01-12, The Navy's own report on the Tic Tac surfaces.** 72 range fouler reports posted to the Navy FOIA reading room include the 14 November 2004 incident: a 46-foot object with no nacelles, pylons or wings, and the disposition line 'Reporting terminated at 3rd Fleet, there was no process to share the reporting.' On the node USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-01-13, 72 range fouler reports released.** The Navy releases 72 previously Secret//FVEY range fouler reports covering 2004-2021, including the Nimitz report describing a 46-foot object with no nacelles, pylons or wings. On the node Navy Range Fouler Reports (72 reports, 2004-2021). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-01, DoD IG interviews Elizondo.** A 103-page transcript of the Inspector General's interview of Elizondo is later released under FOIA, along with 126 pages of DoD IG UAP emails in February 2024. On the node Elizondo complaint to the DoD Inspector General, May 2021. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-10, Alaska object destroyed.** US fighters destroy an object at 40,000 feet described as the size of a small car, cylindrical and silver-grey with no apparent propulsion, over Prudhoe Bay. It is tracked as UAP #20. On the node February 2023 Alaska, Yukon and Lake Huron Shootdowns. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-11, Yukon object destroyed.** An F-22 destroys a small cylindrical object at 40,000 feet over the Yukon; the Canadian Privy Council records it as UAP #23, unverified as to function, propulsion or national affiliation. On the node Charette Privy Council Memorandum (Secret, 14 Feb 2023). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-12, Lake Huron object destroyed.** Two F-16CMs of the 148th Fighter Wing destroy an object at roughly 20,000 feet with an AIM-9X. A pilot reports 'I can't tell if it's metallic or what, but I can see like lines coming down below it.' On the node February 2023 Alaska, Yukon and Lake Huron Shootdowns. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-13, Galileo Project overview published.** Loeb and Laukien publish the project's architecture in the Journal of Astronomical Instrumentation, alongside Cloete et al.'s detection and tracking platform - methods published before observations. On the node The Galileo Project. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-14, Charette Secret memo to Trudeau.** The Clerk of the Privy Council records that exploitation of UAP #20 is incomplete and that UAP #23's function and propulsion are unknown. On the node Charette Privy Council Memorandum (Secret, 14 Feb 2023). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-15, USS Jackson records four objects.** At about 1915 PST in Warning Area W-291, USS Jackson's CIC records four self-luminous tic-tac objects, one emerging from the ocean, on a Star SAFIRE EO/IR system with intact position and time metadata. On the node USS Jackson (LCS-6) Encounter, 15 February 2023. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-02-15, USS Jackson: a light rising from the waterline.** At about 1915 PST in W-291 off Southern California, Senior Chief Alexandro Wiggins observes a self-luminous object emerge from the ocean, join three others, and depart with near-instantaneous acceleration. CIC records it on the ship's Star SAFIRE and reports to the Tactical Action Officer. On the node Alexandro Wiggins. Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.
- **2023-02-16, Sixteen senators demand full AARO funding.** Gillibrand and Rubio lead fourteen colleagues insisting AARO receive the amount in the classified attachment, arguing the office offers 'increased transparency to the American people.' On the node Kirsten E. Gillibrand. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-02-28, The EG&G account published as Congress reopens the question.** Liberation Times publishes George Knapp's account of Al O'Donnell describing a recovered saucer and a live being, together with D'Amato's public statement and his reported private view that a private-company cover-up was 'entirely feasible.' On the node Alfred 'Al' O'Donnell. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-03-11, Public call to audit Lockheed Martin's special access programs.** Jeremy Corbell challenges the Senate Appropriations Committee to audit Lockheed Martin SAPs; Elizondo responds that the inquiry should extend to several other aerospace companies that have likely played a key historical role. On the node Lockheed Martin Corporation. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-04-04, DoD clears Grusch's statements for open publication.** DOPSR approves his first submission on 4 April and his second on 6 April 2023. Internal reviewer correspondence in the FOIA release shows the sensitive-areas references were raised and judged not a problem. On the node Grusch DOPSR Prepublication Clearance (FOIA 23-F-0946). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-04-07, Grusch leaves federal service.** He departs NGA and the government to pursue accountability through public disclosure, his information cutoff date for anything he can attest to from inside. On the node David Charles Grusch. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-04-19, Kirkpatrick testifies to SASC on AARO.** The Senate Armed Services Subcommittee on Emerging Threats and Capabilities holds an open hearing on 'The Mission, Activities, Oversight, and Budget of the All-Domain Anomaly Resolution Office' with AARO Director Sean Kirkpatrick as sole witness, following a closed session the same day. Printed as S. Hrg. 118-648 (CHRG-118shrg60390). On the node Senate Armed Services Emerging Threats subcommittee hearing, 19 April 2023 - 'The Mission, Activities, Oversight, and Budget of the All-Domain Anomaly Resolution Office'. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-04-19, Kirkpatrick testifies to the Senate.** The only open Senate hearing on AARO under its first director. Kirkpatrick reports 650+ cases and no credible evidence of extraterrestrial activity. Published as S.Hrg. 118-648. On the node Senate Armed Services Emerging Threats subcommittee hearing, 19 April 2023 - 'The Mission, Activities, Oversight, and Budget of the All-Domain Anomaly Resolution Office'. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-01, IM1 Pacific expedition.** The Galileo Project drags a magnetic sled across the seabed off Papua New Guinea, recovering several hundred sub-millimetre spherules from the CNEOS 2014-01-08 site. On the node Galileo Project Pacific Ocean expedition to the CNEOS 2014-01-08 (IM1) site, June-July 2023. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-05, Grusch names the mechanism on the record.** In The Debrief, Leslie Kean and Ralph Blumenthal quote Grusch: 'Individuals on these UAP programs approached me in my official capacity and disclosed their concerns regarding a multitude of wrongdoings, such as illegal contracting against the Federal Acquisition Regulations and other criminality and the suppression of information across a qualified industrial base and academia.' Procurement fraud, not merely secrecy, is the accusation. On the node The misappropriation allegation: Grusch's ICIG complaint and sworn testimony. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-05, Grusch allegations published.** The Debrief publishes David Grusch's crash-retrieval allegations; Tim Burchett begins publicly demanding a House hearing. On the node Timothy Floyd Burchett. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-05, The Debrief publishes the Grusch allegations.** Leslie Kean and Ralph Blumenthal report that the US has retrieved intact and partially intact craft of non-human origin, with on-record corroboration from Karl Nell, Christopher Mellon and a serving NASIC officer using the name Jonathan Grey. On the node The Debrief: Intelligence Officials Say U.S. Has Retrieved Craft of Non-Human Origin. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-05, Coulthart broadcasts Grusch.** Ross Coulthart's NewsNation interviews convert the ICIG complaint from a written allegation into a broadcast event, giving it a face and a national audience. On the node Ross Coulthart. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-05, Grusch story published by The Debrief.** Kean and Blumenthal publish David Grusch's claim that the US operates a multi-decade crash retrieval and reverse-engineering programme. The New York Times, which ran their 2017 scoop, does not run it; The Debrief does. On the node The Debrief. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-05, The paper of record declines the Grusch story.** Leslie Kean and Ralph Blumenthal, whose 2017 AATIP scoop the New York Times published, take the David Grusch story to The Debrief instead. The story did not fail verification; it failed placement. The change of venue is an artefact of institutional appetite and belongs in the concealment record, not the journalism record. On the node The Debrief. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-06, Department denies Grusch's claims.** OSD Public Affairs, through Susan Gough, states AARO has not discovered verifiable information substantiating claims of a programme possessing or reverse-engineering extraterrestrial materials - a statement bounded by AARO's discoveries, not the Department's knowledge. On the node Susan Gough. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-07, Pentagon issues the standing denial.** Susan Gough: AARO has not discovered any verifiable information to substantiate claims that any programs regarding the possession or reverse-engineering of extraterrestrial materials have existed in the past or exist currently. The sentence bounds itself to what one office has discovered. On the node Susan Gough. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-07, The MoD file covering the Calvine period is signed closed until 2076.** DEFE 24/1940, 'UFO reports', 1 August 1990 to 31 January 1991, former reference D/Sec(AS)12/2 Part J, carries closure code 84 under the criterion 'Personal information where the applicant is a 3rd party', a Freedom of Information Act exemption instrument signed on 7 June 2023, and a record opening date of 1 January 2076. The closure is the series norm rather than a singling out: the neighbouring files open between 2072 and 2077 on the same basis. A redacted digital copy, DEFE 24/1940/1, has been open since 20 October 2008 and would not download from this environment. On the node Joint Air Reconnaissance Intelligence Centre (JARIC). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-06-11, First television interview with Ross Coulthart.** Grusch says the recoveries are non-human origin technical vehicles, that we are definitely not alone, that sometimes you encounter dead pilots, and that a sophisticated disinformation campaign targets the public. On the node Ross Coulthart. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-06-22, Senate Intelligence Committee votes to compel a contractor inventory.** S. 2103 Sec. 1104, reported unanimously, would require any current or former federal contractor holding government-derived UAP material to notify the DNI within 60 days and produce 'a comprehensive list of all non-earth origin or exotic unidentified anomalous phenomena material,' with immunity for compliance. On the node S. 2103 Sec. 1104 - SSCI contractor disclosure and immunity provision (stripped). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-11, S. 2226 introduced.** Chairman Jack Reed introduces the FY2024 NDAA, the vehicle that will carry both UAP regimes. On the node John F. Reed. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.

Warner files S.Amdt. 666, the IAA carrying the contractor turn-in, the same day. On the node S.Amdt. 797 to S. 2226 (Schumer-Rounds UAP Disclosure Act amendment). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-13, Schumer submits the UAP Disclosure Act as S.Amdt. 797.** 'SA 797. Mr. SCHUMER (for himself, Mr. Rounds, Mr. Rubio, Mrs. Gillibrand, Mr. Young, and Mr. Heinrich) submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by him to the bill S. 2226 ... which was ordered to lie on the table.' The text is the Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Disclosure Act of 2023, including the exercise of eminent domain over recovered technologies of unknown origin and biological evidence of non-human intelligence, and a nine-member Senate-confirmed Records Review Board. On the node S.Amdt. 797 to S. 2226 (Schumer-Rounds UAP Disclosure Act amendment). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.

The artefact is dated and openable; the account within it is a man's own statement and nothing more. On the node Michael Herrera. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.

Grusch's official witness title places the National Reconnaissance Office inside the chain of custody of the modern allegation. On the node National Reconnaissance Office. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.

Fravor's statement: 'the incident was never investigated, none of my crew were ever questioned, tapes were never taken.' On the node House Oversight hearing, 26 July 2023 - 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena: Implications on National Security, Public Safety, and Government Transparency'. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.

His written statement describes a 'multi-decade UAP crash retrieval and reverse engineering program' to which he was denied access and refers to 'Non-Human Reverse Engineering Programs'. It contains no funding detail; that he reserved for classified session. On the node The misappropriation allegation: Grusch's ICIG complaint and sworn testimony. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.

- **2023-07-26, Non-human biologics, under oath.** Answering Mace, Grusch testifies that biologics came with some recoveries and that they were assessed non-human by people with direct knowledge still on the program. On the node David Charles Grusch. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.

- **2023-07-26, Ogles names the Holman Rule.** Ogles volunteers to initiate the Holman Rule against any personnel, program or agency that denies Congress access to Grusch in a SCIF. No such amendment has been offered. On the node Andrew Ogles. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-26, AOC finds the notification loophole.** Ocasio-Cortez identifies that there are no statutory definitions or requirements for congressional notification and asks whether notification means a phone call to a chairman or 'an email to, hypothetically, a dead email box.'
