- **2023-07-26, House Oversight hearing.** Grusch testifies under oath, thirteen days after the amendment premised on his allegations is filed. On the node David Charles Grusch. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.

- **2023-07-26, House Oversight UAP hearing.** George Knapp attends; the hearing establishes the congressional venue that Shellenberger will use sixteen months later. On the node House Oversight hearing, 26 July 2023 - 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena: Implications on National Security, Public Safety, and Government Transparency'. Strand: oversight. On the node House Oversight hearing, 26 July 2023 - 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena: Implications on National Security, Public Safety, and Government Transparency'. Strand: program. - **2023-07-26, Grusch, Fravor and Graves testify.** The first congressional hearing at which a former intelligence officer testifies under oath to a multi-decade crash retrieval programme. On the node House Oversight hearing, 26 July 2023 - 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena: Implications on National Security, Public Safety, and Government Transparency'. Strand: oversight. - **2023-07-26, Grusch testifies under oath.** House Oversight hearing at which Grusch testifies that the UAP Task Force Director tasked him to inventory relevant SAPs and that the read-ons were refused. On the node David Charles Grusch. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-26, Three witnesses under oath.** David Grusch, David Fravor and Ryan Graves testify under oath before the House Oversight Committee. Grusch describes a multi-decade retrieval and reverse-engineering programme reported to him by individuals he named to the Intelligence Community Inspector General; Fravor and Graves describe what they personally observed with sensor corroboration. On the node Witness Chain. Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2023-07-27, Senate passes both regimes.** S. 2226 passes the Senate carrying Division G, Secs. 9001-9015 including the eminent domain command, and Division M Sec. 1102, the contractor turn-in with immunity. On the node UAP Disclosure Act of 2023 (S.Amdt. 797 to S. 2226; Division G of the Senate-passed FY2024 NDAA). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-27, AARO director calls the hearing insulting.** Kirkpatrick posts, in a statement the Pentagon authenticates, that the hearing was insulting to DoD and IC officers and that the central source of the allegations has refused to speak with AARO. On the node Sean M. Kirkpatrick. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-27, Senate passes eminent domain over privately held recovered technology.** Division I of the Senate-passed NDAA includes Sec. 9010: 'The Federal Government shall exercise eminent domain over any and all recovered technologies of unknown origin and biological evidence of non-human intelligence that may be controlled by private persons or entities.' On the node UAP Disclosure Act of 2023 (S.Amdt. 797 to S. 2226; Division G of the Senate-passed FY2024 NDAA). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-27, Senate passes S. 2226 carrying the full UAP Disclosure Act.** S. 2226 as engrossed in the Senate contains DIVISION G--UNIDENTIFIED ANOMALOUS PHENOMENA DISCLOSURE, secs. 9001-9015, in full: the Records Collection, the Review Board, the eminent-domain provision, the 25-year disclosure deadline, and the appropriations authorization. On the node S. 2226 (118th Congress) - Senate FY2024 NDAA vehicle for the UAP Disclosure Act. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-07-28, Kirkpatrick calls the hearing 'insulting'.** The sitting AARO director posts publicly that the congressional UAP hearing was insulting to the workforce; the White House distances itself from the remarks. On the node Sean M. Kirkpatrick. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-07-30, Turner disparages the testimony.** The HPSCI chairman says the striking thing about the hearing was that 'repeatedly over and over again, the whistleblowers had to say, actually, I don't have any knowledge.' On the node Michael R. Turner. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-07-31, Hicks takes direct oversight of AARO.** Days after Grusch testifies, the Deputy Secretary pulls AARO out of the USD(I&S) chain; its director now reports directly to her. On the node Kathleen H. Hicks. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-08-09, Grusch's psychiatric-detention records published.** Ken Klippenstein publishes Loudoun County police records of Grusch's 2014 and 2018 emergency custody episodes, obtained under Virginia FOIA, two weeks after the sworn testimony. Members of Congress later describe the leak as a discrediting operation. On the node Ken Klippenstein. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-08-15, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2023-08-15, DODIG-2023-109 issued and classified.** The DoD Inspector General completes the only formal evaluation of the Department's UAP conduct three weeks after Grusch testifies. The report is classified; only an unclassified summary is released. On the node Department of Defense Office of Inspector General. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-08-15, DoD IG completes DODIG-2023-109.** The Inspector General finds no department-wide UAP policy and component-level stovepipes, and cannot confirm that UAP threats have been identified and addressed. The full report stays classified. On the node DoD OIG - Evaluation of the DoD's Actions Regarding Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (DODIG-2023-109). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-08-15, Sol Foundation founded.** Garry Nolan and Peter Skafish co-found The Sol Foundation, the first institution to treat UAP simultaneously as a materials, governance and public-understanding problem. On the node The Sol Foundation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-08-15, DoD IG issues DODIG-2023-109.** The Inspector General finds no comprehensive DoD approach to UAP, combatant commanders largely excluded, and no requirement that components report UAP incidents to AARO. On the node DoD OIG - Evaluation of the DoD's Actions Regarding Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (DODIG-2023-109). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-08-22, UAP Caucus founded.** Burchett launches the bipartisan Congressional UAP Caucus and leads a caucus letter to the Intelligence Community Inspector General about UAP retrieval programs. On the node Congressional UAP Caucus. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-08-31, aaro.mil launched.** AARO launches its public website, initially without the mandated reporting mechanism; the secure reporting portal for USG employees and contractors follows in October 2023. On the node All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-09-14, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2023-09-14, NASA publishes the Independent Study Team report.** Spergel's 16-member panel concedes that 'much of the data collected by military sensors or intelligence satellites are classified' and Nicola Fox announces a NASA Director of UAP Research. On the node National Aeronautics and Space Administration. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-09-14, NASA report released; director unnamed.** The UAP Independent Study Team report finds analysis 'hampered by poor sensor calibration, the lack of multiple measurements, the lack of sensor metadata.' NASA announces a Director of UAP Research and declines to name him at the briefing. On the node NASA UAP Independent Study Team. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-09-14, NASA panel reports and dissolves.** The UAP Independent Study Team delivers its report - no evidence of extraterrestrial origin, data quality is the binding constraint - and dissolves the same day. It had never been given the classified holdings. On the node NASA Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Independent Study Team. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-10-01, First select committee push dies.** Burchett's effort to create a House select committee on UAP collapses when Speaker Kevin McCarthy loses the speakership. On the node Proposed House Select Committee on UAP. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-10-25, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2023-10-25, AARO FY2023 annual report.** First statutory annual report: 291 new reports, 801 total holdings. It contains no finding of any kind on extraterrestrial technology. On the node AARO FY2023 Consolidated Annual Report on UAP. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-10-25, FY2023 annual report.** AARO's first statutory annual report: 291 new reports, cumulative holdings 801, no explicit extraterrestrial denial - that formula appears only from FY2024. On the node AARO FY2023 Consolidated Annual Report on UAP. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-10-31, Sue Gordon elected MITRE board vice chair.** A former Principal Deputy Director of National Intelligence becomes vice chair of MITRE's governing board, three months after David Grusch's House Oversight testimony. On the node Susan M. Gordon. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-11-14, Grusch does not appear for the AARO interview.** Having agreed on 10 November to meet, Grusch does not attend the Arlington appointment. AARO's memorandum for record makes this the centerpiece of its impeachment; the same document shows five prior months of approaches routed through intermediaries. On the node AARO Memorandum for Record: Invitations to Interview Mr. David Grusch (FOIA 24-F-0266). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-11-17, Inaugural Sol Foundation conference.** Two days at Stanford, sponsored by the Nolan Laboratory and Stanford School of Medicine, with Eric Davis, Tim Gallaudet, Avi Loeb, Leslie Kean, Christopher Mellon, Karl Nell, Hal Puthoff and Jacques Vallee speaking. The movement acquires an academic address. On the node The Sol Foundation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-11-27, Turner and Rogers named.** Reporting identifies Turner and Rogers as leading the effort to eliminate or alter the Act, with McConnell and Speaker Johnson supporting; Sheehan states Turner demanded the eminent domain provision and the subpoena power be struck. On the node Michael R. Turner. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-12-01, Kirkpatrick leaves AARO for Oak Ridge.** AARO's founding director departs government and becomes Chief Technology Officer for Defense and Intelligence Programs at the Battelle-operated laboratory that had performed AARO's materials analysis. On the node Sean M. Kirkpatrick. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-01, Kirkpatrick departs AARO.** Announced 7 November, effective 1 December. He joins Oak Ridge National Laboratory as CTO for Defense and Intelligence Programs the same month. Tim Phillips becomes Acting Director. On the node Sean M. Kirkpatrick. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-06, Langley incursions begin.** The Air Force confirms Joint Base Langley-Eustis 'first observed UAS activities the evening of December 6' and experienced multiple incursions through the month. F-16 CAPs, NASA WB-57F orbits and FBI coordination follow. On the node Langley AFB Drone Incursions (December 2023). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-12, Senate accepts the gutting in silence.** The Senate's final consideration of the FY2024 NDAA produces no transcribed remarks containing the word 'anomalous'; no member objects aloud to the removal of the division the chamber had passed. On the node John F. Reed. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2023-12-22, Sections 1841-1843 of Public Law 118-31 enacted.** The UAP Disclosure Act is cut down in conference: the National Archives collection survives, the review board with subpoena power and the eminent domain provision over recovered technologies do not. On the node National Archives Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Collection. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-22, Congress bars UAP-related IR&D as an allowable indirect cost.** Section 1687 of the FY2024 NDAA bars funds for UAP activity under special or restricted access limitation absent disclosure to the defense and intelligence committees and congressional leadership, and provides that 'independent research and development funding relating to unidentified anomalous phenomena shall not be allowable as indirect expenses'. Section 7343 of the Intelligence Authorization Act enacts the same bar through the Director of National Intelligence. Congress has named the mechanism. On the node FY2024 NDAA sec. 1687, Limitation on Use of Funds for Certain Unreported Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-22, The stripping is enacted.** P.L. 118-31 is signed. Fifteen sections become three. The Review Board, the eminent domain clause, the Controlled Disclosure Campaign Plan, the $20,000,000 authorization, the contractor turn-in and the immunity grant are all gone. Sec. 7343 is left referring to an instruction it no longer identifies. On the node FY2024 NDAA, Public Law 118-31 - Div. A, Title XVIII, Subtitle C (secs. 1841-1843), UAP Records Collection; Div. G (IAA FY2024) secs. 7341, 7343. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-22, Eminent domain and the contractor inventory are stripped; the IR&D clause survives.** Public Law 118-31 is enacted without the Review Board, the eminent domain provision or the SSCI contractor disclosure regime. It retains the records collection at Secs. 1841-1843 and the funding limitations at Secs. 1687 and 7343, including the IR&D restriction citing DoDI 3204.01 by change date. On the node FY2024 NDAA, Public Law 118-31 - Div. A, Title XVIII, Subtitle C (secs. 1841-1843), UAP Records Collection; Div. G (IAA FY2024) secs. 7341, 7343. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-22, FY2024 NDAA creates the UAP Records Collection.** Sections 1841-1843 of P.L. 118-31 mandate a UAP Records Collection at the National Archives - without the review board, subpoena power or contractor reach in the original UAP Disclosure Act. On the node FY2024 NDAA, Public Law 118-31 - Div. A, Title XVIII, Subtitle C (secs. 1841-1843), UAP Records Collection; Div. G (IAA FY2024) secs. 7341, 7343. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-22, Money is cut off for unreported UAP special-access activity.** Pub. L. 118-31 sec. 1687 (Secretary of Defense; this Act's funds) and div. G sec. 7343 (DNI; that division's funds) bar obligation or expenditure for any activity involving unidentified anomalous phenomena 'protected under any form of special access or restricted access limitation' unless details are provided to a committee list wider than the 10 U.S.C. 119(g) defense committees, plus the four congressional leaders. Both sections also disallow UAP-related independent research and development as an indirect contract expense unless disclosed. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2023-12-22, Congress cuts off money for unreported UAP special access activity.** Sections 1687 and 7343 of Public Law 118-31 bar the obligation of funds for any UAP activity 'protected under any form of special access or restricted access limitations' unless the Secretary of Defense and the Director of National Intelligence respectively provide details to the defense and intelligence committees and congressional leadership, and make UAP-related independent research and development unallowable as an indirect expense. On the node FY2024 NDAA sec. 1687, Limitation on Use of Funds for Certain Unreported Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-22, FY2024 NDAA enacted with the Review Board and eminent domain stripped.** Public Law 118-31 is approved. Only secs. 9004-9006 of the Senate's Division G survive, renumbered as secs. 1841-1843 in Division A, Title XVIII, Subtitle C. The enacted text contains no Review Board and no eminent-domain provision. Postponement decisions revert to 'the original classification authority' holding the record, and Senior Agency Officials may unilaterally withdraw records from the Collection on notice to Congress. Sec. 1687 and IAA sec. 7343 impose conditional funding limits on unreported UAP special-access activity. On the node FY2024 NDAA, Public Law 118-31 - Div. A, Title XVIII, Subtitle C (secs. 1841-1843), UAP Records Collection; Div. G (IAA FY2024) secs. 7341, 7343. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2023-12-22, The Review Board is stripped in conference.** P.L. 118-31 is enacted with Subtitle C - a UAP Records Collection at NARA, an agency duty to transmit, and grounds for postponement. The independent Review Board and the eminent domain provision over recovered technologies, both passed by the Senate as Division I of H.R. 2670, are removed in conference where no recorded vote attaches. On the node Legislative Process (proposed and pending). Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
### 2024

- **2024-01-08, AARO memorandum on Grusch interview attempts.** AARO documents eight approaches to Grusch between June and November 2023 and concludes he had no intention of providing information. Later released as FOIA 24-F-0266. On the node AARO Memorandum for Record: Invitations to Interview Mr. David Grusch (8 Jan 2024). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-01-11, Garcia files the reporting bill.** Ranking Member Robert Garcia introduces H.R. 6967, the Safe Airspace for Americans Act, with Republican subcommittee chairman Glenn Grothman. On the node H.R. 6967 - Safe Airspace for Americans Act (118th Congress). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2024-01-12, The ICIG briefing produces nothing.** Inspector General Thomas Monheim briefs about sixteen House members for ninety minutes. Krishnamoorthi did not get the answers he hoped for; Burchett calls it 'more of the same' and a 4/10; Ogles says he asked specific questions and got no specific answers. On the node Closed ICIG UAP briefing to House Oversight members. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-01-13, ICIG briefs House Oversight members in closed session.** Members emerge saying they learned nothing new about UAP transparency from the Inspector General of the Intelligence Community. On the node Inspector General of the Intelligence Community. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2024-01-19, Kirkpatrick publishes the circular-reporting thesis.** In Scientific American the departing AARO director attributes the allegations to inadvertent disclosures of legitimate programs or unsupported belief, seven weeks before his agency's report advances the same frame. On the node Kirkpatrick, 'Here's What I Learned as the U.S. Government's UFO Hunter' (Scientific American). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-01-19, Kirkpatrick op-ed in Scientific American.** Seven weeks after leaving office and seven weeks before his report is published, Kirkpatrick calls the disclosure record circular reporting from a small group of interconnected believers and calls the whistleblowers suspect. On the node Kirkpatrick, 'Here's What I Learned as the U.S. Government's UFO Hunter' (Scientific American). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-01-25, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2024-01-25, IG summary released.** The unclassified summary of DODIG-2023-109 is published; the findings are structural and the full report remains withheld. On the node DoD OIG - Evaluation of the DoD's Actions Regarding Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (DODIG-2023-109). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-01-25, DoD IG unclassified summary published.** The public summary of DODIG-2023-109 lands, documenting that DoD does not require components to report UAP incidents to its own authoritative UAP office. On the node DoD OIG - Evaluation of the DoD's Actions Regarding Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (DODIG-2023-109). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-01-25, The Inspector General reports, mostly classified.** The DoD Inspector General releases an unclassified summary of DODIG-2023-109, its evaluation of the Department's actions regarding UAP. The evaluation was initiated on personnel complaints; the public receives the summary. On the node Congressional Oversight. Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-01-31, IM1 spherules classified.** Loeb et al. publish the recovery and classification of the Pacific spherules in Research Notes of the AAS, claiming a distinct extrasolar composition. On the node Galileo Project Pacific Ocean expedition to the CNEOS 2014-01-08 (IM1) site, June-July 2023. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-02-01, AARO Historical Record Volume I.** AARO reaffirms the MOGUL attribution and finds 'no empirical evidence' for reverse-engineering claims, without addressing the 1995 records-destruction finding or the JANAP channel. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-02-01, AARO answers on the record.** AARO publishes Volume I of its historical record report, concluding it found no empirical evidence that the US government or private companies have reverse-engineered extraterrestrial technology. The report is catalogued here in full: its scope, its access and its methodology are the parts a hostile audit turns on. On the node Official Denial. Strand: coverup. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-02-18, Gerb publishes the Battelle investigation.** UAP Gerb's first corporate investigation, published with a full primary-document citation list including Project Stork, Special Report No. 14 and the Defense.gov Moultrie roundtable transcript. The method that will define the domain is established. On the node UAP Gerb - Battelle Memorial Institute investigation. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-01, McConnell retires after 37 years.** Kirk McConnell leaves Capitol Hill after 37 years on defence and intelligence, including the Senate Armed Services Committee and both intelligence committees, and joins the UAP nonprofit sector. On the node Kirk McConnell. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-01, Seismic signal attributed to a truck.** Fernando et al. publish in Geophysical Journal International that the seismic signal used to localise the IM1 search was consistent with truck traffic, not an impact - the search may have been in the wrong place. On the node Galileo Project Pacific Ocean expedition to the CNEOS 2014-01-08 (IM1) site, June-July 2023. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-06, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2024-03-08, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2024-03-08, AARO Historical Record Report Vol. I published, confirming $22M FY2008/FY2010 appropriations at Reid's direction and AAWSAP=AATIP.**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-03-08, AARO Historical Record Report Volume I.** AARO states it partnered with 'the Special Access Program Control Offices for the DoD, IC, and DHS' and that its investigators had 'full access to all pertinent sensitive USG programs' - an assurance whose scope was defined by the offices under review. On the node DoD Special Access Program Central Office (DoD SAPCO). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, AARO Historical Record Report Volume I is silent on money.** The Pentagon's report on the historical record addresses retrieval and reverse-engineering claims at length and contains no discussion of misappropriated funds, illegal contracting, independent research and development, or contractor financial arrangements. The official rebuttal does not engage the financial allegation at all. On the node All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, AARO Historical Record Report Volume I released.** The formal DoD rebuttal: alleged reverse-engineering programs do not exist, are misidentified authentic programs, or were disestablished, and none of AARO's interviewees had firsthand knowledge. It identifies KONA BLUE, never engages Karl Nell by name, and never addresses the ICIG determination. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, AARO publishes the corporate denials without naming a company.** The Historical Record Report states that executives and chief technology officers of the named companies met AARO's Director and denied on the record ever having recovered, possessed or reverse-engineered extraterrestrial technology. No company is identified and no inspection is described. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, Historical Record Report Volume I.** AARO publishes its 63-page history under DOPSR-2024-0263, finding no empirical evidence of reverse-engineering, disclosing KONA BLUE, and stating it found no NDAs containing UAP-related information. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, AARO Volume I published - and omits the era.** AARO's historical record report names dozens of programmes but contains no mention of Kirtland, AFOSI, Bennewitz, Doty, MOON DUST, BLUE FLY, MJ-12, AQUARIUS, Rendlesham, Wilson or the 1975 nuclear-base overflights. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, AARO Historical Record Volume 1.** AARO's report treats 2009 as the standup year for AAWSAP and AATIP and states it found 'no evidence for the USG reverse-engineering narrative provided by interviewees'. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, AARO publishes the official history.** DoD confirms the $22M, the Reid direction, the contractor's UFO research 'with the support of the DIA program manager' and the ranch investigation, then closes the file and names no one. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-08, Historical Record Report Volume I released.** AARO publishes Volume I: no empirical evidence of reverse-engineering, ~30 interviewees, the alleged sample identified as a terrestrial Mg-Zn-Bi alloy, Kona Blue disclosed, and the finding that AARO located no UAP-related NDAs. Tim Phillips fronts the rollout; Hicks does not endorse the findings. On the node AARO Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with UAP, Volume I. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-12, Burlison: 'They weren't transparent to us in a SCIF setting'.** Burlison leads a bipartisan call for a House select committee on UAP focused first on expenditures, and states that DoD officials were not truthful to members in classified session. On the node Proposed House Select Committee on UAP. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-03-15, Langley story surfaces.** The War Zone publishes the Air Force's confirmation of the December 2023 incursions, three months after they ended. On the node Langley AFB Drone Incursions (December 2023). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-04-01, ORNL magnesium specimen analysis.** AARO publishes the Oak Ridge synopsis of a metallic specimen with its own supplement - the first of only two physical-specimen characterisations it has released. On the node AARO / Oak Ridge National Laboratory materials analyses. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2024-04-01, 'ORNL Synopsis: Analysis of a Metallic Specimen'.** Oak Ridge reports the specimen alleged to derive from a 1947 crash is a terrestrial magnesium alloy - while stating that neither AARO nor ORNL could verify its historical origin and that records of failed magnesium alloy designs are scant. On the node ORNL Synopsis: Analysis of a Metallic Specimen. Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-05-16, First UAP Transparency Act.** Burchett introduces H.R. 8424. It dies in referral. On the node H.R. 8424 - UAP Transparency Act (118th Congress). Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier C.
- **2024-06-01, AARO and DHS publish the KONA BLUE history.** The joint paper confirms that a DHS component head established a prospective special access program in 2011 and that the Deputy Secretary killed it six months later. On the node KONA BLUE. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-06-01, Kona Blue documents made public.** The declassified DHS Kona Blue package, transmitted by Acting Deputy Secretary Kristie Canegallo on 5 February 2024, is published - a chartered prospective SAP intended to house UAP recovery and reverse-engineering, terminated 10 February 2012. On the node KONA BLUE. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-06-15, .**  No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at no tier recorded.
- **2024-07-11, UAPDA refiled as SA 2610.** Rounds, for himself and Schumer, refiles the complete Act against the FY2025 NDAA with the eminent domain command unchanged. It is ordered to lie on the table and never called up. On the node UAP Disclosure Act of 2024 (S.Amdt. 2610 to S. 4638). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-07-11, ORNL reports the alleged crash specimen is terrestrial.** AARO releases the Oak Ridge analysis of the magnesium alloy sample acquired from a private UAP organisation and the US Army: terrestrial isotopic composition, no waveguide capability, and an undocumented chain of custody. On the node AARO / ORNL Synopsis: Analysis of a Metallic Specimen. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-07-15, Grusch sues over the records release.** Files in Loudoun County Circuit Court against Sheriff Mike Chapman and a Sheriff's Office employee over the FOIA release of his emergency-custody records. On the node David C. Grusch v. Michael Chapman et al. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-07-17, Off-World Technologies Division account published.** Gerb documents witness 'RA', a Green Beret describing two non-human artifacts held underground near Crane, Indiana between 2013 and 2015, with alleged SAIC and Sandia connections. On the node Off-World Technologies Division. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2024-08-20, Imminent published after a year of DOPSR review.** Elizondo's memoir debuts at number one on the New York Times bestseller list. He later testifies that 'what is in the book is what I was told I am allowed to talk about'. On the node Imminent: Inside the Pentagon's Hunt for UFOs. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-08-26, Kosloski named AARO director.** Dr. Jon T. Kosloski, previously of the National Security Agency, becomes AARO's full-time director. On the node Jon T. Kosloski. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-08-26, A signals technologist takes AARO.** Jon Kosloski, a senior National Security Agency researcher, is named to lead AARO - placing the government's UAP analytic hub under someone whose professional formation is in the collection discipline that holds the most relevant withheld data. On the node Signals & Sensor Intelligence. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-09-03, Battelle's Mark Peters becomes MITRE CEO.** MITRE's tenth president and CEO arrives directly from the post of executive vice president for laboratory management at Battelle Memorial Institute, having previously directed Idaho National Laboratory. On the node Mark Todd Peters. Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-09-12, DoDD 5205.07 reissued.** The SAP policy directive is reissued, cancelling the 2010 version and incorporating the July 2023 SAP Enterprise Reform memorandum. The requirement to notify DoD SAPCO before briefing Congress with SAP material is carried forward. On the node DoD Special Access Program Central Office (DoD SAPCO). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-09-12, DoDD 5205.07 and DoDI 5205.11 reissued the same day.** Both signed by Deputy Secretary of Defense Kathleen H. Hicks. The reissued Directive cancels the 2010 version and no longer contains the words 'waived', 'unacknowledged', 'carve-out' or a reference to 10 U.S.C. 119; those provisions now sit in DoDI 5205.11, which retains the glossary definitions, the para 1.2.f waiver designation, the Section 7 congressional access rules, and at para 1.2.i a scheduled phase-out of DCSA NISP oversight in July 2029. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-09-12, DoD deletes acknowledged, unacknowledged, waived and carve-out from its SAP directive.** The reissued DoD Directive 5205.07 cancels the 2010 version, incorporates the 11 July 2023 SAP Enterprise Reform guidance, and replaces the three-state taxonomy with 'Joint Force Integration SAP' and 'Strategic Enabler SAP'. Neither 10 U.S.C. 119(e) nor 32 CFR 117.6(b)(3) is repealed; the mechanisms survive while the vocabulary is removed from the public charter. On the node DoDD 5205.07, DoD Special Access Program (SAP) Policy (12 Sep 2024). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.

Burlison plays it at a hearing eleven months later; the Department has never confirmed it. On the node MQ-9 Hellfire engagement of an orb off Yemen (October 2024). Strand: witness. Recorded at Tier 2 named person on the record.

Scott's interview notes on 'Brad', 3 December 1990. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier C.

Eric Davis)' on 'American Alchemy'; the channel and the title now differ, and the corpus timestamp 0:36:24 to 0:38:00 falls inside the 52:45 runtime. On the node New Jersey and East Coast Drone Wave (Nov-Dec 2024). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-12-17, Four-agency joint denial.** DoD, DHS, FBI and FAA: 'We have not identified anything anomalous.' About 100 of more than 5,000 reports were run down and attributed to commercial, hobbyist and law enforcement drones, manned aircraft, helicopters and stars. On the node New Jersey and East Coast Drone Wave (Nov-Dec 2024). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-12-19, Reissued ICD 906 posted.** The reissued ICD 906, Controlled Access Programs, carries a PDF creation date of 19 December 2024 and states that it supersedes the ICD 906 signed 17 October 2015. It requires annual written re-justification of every unacknowledged CAP, terminates any CAP not submitted and approved, and vests non-discoverability approval in the DNI or PDDNI subject to annual re-validation. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-12-19, Reissued ICD 906 released.** The PDF of the reissued ICD 906 posted by ODNI carries a creation date of 19 December 2024 and states that it supersedes the ICD 906 signed 17 October 2015. It retains unacknowledged CAPs, adds the requirement that all CAPs and accesses be recorded in Scattered Castles subject only to DNI or PDDNI exemption, and confirms that neither it nor the DoD regime reaches 'special access programs under the purview of the National Security Council, such as covert action'. On the node ICD 906, Controlled Access Programs (ODNI). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-12-22, Deadline passes for the SAP access-population reports.** FY2024 IAA sec. 7606(c) required each agency head authorized by E.O. 13526 to establish a SAP to review, within one year of enactment (22 December 2023), the number of agency personnel eligible for access to such programs and report to a nine-committee list including both Judiciary Committees. No public record of any such report was located. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-12-22, Deadline passes for the SAP access population reports.** Section 7606(c) of the FY2024 Intelligence Authorization Act required each of the six officials authorised by E.O. 13526 to establish special access programs to report to nine congressional committees, including House Oversight, on how the number of personnel eligible for SAP access will be limited to the minimum required. The one-year deadline expired on this date. No such report has been publicly released. On the node FY2024 IAA sec. 7606(c), Agency Review of Special Access Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier B.
- **2024-12-23, FY2025 NDAA sunsets the Historical Record Report audit requirement.** Section 6802 of the FY2025 NDAA ties the quarterly and semiannual audit and reporting requirements on the UAP Historical Record Report to 90 and 180 days after delivery of the final volume. The same Act does not re-enact the section 1687(a) funding bar, which lapsed with fiscal year 2024. On the node NDAA FY2026 UAP provisions (Secs. 1671-1673). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-12-23, FY2025 enacted with no disclosure regime.** P.L. 118-159 contains only a GAO review of AARO and a sunset on audit briefings. The FY2024 funding bar on undisclosed UAP special access programmes is not renewed. On the node IAA FY2025 Title LXVIII, Secs. 6801-6802 (P.L. 118-159). Strand: coverup. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-12-23, Congress bars moving a capability from a CAP into a SAP without notice.** Pub. L. 118-159 sec. 6603 amends 50 U.S.C. 3091a(b) to prohibit an IC element head from transferring a capability from a controlled access program 'to a special access program (as defined in section 1152(g) of the National Defense Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 1994 (50 U.S.C. 3348(g))), or to anything else outside the controlled access program' until notice of intent goes to the appropriate committees and congressional leadership -- with self-certified exceptions for an 'urgent counterintelligence issue' or 'organizational restructuring'. Sec. 6602 separately cuts off NIP funds for any new CAP until the subsection (b) notification is submitted. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
- **2024-12-23, Congress prohibits moving a capability from a CAP into a SAP without notice.** Sections 6602 and 6603 of Public Law 118-159 add a funding cutoff to 50 U.S.C. 3091a and prohibit the head of an IC element from transferring a capability from a controlled access program to a special access program, or to anything else outside the CAP, without prior notice to the intelligence and appropriations committees and congressional leadership - subject to exceptions for urgent counterintelligence and organisational restructuring. On the node 50 U.S.C. 3091a, Congressional Oversight of Controlled Access Programs. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.
- **2024-12-23, FY2025 NDAA orders a GAO review of AARO and sunsets the Historical Record audits.** Public Law 118-159 is approved. Title LXVIII sec. 6801 directs the Comptroller General to review AARO's implementation of 50 U.S.C. 3373; sec. 6802 ends the GAO briefing and reporting cadence on the UAP Historical Record Report a set period after AARO delivers its final volume. On the node IAA FY2025 Title LXVIII, Secs. 6801-6802 (P.L. 118-159). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier B.
### 2025

- **2025-01-01, Lacatski publishes 'New Insights'.** The third volume of the DOPSR-cleared series continues releasing AAWSAP-derived material through trade publication rather than through FOIA or testimony. On the node Inside the U.S. Government Covert UFO Program (Lacatski, 2023-2026). Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier B.

An independent YouTube channel breaks a crash-retrieval whistleblower to a million people. On the node American Alchemy. Strand: witness. Recorded at board tier A.

Burchett files H.R. 1187 the same day. On the node Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets. Strand: oversight. Recorded at board tier A.

On the node 'UAP Gerb' (Sam). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier A.

Burlison.** The central whistleblower in the record takes a formal position inside the legislative branch, with a brief on UAP transparency and service on the Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets. Crane writes DoD on Grusch's reprisal.** Crane notifies the Department that Grusch suffered removal of his clearance, denial of promotion, and loss of medical retirement for his participation in UAP disclosure. - **2025-08-01, AARO data standardization workshop.** AARO holds a private workshop hosted by Associated Universities, Inc. in the Washington area on UAP data standardisation; the white paper is published in February 2026. On the node All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO). Strand: program. Recorded at board tier C.

Sec. 1672 repeals 50 U.S.C. 3373a and adds a sources-and-methods qualifier to the immediate-data-availability mandate. Sec. 1673 orders an accounting of the security classification guides governing UAP information and authorises a consolidated UAP classification matrix. - **2026-08-28, Air Force becomes the first military department to transfer into RG 615.** The National Archives accessioned 'Records from the Department of the Air Force Relating to Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena' (catalog series 656595571, accession TR-0615-2026-0003) into Record Group 615: 30 PDF files dated 25 October 1945 to 31 December 1969, access and use both Unrestricted, carrying contiguous UAP Record Identifiers 342UAP00001 to 342UAP00030. It is the eighth agency series in RG 615 and the first from a military department in its own right. The documents' own declassification blocks show the Air Force Declassification Office cleared them on 5 May 2025 under the FY24 NDAA authority now codified at 44 U.S.C. 2107, fifteen months before the records reached NARA. On the node National Archives Record Group 615 - UAP Records Collection. Strand: oversight. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.

- **2029-07-01, Scheduled: DCSA NISP oversight phase-out.** DoDI 5205.11 para 1.2.i: 'Defense Counterintelligence and Security Agency (DCSA) oversight for the administration of the National Industrial Security Program will be phased out in July 2029 in accordance with the carve-out provision.' Forward-dated commitment recorded in a 12 September 2024 issuance. No node attached. Strand: program. Recorded at Tier 1 documented.
